# CROSS-FIELD IDEAS — out-of-CS inspiration for the SOTA push

**Writer:** `rec-sota-crossfield-loop` (every 6h, :05) — appends idea blocks here.
**Reader:** `rec-sota-research-loop` (in-field, :15) — grounds the strongest idea into a runnable `RESEARCH_QUEUE.md` proposal within the same 6h window.

The problem to bring outside inspiration to (state it abstractly so non-CS analogies surface):
> *Given a user's time-ordered history of past chosen items (each item carries a text description), predict the single next item out of a catalog of ~25k, under strong popularity bias, recency effects, and long-tail sparsity. Current model = a causal pointwise-attention sequence encoder over learned item embeddings fused with frozen text-embedding features. We are stuck at a ceiling (~0.064–0.065 NDCG@10) and need a structurally new inductive bias.*

Append blocks in this format:

```
## XF <YYYY-MM-DD>-<n> — <domain>: <short title>  [status: NEW]
- **Domain & principle:** <field, and the specific mechanism/law/phenomenon>
- **The analogy:** <how that maps onto the next-item-from-history problem above>
- **Concrete model mechanism (pure-PyTorch implementable here):** <what operation/layer/loss it becomes>
- **Why no CS person would default to this:** <the outside-the-box justification>
- **Novelty vs our ledger:** <nearest existing probe and how this differs>
- **Feasibility / risk:** <can it be a zero-init flag in run_sasrec_sbert.py? main risks?>
```

Status lifecycle: `NEW` → (in-field researcher) `GROUNDED→PROPOSAL <id>` (translated into the queue) or `PASS <reason>` (not feasible/novel enough).

Be genuinely wild but require a plausible mechanistic path to a pure-PyTorch implementation on one consumer GPU — an analogy with no implementable mechanism is noise, not an idea.

---

## Ideas

## XF 2026-06-16-1 — Ecology / evolutionary biology: niche-overlap "fitness sharing" as catalog-score repulsion  [status: GROUNDED→PROPOSAL 2026-06-16-2]
- **Domain & principle:** Limiting-similarity / species-packing theory (MacArthur & Levins 1967; May 1973) + its operationalization as **fitness sharing** in evolutionary computation (Goldberg & Richardson 1987). When two species' resource-utilization curves overlap, each suppresses the other via a competition coefficient that decays with niche distance; coexistence fails once separation `d` drops below niche width `w` (May's `d/w` criterion). Goldberg–Richardson make this a concrete operator: an individual's **shared fitness** is `F'(i) = F(i) / Σ_j sh(d_ij)` with a triangular sharing kernel `sh(d) = 1 − (d/σ_share)^α` for `d ≤ σ_share`, else `0` (α=1 standard). The denominator is the local "crowding" — fitness in a densely populated niche is divided down, so a finite resource is **split** among near-identical individuals instead of all of them scoring high.
- **The analogy:** The single "next-item slot" is the scarce resource; the ~25k catalog items are the competing species; the frozen text-embedding space is the niche/trait axis. Our score `h @ all_items.T` (run_sasrec_sbert.py L693-694) is a **pure independent dot product per item** — there is *no* term anywhere that lets near-duplicate items compete for that one slot. Under strong popularity bias the model therefore piles probability onto a tight cluster of nearly-identical popular items (same franchise / sequel / re-skin). This is exactly the **IIA-violation** that breaks multinomial logit — the "red-bus/blue-bus" problem (Debreu 1960; McFadden) and Tversky's **similarity effect** (1972): adding a near-duplicate should split that item's share, not inflate the cluster. Niche-overlap competition is the inductive bias that says "semantically redundant items must share the exposure mass."
- **Concrete model mechanism (pure-PyTorch implementable here):** Add a **frozen-neighborhood crowding penalty** to the full-softmax target score inside `chunked_full_softmax_loss` (L698-764). Reuse the already-computed frozen TAPE soft assignment `A` (n_items×K, L588-592) as a cheap factored proxy for text-space niche position: define each item's crowding `m_i = Σ_k A_ik · (Σ_j A_jk · pop_j)` = `A @ (Aᵀ pop)` — an O(n·K) matvec (K≈512), giving the soft count of popularity-weighted items sharing item i's prototypes (the fitness-sharing denominator, with cluster co-membership as `sh(d)`). Then **subtract a niche-shared penalty from the target logit only**: replace `score_y` in the CE with `score_y − β·log(1 + m_y)` (β a single `nn.Parameter`, **zero-init ⇒ exact no-op at start**). Effect: when the ground-truth next item sits in a crowded popular niche, its reward is discounted, forcing the encoder to earn probability via *distinguishing* signal rather than niche popularity — directly penalizing the redundant-cluster flooding. (Symmetric variant: subtract `β·log(1+m_k)` from every chunk logit via the already-accumulated chunk sum, a catalog-wide niche prior; start with target-only.) Flag: `--niche-share-beta 0.0` (default OFF, zero-init β ⇒ bit-identical to current loss).
- **Why no CS person would default to this:** ML treats the catalog softmax as i.i.d. independent classes — the entire training objective scores each item in isolation; "items competing with each other for one slot" is an ecological framing, not a classification one. The closest CS tool, a **Determinantal Point Process**, would build and invert an L-kernel over a *candidate set* at *re-rank* time (set selection) — heavy, post-hoc, and never touches training. The ecology/GA framing instead yields a single O(n·K) additive division folded straight into the training loss with one learnable scalar. A recsys engineer reaches for IPS reweighting or DPP re-ranking, not "divide the target reward by its niche crowding."
- **Novelty vs our ledger:** Nearest probes are `--text-sim-bias` and TAPE. Both are **attractive** and operate on *representation/attention*: text-sim-bias adds `cos(text_i,text_j)` inside attention (L653-657) to pull similar *history positions* together; TAPE adds shared learnable capacity to similar items' *features* (L588-592). This is the **opposite sign and a different locus**: a *repulsive* term in the *loss/score* that makes semantically-similar *candidate* items compete and split exposure. No ledger probe (c1 experts, c2 distill, c3 decay-kernel, c4 ensemble, label-smoothing) introduces any inter-item interaction at scoring/loss time — every one scores items independently. Genuinely new axis.
- **Feasibility / risk:** Zero-init β flag, default-OFF, no-op at init ⇒ safe. `A @ (Aᵀ pop)` is one O(n·K) matvec per step (negligible vs the chunked softmax); `pop` (train item-frequency vector) is computed once at load. Main risk: it could *hurt* test NDCG if penalizing popular-niche targets fights the genuine popularity signal (the metric itself rewards popular hits) — but β is learnable and can shrink toward 0, and the J-ablation shows the stack over-relies on popularity, so a mild repulsion may improve tail discrimination. Second risk: target-only penalty biases the *loss* but not eval scores; if it helps, promote to the symmetric catalog-wide score prior. Cheap to test on VG (~6 min/run); clean single-flag ablation.

## XF 2026-06-16-2 — Psychophysics / neuroscience: Weber–Fechner log-popularity as an explicit calibrated logit channel  [status: PASS 2026-06-20 (in-field): MODEL-LEVER, foreclosed. New-lever search CLOSED (supervisor cycles 4/6/7/8, sealed by CF1); cycle-8 CONVERGENCE makes the user-mode titration the LAST sanctioned experiment ⇒ no new levers groundable. This one also adds a popularity term that applies at EVAL = the popularity-inflation trap the supervisor auto-rejects. Foreclosed, not refuted on merit.]
- **Domain & principle:** The **Weber–Fechner law** (Fechner 1860) — perceived intensity grows with the *logarithm* of stimulus magnitude, and the just-noticeable difference scales with the stimulus (`ΔS/S = const`). Modern **efficient-coding** theory explains *why*: when a capacity-limited system encodes a variable whose natural distribution is ≈`1/f` (power-law), the information-maximizing code is **logarithmic** (verified fly photoreceptors → primate numerosity; recently in transformer magnitude representations, where representational distance fits Stevens' law `d = a + b·|log n₁ − log n₂|` with the exponent collapsing to ≈pure-log). Magnitudes that span orders of magnitude *must* be compressed logarithmically to be discriminable at all scales.
- **The analogy:** Item **popularity (interaction frequency) is Zipfian** — a textbook `1/f` power law spanning 4–5 orders of magnitude (long-tail sparsity is literally the problem statement). Our model has **no explicit popularity channel**: it must launder all popularity signal through the learned item-embedding *norm* and the softmax, on a *linear* dot-product scale that a few head items dominate. Efficient coding says this is the wrong representation — the head saturates the scale and the tail is crushed below discrimination threshold, which is precisely the long-tail failure. Weber–Fechner prescribes the fix: enter popularity as an explicit **logarithmic** term so equal *ratios* of frequency map to equal score increments, making head and tail equally discriminable.
- **Concrete model mechanism (pure-PyTorch implementable here):** Add a **learnable log-frequency logit bias** to the per-item score. Precompute `logpop = log(1 + train_freq)` (n_items vector, once at load, zero for pad). In `forward` (L693-694) and identically in the chunked loss (L729-752, since scoring must match), add a per-item scalar to the logits: `logits = h @ all_items.T + γ · (logpop − logpop.mean())`, where `γ = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(1))` is a single learnable **Weber fraction** (sign free: the model can *boost* head calibration or *suppress* it). **Zero-init γ ⇒ exact no-op at init.** A 1-extra-parameter, per-item additive channel that is constant across users (a global popularity prior) — optionally extend to a per-user gate `γ_u = w·pooled_history` (rank-1, still ~d params) so users with niche histories get less popularity push. Centering keeps the softmax shift-invariant and the gradient well-scaled. Flag: `--weber-logpop` (default OFF; γ zero-init ⇒ bit-identical at start).
- **Why no CS person would default to this:** Sequence-rec orthodoxy is "the model *learns* popularity end-to-end from data; injecting a hand-built frequency feature is a crutch." So nobody adds an explicit popularity logit — they trust the embedding norm. And when CS *does* address popularity it reaches for **IPS** (inverse-propensity *loss reweighting*, a sample weight) or logit-adjustment heuristics, not a *psychophysically-motivated logarithmic perceptual channel inside the score* with a single learnable Weber fraction. The "encode a power-law magnitude on a log scale for cross-scale discriminability" framing is sensory-neuroscience, not recsys.
- **Novelty vs our ledger:** No ledger probe touches an explicit popularity term — popularity has *never* been an input or a score channel in this campaign; it is only ever implicit in the learned table. Distinct from label-smoothing (U-series): that adds a *uniform* floor over all items at the *loss target*; this adds a *frequency-shaped log* term to the *score itself*, calibrating head-vs-tail rather than flattening targets uniformly. Distinct from TAPE/text features (those are *semantic*, not *frequency*). Distinct from IPS (a loss reweighting, not a score channel). New signal (item frequency) and new attachment (additive calibrated logit).
- **Feasibility / risk:** One parameter, zero-init, default-OFF no-op ⇒ maximally safe; adds a length-n vector add to logits (negligible). Must add the term in **both** `forward` and `chunked_full_softmax_loss` so train and eval score identically (eval is full-catalog dot-product ranking — the term applies at eval too, which is the point: it re-calibrates the ranking). Main risk: because full-catalog NDCG *rewards* popular hits, a positive γ could trivially inflate val by up-ranking head items without genuine personalization — guard by (a) centering, (b) watching the val≫test gap (if γ just exploits popularity, test won't follow, the campaign's known signature), and (c) preferring the per-user-gated variant which can only push popularity where the user's own history is generic. Cheap VG ablation; γ's learned sign/magnitude is itself an interpretable finding.

## XF 2026-06-16-3 — Statistical mechanics: Tsallis q-exponential (non-extensive) softmax for heavy-tailed long-tail likelihood  [status: PASS 2026-06-20 (in-field): MODEL-LEVER (loss family), foreclosed. New-lever search CLOSED (cycles 4/6/7/8, CF1); cycle-8 CONVERGENCE forbids any new lever/experiment. Loss-tail reshaping sits squarely in the saturated "regularizer-on-V2" axis (LS already the lone confirmed loss-side gain). Foreclosed, not refuted on merit.]
- **Domain & principle:** **Tsallis non-extensive statistical mechanics** (Tsallis 1988) generalizes the Boltzmann–Gibbs exponential to the **q-exponential** `exp_q(x) = [1 + (1−q)x]_+^{1/(1−q)}` (positive-part clipped; → `e^x` as q→1), with inverse **q-logarithm** `ln_q(x) = (x^{1−q} − 1)/(1−q)`. The maximum-q-entropy distribution `p ∝ exp_q(−βE)` is the canonical model for systems with long-range correlations and **power-law (heavy) tails**: for **q > 1** the tail decays as a *power law*, far heavier than the standard exponential Boltzmann tail (q=1); q<1 decays faster with compact support. It is the principled "heavy-tailed Boltzmann distribution."
- **The analogy:** The next-item softmax `p_i = e^{s_i}/Σ_j e^{s_j}` is **exactly a Boltzmann distribution** over catalog "energy states" `−s_i` at temperature 1 — ranking *is* energy minimization, and CE training pushes the system toward the ground state (the target item). But the standard (q=1) softmax has **exponential tails**, so it assigns vanishing probability to long-tail items and, in training, produces the over-confident peaked posterior the campaign keeps diagnosing (persistent val≫test gap = an over-sharp softmax that doesn't transfer). The item-popularity distribution this system lives in is heavy-tailed; matching the *likelihood's* tail to the data's tail argues for a **q>1 (heavy-tailed) softmax** so the model is structurally less able to starve the tail and less prone to over-confident peaks.
- **Concrete model mechanism (pure-PyTorch implementable here):** Replace the Boltzmann CE in `chunked_full_softmax_loss` (L698-764) with a **q-exponential generalized CE**, gated by a single learnable index. Parameterize `q = 1 + softplus(q_raw)` with `q_raw = nn.Parameter(-large)` so **q≈1 at init ⇒ recovers the exact current softmax (no-op)**, and the model can climb to q>1. Two implementable routes: (a) **q-loss only** — keep the standard softmax `p`, but use the Tsallis loss `L = ln_q(1/p_y) = (p_y^{1−q} − 1)/(1−q)` (the "q-cross-entropy"; reduces to `−log p_y` at q=1), a one-line change to the final loss that down-weights already-confident targets (q>1) and is robust on imbalanced/long-tail data — needs only the already-computed `p_y = exp(score_y − lse)`, no change to the chunked normalizer; (b) **full q-softmax** — normalize with `exp_q`: accumulate `Z_q = Σ_j exp_q(s_j − s_max)` chunk-wise (same incremental pattern as the current `lse` loop, swapping `exp`→`exp_q` and `logsumexp`→running sum), then `p_y = exp_q(s_y − s_max)/Z_q`, loss `−ln_q(p_y)`. Route (a) is the safe first probe (minimal, numerically tame). Flag: `--tsallis-q` (default OFF / q_raw→q≈1 ⇒ bit-identical CE).
- **Why no CS person would default to this:** Cross-entropy = the softmax = Boltzmann–Gibbs is so foundational in ML that its *exponential tail* is invisible — nobody questions whether the likelihood family matches the data's tail; they tune temperature (still exponential) or add a uniform smoothing floor. Choosing a **non-extensive (q≠1) statistical-mechanics likelihood** because the *system has long-range/heavy-tailed structure* is a physics modeling decision, not an ML one. A recsys/ML practitioner facing long-tail reaches for focal loss, class-balanced reweighting, or logit adjustment — not "swap Boltzmann for Tsallis statistics and learn the entropic index q."
- **Novelty vs our ledger:** The loss axis is heavily probed (full-softmax > sampled+dot > sampled+cosine; cosine-temp scoring; **label smoothing** U-series = current best) but **every** probe stayed inside the **q=1 exponential family** — temperature and smoothing both preserve exponential tails. This changes the *tail exponent of the likelihood itself* (power-law vs exponential) via one learnable scalar — a structurally different lever than any tried. Distinct from label smoothing: smoothing adds a *uniform* eps floor to the *target* (flat); q-softmax reshapes the *entire normalizer's tail* to a power law (popularity-shape-aware). Distinct from focal loss (a fixed `(1−p)^γ` reweight, still exponential softmax). New: the likelihood family is the variable.
- **Feasibility / risk:** Route (a) is ~3 lines in the existing loss with one zero-effect-at-init parameter (q≈1 ⇒ exact CE) ⇒ very safe and cheap. Numerical care: `exp_q` needs the `[·]_+` clip and `q` bounded (e.g. q∈(1, 2.0) via `1+softplus`); compute in fp32 (loss already does). Risk: route (b)'s `Z_q` over 25k items can be dominated by the clip/tail and is less numerically tame — defer it unless (a) shows signal. Honest caveat: the persistent val≫test gap has resisted every loss-side regularizer except label smoothing, and the verdict attributes the residual to unreproducible HSTU-kernel numerics — so this targets *long-tail likelihood shape*, a real but possibly small lever; its value is partly diagnostic (does a heavier-tailed likelihood move the tail-vs-head error balance?). Could **stack** with label-smoothing 0.2 (orthogonal: target-floor × tail-shape). Cheap VG ablation; clean single-flag, single-parameter test.

## XF 2026-06-16-4 — Statistics (Stein's paradox / empirical Bayes): frequency-adaptive James–Stein shrinkage of item embeddings toward their semantic-cluster mean  [status: GROUNDED→PROPOSAL 2026-06-16-3]
- **Domain & principle:** **Stein's paradox** (Stein 1956; James & Stein 1961) and its empirical-Bayes reading (Efron & Morris 1973–77, the baseball-batting-average example). When you estimate ≥3 means from noisy data, the per-item maximum-likelihood estimate (the raw sample mean) is **inadmissible**: an estimator that *shrinks every estimate toward a common mean* `m` has strictly lower total risk, for **all** parameter values. The empirical-Bayes form gives the shrinkage weight explicitly — posterior mean = `(1−λ)·MLE + λ·m` with `λ = σ²/(σ²+τ²)` — and crucially, with per-item sample sizes `n_i`, the noise variance of item i's estimate is `σ²/n_i`, so the **shrinkage is heavier for low-`n_i` (rarely-observed) items** and vanishes for well-observed ones. The geometric reason (from the search: Efron-Morris; Stein-paradox expositions) is that in dimension `D≥3` a noisy sample sits, on average, a factor `~√D` *too far* from the true mean — shrinking corrects this systematic outward bias, and the correction is largest exactly where data is sparsest.
- **The analogy:** Each catalog item's **learned id-embedding `id_i` is precisely a per-item MLE estimated from that item's interactions** — and the catalog is dominated by long-tail items seen only a handful of times (the problem statement's "long-tail sparsity"). These tail embeddings are the high-variance, few-sample estimates Stein's theorem says are *guaranteed* to be improved by shrinkage toward a group mean. We already *have* the ideal group mean for free: the **TAPE prototype reconstruction `m_i = A_i @ P`** (run_sasrec_sbert.py L591-592) is the learnable capacity shared across an item's semantic neighborhood — i.e. the "grand mean of items like me." Stein says: don't trust a rare item's own noisy 64-dim vector; pull it toward its semantic-cluster prototype, by an amount set by how little we've seen it. The embedding dimension here is `d=64 ≫ 3`, so the dominance condition is amply met, and the per-item interaction count gives a principled, observable `n_i` for the shrinkage weight.
- **Concrete model mechanism (pure-PyTorch implementable here):** New flag **`--js-shrink` (default OFF ⇒ exact no-op)**. (a) Precompute a **train-only** frequency vector `n_i` (the same leak-free `pop` from TRAIN interactions established in PROPOSAL 2026-06-16-2 — never touches val/test target rows); register `self.register_buffer("item_freq", n)`. (b) One learnable scalar **shrinkage intensity** `self.shrink_c = nn.Parameter(torch.tensor(-8.0))`; map to `c = softplus(shrink_c)` so `c→0` at init. (c) In `item_features` (L588-592), where `m_i = a @ proto_emb` is *already computed*, set the per-item weight `λ_i = c / (c + item_freq[ids])` and **shrink the learned id-vector toward the prototype mean**: change `e = item_emb(ids) + sbert_proj(s) + m` to `e = [(1−λ_i)·item_emb(ids) + λ_i·m] + sbert_proj(s) + m`. At `c=0 ⇒ λ_i≡0 ⇒ e = item_emb + sbert + m`, **bit-identical to current**. As `c` grows, rare items (small `n_i`) get `λ_i→1` (id-vector replaced by its semantic-cluster mean), head items keep their own vector. This is the Efron–Morris posterior mean with `n_i`-modulated shrinkage, attached in one line at the embedding-construction site (so it applies identically in encode and in `all_item_features` scoring — a representation regularizer, **not** a logit-popularity injection, so it does not court the eval-time popularity trap the supervisor flagged for XF-2). Cost: one elementwise vector op; `n_i` computed once.
- **Why no CS person would default to this:** The ML reflex for under-trained rare embeddings is to **add capacity or augment signal** — the search confirms it (adaptive gradient gating for rare tokens, embedding-matrix augmentation, frequency-tiered embedding dims). Stein's paradox prescribes the *opposite and counterintuitive* move: **deliberately bias rare estimates by shrinking them**, accepting bias to cut variance, with a guarantee that total error drops. "Shrink your rarest item vectors toward a shared mean, hardest where you've seen them least" is a 1950s mathematical-statistics result (famous precisely because it offends the MLE intuition), not a representation-learning instinct — no recsys engineer defaults to admissibility theory.
- **Novelty vs our ledger:** Nearest probes are **TAPE** and **text-init warm-start (S1, neutral)**. TAPE *adds* a shared prototype term `m_i` (always-on, frequency-agnostic, additive); text-init *initializes* the table from text PCA then lets it move freely. This is categorically different: a **frequency-adaptive convex shrinkage** of the per-item vector *toward* that shared mean — a regularizer that **removes** effective capacity from the tail, modulated by observed count. No probe in the ~55-probe ledger does adaptive, sample-size-weighted shrinkage; every embedding lever so far either adds capacity or warm-starts it. It is also in the **only proven-to-transfer class** (label-smoothing is the lone confirmed gain, and it too is an anti-overconfidence regularizer) and directly attacks the analyst's diagnosed long-tail-sparsity + val≫test-overfit failure mode by *reducing* tail capacity rather than feeding val-inflating capacity.
- **Feasibility / risk:** Zero-init `shrink_c` ⇒ exact no-op; one extra scalar parameter + one buffer; one elementwise line in `item_features`; ~7 min/VG run. Stacks cleanly on the LS0.2 winning stack and **requires `--text-prototypes`** (assert it, as `--expert-heads` does at L566) since `m_i` is the shrinkage target. Main failure mode: shrinking rare items toward cluster means could erase their *distinguishing* signal and drop tail recall — but `c` is learnable and can shrink to 0 (a clean negative if it does), and the learned `c` is itself an interpretable readout of how much the tail was over-fit. Second risk: if the dominant gain in this metric is head-item popularity (full-catalog NDCG rewards popular hits), shrinking the tail may be net-neutral on the headline number even while improving tail discrimination — report tail-sliced NDCG if flat. Cheap, leak-free (train-only `n_i`), clean single-flag ablation; could also sweep `c` as a fixed hyperparameter `{0.5,1,2,5}·median(n_i)` if the learnable scalar is sluggish.

## XF 2026-06-16-5 — Physics (heat equation / Gibbs measure on the text manifold): diffused semantic soft-target ("heat-kernel label smoothing")  [status: GROUNDED→PROPOSAL 2026-06-16-4]
- **Domain & principle:** The **heat kernel**. The solution of the diffusion equation `∂u/∂t = Δu` started from a point source (a Dirac delta at `y`) is the Gaussian/heat kernel `K_t(y,j) ∝ exp(−dist(y,j)²/4t)` — probability mass that spreads from `y` to nearby points, with the **diffusion time `t` setting the spread** (t→0 recovers the delta). Equivalently, in statistical mechanics, this is the **Gibbs/Boltzmann measure** `q_j ∝ exp(−E_j/T)` with energy `E_j = dist(y,j)²` and temperature `T=2t`: the maximum-entropy distribution consistent with a mean distance from `y`. On a data manifold the same object is the graph heat kernel `exp(−tL)` (L = Laplacian) — diffusion respecting the manifold's geometry.
- **The analogy:** Cross-entropy trains the model toward a **Dirac delta on the single held-out item `y`** — it punishes equally for ranking a near-identical sequel/re-skin second and for ranking a random unrelated item second. But under leave-last-out, that one held-out item is just **one sample** of "what the user did next"; several semantically-near items were near-equally plausible. Forcing a delta target is exactly the **over-confidence** that the campaign's structural val≫test gap diagnoses (the model fits the val-cut's idiosyncratic single target and fails to transfer one step). Diffusing the target — letting heat flow from `y` to its text-space neighbors for a learnable time `t` — replaces the delta with a **Gibbs soft-target over the catalog's semantic geometry**: the model gets partial credit for predicting items close to `y`, and is no longer punished for plausible near-misses. This is the geometry-aware generalization of the one lever that *worked* (label smoothing) — LS diffuses to a **uniform** distribution (infinite temperature, geometry-blind); the heat kernel diffuses along the **text manifold** (finite temperature, geometry-aware).
- **Concrete model mechanism (pure-PyTorch implementable here):** New flag **`--heat-target` with a learnable `--heat-temp` (default OFF / t→0 ⇒ exact delta = current CE).** (a) **Precompute once** (leak-free, frozen text only): for each item `y`, its top-`m` (≈20) nearest neighbors in the **frozen SBERT/text space** and their cosine sims `s_{yj}`; register buffers `nbr_idx (n_items,m)` and `nbr_sim (n_items,m)`. (To stay O(n·m) and avoid the 25k² all-pairs cost, build neighbors from the already-frozen TAPE assignment `A` — items sharing prototypes — or a single batched top-m on the text matrix at load.) (b) Soft target = heat/Gibbs weights over `{y}∪neighbors`: `q_{yj} = softmax(s_{yj}/T)` with `T = softplus(heat_temp_raw)`; mass on `y` itself included. (c) In `chunked_full_softmax_loss` (L698-764), the numerator currently uses only `target_scores` (L752). Additionally **gather the scores at each target's `m` neighbor ids** (cheap — `m≈20` extra gathers reusing the same chunked `chunk_logits`, or a small dedicated gather over `all_item_features`), and replace the CE numerator `target_scores` with the **soft-target expectation** `Σ_j q_{yj}·score_j`: i.e. `loss = (lse − Σ_j q_{yj}·score_{yj}).mean()`. As `T→0`, `q→[1,0,…]` on `y` ⇒ reduces to the exact current `lse − target_scores` (**no-op**). This is **training-only**: eval scoring (L732/`all_item_features`) is untouched, so there is **zero eval-time manipulation** (no val-inflation trap). Composes additively with uniform LS0.2 (floor) — they are orthogonal (uniform target floor × geometry-shaped target spread).
- **Why no CS person would default to this:** A practitioner facing over-confidence reaches for **uniform** label smoothing, temperature, or focal loss — all geometry-blind. The move "treat the training target as a *heat distribution diffusing along the data manifold for a learnable physical time*, so the loss gives partial credit by semantic distance" is a **diffusion-equation / Gibbs-measure modeling decision**, not an ML one. (Honest CS prior art exists — *discrimination-aware* / *similarity-prototype* label smoothing in image/text **classification**, cited below — but it is confined to small fixed label sets with a dense K×K similarity matrix; nobody applies a heat-kernel soft target at **25k-catalog sequential-recommendation** scale with a sparse top-m neighborhood and a *learnable diffusion temperature*.)
- **Novelty vs our ledger:** Distinct from **label-smoothing** (U-series, current best): LS is the **infinite-temperature, geometry-blind** limit of this (uniform spread); this is **finite-temperature, text-manifold-shaped** (spread concentrated on semantic neighbors). Distinct from **text-distillation (c2, DEAD)**: that aligned the *hidden state* to the target's *text vector* in representation space; this reshapes the *target distribution* at the *loss* over the *catalog*. Distinct from **text-sim-bias / TAPE**: those act on attention/representation; this acts purely on the CE target. No ledger probe makes the training target a geometry-aware soft distribution; every probe used either a hard delta or a uniform LS floor. New axis: the *shape of the supervision target*, set by manifold diffusion.
- **Feasibility / risk:** Default-OFF, `T→0` no-op ⇒ safe; one learnable scalar + two precomputed neighbor buffers; the extra cost is `m≈20` score gathers per target (negligible vs the 25k-item chunked softmax). Honest CS prior art **must be cited** (discrimination-aware label smoothing; similarity-prototype softening — see search) so this is framed as a *novel catalog-scale, learnable-temperature, manifold-diffusion* instantiation, not a wholesale invention. Main risk: if `T` learns large the target over-diffuses and dilutes the true-item signal (test drops) — `T` is learnable and the model can drive it toward 0 (clean negative); cap `T` and/or floor the self-mass `q_{yy}≥0.5`. Second risk: neighbor quality depends on the frozen text space — but SBERT neighbors are exactly the "near-duplicate franchise/sequel" clusters the analyst flagged, which is the intended target of the partial credit. Cheap VG ablation; stack-on-LS0.2 single-flag; the learned `T` (effective diffusion radius) is an interpretable readout.

## XF 2026-06-16-6 — Neuroscience (free-energy principle / predictive coding): per-context precision-weighted loss ("learned aleatoric attenuation" of the next-item CE)  [status: PASS 2026-06-20 (in-field): MODEL-LEVER (per-example loss weighting), foreclosed. New-lever search CLOSED (cycles 4/6/7/8, CF1); cycle-8 CONVERGENCE forbids new levers. Same anti-overconfidence/loss class as the saturated W1/X1/Y1/GD1 axis (all voted off). Foreclosed, not refuted on merit.]
- **Domain & principle:** The **free-energy principle / active inference** (Friston 2006–; predictive coding). The brain does not minimize raw prediction error — it minimizes **precision-weighted** prediction error. Each prediction-error signal is multiplied by its **precision** `π = 1/σ²` (inverse variance / confidence), so errors the system *expects to be noisy* (low precision, e.g. a dark/ambiguous scene) are **down-weighted** and do not drive learning hard, while high-precision errors dominate. Precision is itself **estimated** (encoded by neuromodulators — cholinergic/dopaminergic gain control) and is **context-dependent**: the brain learns *which situations are inherently unpredictable* and attenuates its surprise there, rather than over-fitting to the one outcome it happened to observe. Formally this is the free-energy term `F = ½ π·ε² − ½ log π` (the `−log π` term stops the trivial `π→0` collapse) — the same object as **heteroscedastic aleatoric loss attenuation** in ML (Kendall & Gal 2017, `L = ε²/2σ² + ½log σ²`), but motivated as a *normative perceptual law*, not a noise model.
- **The analogy:** Under leave-last-out, the single held-out next item `y` is **one noisy sample** of an inherently stochastic quantity — "what this user does next." For some contexts the next choice is **highly determined** (a user mid-binge through a franchise; strong recent signal ⇒ low aleatoric variance), for others it is **near-random** (a generic, sparse, or stale history ⇒ high aleatoric variance). Standard cross-entropy applies the **same loss weight to every user**, so the optimizer is forced to drive the loss to zero even on the intrinsically-unpredictable users — i.e. to *over-fit the idiosyncratic single target of an ambiguous context*. That is **exactly the structural val≫test (~0.0088) LLOO over-confidence the analyst keeps diagnosing**: the model fits the val-cut's one held-out item and fails to transfer one step. The free-energy fix is precision-weighting: let the model *estimate the predictability of each user's context* and **attenuate the loss where the next item is inherently uncertain**, spending capacity only where the signal is real. This is the per-context, heteroscedastic generalization of label smoothing — LS lowers confidence **uniformly for every example**; precision-weighting lowers it **adaptively, only for the ambiguous contexts**, leaving the determined contexts sharp.
- **Concrete model mechanism (pure-PyTorch implementable here):** New flag **`--precision-weight` (default OFF ⇒ exact no-op).** The encoder's final-position hidden state `h_u` (the same vector already dotted with items at L693-694) is the context. (a) Add a **one-line precision head**: `self.logvar = nn.Linear(d_model, 1)` with **`weight`/`bias` zero-init** ⇒ `s_u ≡ logvar(h_u) = 0` for every example at init. (b) In `chunked_full_softmax_loss` (L698-764) the per-example CE is already `ce_u = lse_u − target_score_u` (L752); replace the final `ce.mean()` with the **precision-weighted free-energy loss** `L = mean( exp(−s_u)·ce_u + λ·s_u )`, where `s_u = logvar(h_u)` is the per-user log-variance and `λ` (default 1.0, the Kendall–Gal `½`-style penalty weight) prevents the trivial `s_u→+∞` (down-weight-everything) collapse. **At init `s_u=0 ⇒ exp(0)=1, λ·0=0 ⇒ L = mean(ce_u)` = bit-identical current CE.** As training proceeds the model raises `s_u` for ambiguous contexts (loss attenuated, gradient shrunk) and lowers it for determined ones (loss sharpened). (c) **Training-only — eval scoring is untouched**: ranking is still `h·all_items` (L693-694), `s_u` never enters the eval logits, so there is **zero eval-time manipulation** and **no popularity-trap exposure** (the failure mode the supervisor flagged for XF-2 and that killed eval-side levers). Optional richer variant: the Kendall–Gal *logit-corruption* form — perturb the target score `t̃ = target_score + σ_u·ε, ε~N(0,1)`, `σ_u=softplus(s_u)`, MC-average `T≈5` (a few extra cheap evals of the already-computed target logit); defer unless the scalar-weight form shows signal. Cost: one `Linear(d,1)` + two elementwise ops per step (negligible).
- **Why no CS person would default to this:** A recsys/ML practitioner facing over-confidence reaches for **uniform** label smoothing, temperature scaling, or focal loss — all **homoscedastic** (same softening for every example). Heteroscedastic *aleatoric* attenuation does exist in CS, but **only in regression / dense computer-vision** (depth, segmentation, pose — Kendall & Gal); **nobody applies a learned per-context precision to the next-item softmax CE in sequential recommendation** — the orthodoxy is "every training example is an equally-valid label; re-weighting them is throwing away data." The *free-energy* framing — "the model should learn **which users are inherently unpredictable** and stop trying to over-fit their one observed next-click" — is a perceptual-neuroscience normative principle (precision = neuromodulatory gain), not a recsys instinct. The interpretable learned `s_u` (a per-user predictability score) is itself a finding no standard pipeline produces.
- **Novelty vs our ledger:** This is the **first probe on an entirely untouched axis — the per-example *magnitude/precision* of the loss.** Every one of the ~57 ledger probes treats the loss as a **uniform sum** over examples; levers vary the *target shape* (LS = uniform floor; heat-kernel XF-5 = semantic spread; Tsallis XF-3 = tail exponent), the *embedding* (TAPE, James–Stein X1, causal filter), or the *score* — but **none re-weights examples by learned context confidence.** Distinct from **label-smoothing** (the confirmed lever): LS is the **homoscedastic, input-independent** special case (one global ε for all); this is its **heteroscedastic, input-conditioned** generalization (a per-`h_u` learned σ). Distinct from **focal loss** (a fixed `(1−p)^γ` function of the *prediction*, not a learned function of the *context*; and focal *up*-weights hard examples, the opposite move). It is squarely in the empirically **proven-to-transfer class** (anti-over-confidence regularizer, training-only, capacity-reducing — the same class as LS and the causal filter, the only two confirmed gains), which is precisely why it is a high-prior next lever.
- **Feasibility / risk:** Zero-init no-op, one `Linear(d_model,1)` + two elementwise ops, training-only, leak-free by construction (no item-frequency, no eval-side term) ⇒ maximally safe; ~7 min/VG run; stacks cleanly on the V2 winning stack (or on U2/LS0.2 for clean attribution of *precision-on-top-of-uniform*). **Apply the analyst's ep10–20 gap go/no-go** (ANALYSIS 2026-06-16-2 #5): a genuine precision lever should lift test with the ~0.0088 gap *stable or shrinking* (it attacks the over-confidence that drives the gap); if instead the gap widens past ~0.011 with flat test it is the c3 pattern → kill early. Main failure mode: the model could drive `s_u→0` everywhere (clean negative = "no exploitable heteroscedasticity in VG contexts," itself an honest finding) or, if `λ` is too small, collapse to `s_u→∞` (down-weight-everything → degenerate); guard with `λ≈1.0` and clamp `s_u∈[−4,4]`. Second consideration: because it only *re-weights* training, it cannot help if the val≫test gap is purely the documented LLOO timing shift rather than over-confidence — but LS already proved some of that gap *is* convertible by an anti-confidence regularizer, so a per-context version has real upside. The learned `s_u` distribution (which users/histories the model marks unpredictable) is an interpretable readout regardless of the test delta.

## XF 2026-06-16-7 — Quantum cognition / quantum probability: a zero-init phase-interference channel for choice suppression in the score  [status: PASS 2026-06-20 (in-field): MODEL-LEVER (score form), foreclosed. New-lever search CLOSED (cycles 4/6/7/8, CF1); cycle-8 CONVERGENCE forbids new levers/experiments. Also the author's own narrow-novelty caveat (complex/Born-rule recsys already exists) + eval-touching risk make it a weak bet even absent the freeze. Foreclosed, not refuted on merit.]
- **Domain & principle:** **Quantum probability theory of cognition** (Busemeyer & Bruza 2012; Wang, Busemeyer, Atmanspacher — *Topics in Cognitive Science* 2013). Human choice violates **classical (Kolmogorov) probability**: order effects, the conjunction/disjunction fallacy, and preference reversals are *not* expressible by any additive-probability model. The quantum account: a choice state is a vector in Hilbert space, alternatives are amplitudes, and **probability = |amplitude|² (Born rule)**, so two contributions can **destructively interfere** — `|a+b|² = |a|²+|b|²+2|a||b|cos Δφ`, and when the relative **phase** `Δφ→π` the cross term is *negative*, i.e. **presenting/holding option B can SUPPRESS the probability of option A** below the sum of their independent supports. Classical additive evidence accumulation (and the dot-product softmax) **cannot** produce this suppression — it only ever adds non-negative support.
- **The analogy:** Our score is `s(y) = h · v_y` where `h = Σ_t α_t·enc(x_t)` is an **additive** pool over the history (L693-694). Every past item contributes **independently and additively** to every candidate's score — the model **cannot represent "because the user already played franchise-X item, candidate-Y (a near-duplicate / saturated-niche / wrong-mood sequel) is *suppressed*"** as a genuine destructive interaction; it can only *add* support. (This is the *same* red-bus/blue-bus / similarity-effect pathology XF-1 attacked at the loss — here attacked at the **score/representation**, where choice suppression actually lives.) Quantum cognition says the missing ingredient is **phase**: give items a phase so that a candidate whose phase is *anti-aligned* with the user's recent-history phase gets a **negative interference term**, letting the model express context-dependent *suppression*, not just accumulation — the exact non-additive structure that classical choice models (and our softmax) provably lack.
- **Concrete model mechanism (pure-PyTorch implementable here):** New flag **`--phase-interference` (default OFF ⇒ exact no-op).** Keep the entire real model. Add a **low-rank learnable phase** per item: `self.phase = nn.Embedding(n_items, r)` with `r≈4`, **zero-init**, plus a history-phase pool `φ_h = Σ_t α_t·phase(x_t)` (reuse the encoder's attention weights `α_t`, already computed). The score gains **one additive interference channel**: `s(y) = h·v_y + γ · Σ_{c=1..r} cos(φ_h[c] − phase(y)[c])`, where **`γ = nn.Parameter(0.0)`** ⇒ at init the channel is `γ·(…)=0` and, with zero-init phases, `cos(0)=1` constant (absorbed by the softmax shift-invariance) ⇒ **bit-identical to the current dot-product score.** As `γ` and the phases train, candidates phase-anti-aligned with the recent history (`cos→−1`) are **suppressed** (negative contribution) and phase-aligned ones reinforced — a genuine Born-rule-style cross term folded into the existing score, computable as `r≈4` extra cos terms per candidate (cheap; vectorizes over the chunked-softmax item loop). This applies in **both** `forward` and `chunked_full_softmax_loss` so train/eval score identically (the interference is a representational change, **not a popularity channel** — it does not inject item frequency, so it does *not* court the eval popularity-inflation trap the supervisor flagged for XF-2; like dot-vs-cosine it is a legitimate score-geometry change).
- **Why no CS person would default to this:** ML scores items with a **real inner product** whose every term is additive; the idea that two pieces of evidence should be able to **cancel** is a quantum-probability (and quantum-cognition) move motivated by *empirical violations of classical choice probability*, not a representation-learning instinct. A recsys engineer reaches for a gating MLP or a cross-attention term — not "endow items with a relative **phase** so the score carries a Born-rule interference term that lets recent history **suppress** a candidate." The motivation (order/context effects in human choice are quantum-like) is decision-psychology, not CS.
- **Novelty vs our ledger:** No ledger probe touches the **bilinear/score form** — every probe keeps the **additive real dot product** (cosine-vs-dot only rescaled it). This adds a **non-additive interference term** to the score, a structurally new lever. **HONEST prior-art caveat (must be cited, do not over-claim):** complex-valued / Born-rule *sequential recommenders* already exist (web search surfaced a complex-valued-wavefunction-under-a-learned-Hamiltonian + Born-rule sequential model, and quantum-inspired complex embeddings in IR/recsys). The novelty here is **NOT** "complex embeddings for recsys" (occupied) — it is the **minimal, zero-init, *additive phase-interference channel bolted onto an existing real SOTA model*, motivated specifically by quantum-cognition *choice-suppression / order effects*** rather than a full wavefunction re-architecture; that minimal-ablatable-channel framing on top of the confirmed 0.0674 stack is the defensible contribution. Frame it as such or it is not publishable-novel.
- **Feasibility / risk:** Zero-init `γ` + zero-init phases ⇒ exact no-op; one small `Embedding(n_items, r=4)` + `r` cos terms per candidate (cheap, vectorized). **Higher-risk than XF-6** on three axes the supervisor will weigh: (i) it **touches eval scoring** (intended, but a real architectural change, unlike the training-only XF-6 — run it AFTER the cleaner XF-6); (ii) the prior-art overlap means the novelty bar is "minimal-channel framing," which is narrower; (iii) phases can train to noise and *hurt* (the cross term is unconstrained sign) — mitigate by keeping `r` tiny, `γ` learnable (can shrink to 0 = clean negative), and the analyst's ep10–20 gap go/no-go. Net: a genuinely-new *score-form* axis with a clean no-op, but queue it **below XF-6** (XF-6 is training-only, cleaner-novelty, proven-class; XF-7 is the bolder, eval-touching, narrower-novelty bet). Cheap VG ablation; learned `γ` magnitude + whether suppression concentrates on saturated-franchise candidates are interpretable readouts.

### 2026-06-17 batch — RETARGETED at the LONG-TAIL / COLD-ITEM mission (per ORCHESTRATION CURRENT MISSION + AGENT_FEEDBACK MISSION PIVOT 2026-06-16). Primary metric is now **tail-tercile NDCG@10** (`by_popularity`={tail,mid,head}); the thesis is *the text channel rescues rare/cold items where the ID-embedding channel structurally fails*. These ideas are aimed at that regime, not overall NDCG. They also explicitly engineer against the analyst's diagnosed failure mode: **three consecutive levers (W1 β→−7.42, X1 c→0.004, Y1 T→0.0006) were each a single LEARNABLE scalar the model drove to ≈0 ("voted off").**

## XF 2026-06-17-1 — Statistical physics of complex networks (Bianconi–Barabási "fit-get-richer"): exposure-gated routing from the ID-degree channel to the frozen-text "fitness" channel  [status: GROUNDED→PROPOSAL 2026-06-17-1]
- **Domain & principle:** The **Bianconi–Barabási model** (Bianconi & Barabási 2001). Network growth where the attachment probability is `Π_i = η_i·k_i / Σ_j η_j·k_j` — proportional to BOTH a node's degree `k_i` AND an **intrinsic fitness** `η_i` (everything about the node that isn't its degree). A node's degree then grows as `k(t,t_i,η_i) = m·(t/t_i)^β(η)` with dynamic exponent `β(η)=η/C`, so higher-fitness nodes accrue links faster and a **high-fitness LATECOMER overtakes old high-degree hubs** ("fit-get-richer"; latecomers win, age becomes irrelevant — only fitness determines success). With a broad fitness law the system maps to a Bose gas and can **Bose–Einstein condense** (one fit node grabs a finite fraction of all links). The load-bearing idea: **degree (accumulated popularity) and fitness (intrinsic quality) are SEPARATE channels; for a low-degree node, degree carries almost no signal and fitness is everything.**
- **The analogy:** An item's learned ID embedding `item_emb(ids)` is a **degree/popularity-driven** estimate — it only becomes meaningful after the item accrues interactions (degree). A **cold/tail item is exactly a "low-degree latecomer": its ID vector is near-untrained noise.** But its **frozen text projection `sbert_proj(s)` is its intrinsic FITNESS** — fully observed regardless of exposure. ID methods structurally fail on the tail *because they read degree where there is none*; the mission thesis (text rescues cold items) IS the fit-get-richer principle. B-B prescribes the fix: **when degree is low, route the representation through fitness (text), not degree (ID).**
- **Concrete model mechanism (pure-PyTorch implementable here):** New flag **`--fitness-gate` (default OFF ⇒ exact no-op).** Reuse the leak-free train-only `item_freq` buffer (already registered for X1, `run_sasrec_sbert.py:615`). In `item_features` (L645-663), **damp the ID-vector by an exposure gate** before the additive text/prototype terms: `g_i = 1 − α·σ(−s·(log1p(freq_i) − τ))` (g→1−α for cold/low-freq items, g→1 for warm), and set `e = g_i ⊙ item_emb(ids) + sbert_proj(s) + m`. Parameterize `α = softplus(α_raw)`, `α_raw` init ≈ −8 ⇒ α≈0 ⇒ g≡1 ⇒ **bit-identical to current**. As α grows, cold items' noisy ID vectors are suppressed and their representation is carried by the **frozen-text fitness + prototype mean**; warm items keep their full ID vector. `s` (slope) and `τ` (threshold) are 2 learnable scalars — or **fix τ at the tail/mid tercile boundary in log-freq** to lock the gate to the mission's tail definition. Representation-side (applies identically in `encode` and `all_item_features` scoring) ⇒ **no eval-time popularity-injection trap** — it changes *which representation is trusted*, not a popularity logit. Emit `by_popularity`; the mechanism is engineered to lift the **tail** bucket specifically.
- **Why no CS person would default to this:** The ML reflex for an under-trained rare embedding is to **ADD capacity/signal** (TAPE, augmentation, frequency-tiered dims) or to warm-start it. The B-B move is the opposite: **actively DAMP the ID estimate where exposure is low** and let a separate, exposure-invariant "fitness" channel take over — a network-growth-physics decomposition of *popularity (degree) vs intrinsic quality (fitness)* that a recsys engineer never makes (they let one embedding learn both). Reading the ID table as "degree" and frozen text as "fitness," with an explicit latecomer-advantage gate, is statistical-mechanics-of-networks, not representation learning.
- **Novelty vs our ledger:** Nearest = **X1 (James–Stein, DEAD)** and always-on SBERT features. **The distinction that explains why this can win where X1 failed:** X1 shrank the ID vector toward the **learnable TAPE prototype mean `m`** — but `m` is *itself under-trained for cold semantic clusters* (prototypes get few gradients from rare items), so shrinking a noisy estimate toward another noisy estimate has little to gain, and X1's scalar `c` voted to ≈0.004. XF-8 instead damps the ID vector toward the **FROZEN text projection, which is FULLY INFORMATIVE at zero exposure** — the backoff target is reliable *exactly where the ID is not*. It is also **exposure-GATED with a tail-aligned sigmoid** (not a smooth `c/(c+n)` the model can null globally) and is **judged on TAIL NDCG** (X1 was judged on overall, where any tail gain washes out under head-dominated full-catalog NDCG). Distinct from always-on additive SBERT (`item_features` L661, which adds text to *every* item uniformly): this **removes the unreliable ID channel for cold items only**. New axis: exposure-conditioned channel routing.
- **Feasibility / risk:** Zero-init α ⇒ exact no-op; 2–3 scalar params + reuse of the existing `item_freq` buffer; one elementwise gate in `item_features` (negligible). Leak-free (train-only freq, representation-side). Main failure mode: the model could drive α→0 (clean negative = "no exploitable exposure-routing in VG" — but unlike X1 the *target* channel is reliable, so the prior is materially better); or damping ID could hurt HEAD accuracy if τ/s bleed into warm items — mitigate by fixing τ at the tail-tercile boundary so only the tail band is gated. Report tail vs head deltas; learned α (how much cold-ID the model chose to suppress) is an interpretable readout. Apply the analyst's ep10–20 gap go/no-go.

## XF 2026-06-17-2 — Ecology / coverage estimation (Good–Turing & Chao1 unseen-species): a THEORY-DERIVED, non-learnable cold-item reliability schedule (the antidote to three voted-off scalars)  [status: GROUNDED→PROPOSAL 2026-06-17-1 (gate-shape folded into the XF-8 routing proposal)]
- **Domain & principle:** **Good–Turing frequency estimation** (Good 1953, from Turing) and the **Chao1 richness estimator** (Chao 1984). Good–Turing: an event seen `r` times should have its count adjusted to `r* = (r+1)·N_{r+1}/N_r`, and the total probability reserved for **UNSEEN** events ≈ `N_1/N` (singletons / sample size). Chao1: estimated unseen species = `f₁²/(2 f₂)` (singletons²/2·doubletons), using the rarest classes to infer how many remain undetected. The deep point: **the reliability of a frequency estimate is itself estimable from the frequency-of-frequencies** (how many items were seen once, twice, …) — a sample with many singletons is poorly covered, so rare-item estimates must be discounted toward a backoff **by a theory-fixed amount**, not a tuned one.
- **The analogy:** Each item's interaction count `n_i` is a noisy sample count; the catalog's **singleton/doubleton spectrum tells us how noisy**. The model has no notion of "how much to trust this item's ID vector given the catalog's coverage" — it treats every learned vector as equally reliable. Good–Turing supplies the missing per-item reliability: a singleton item's ID estimate should be discounted toward its content/text prior by the Good–Turing factor. **THE SHARP INSIGHT FOR THIS PIPELINE:** the analyst found three consecutive levers (W1 β, X1 c, Y1 T) DEAD because each was a **single LEARNABLE scalar the model drove to ≈0**. Good–Turing gives the cold-item backoff a **schedule DERIVED FROM DATA, not learned** — it *cannot collapse to zero* because it is not a free parameter. This directly attacks the documented failure mode.
- **Concrete model mechanism (pure-PyTorch implementable here):** New flag **`--good-turing-backoff` (default OFF ⇒ no-op).** Precompute once at load (train-only, leak-free): the frequency-of-frequencies `N_r` and a per-item **Good–Turing reliability** `ρ_i = n_i*/n_i` (clamp to [0,1]; ρ→1 for well-covered head items, ρ↓ for singletons; use the standard simple-Good–Turing / log-linear `N_r` smoothing for sparse high `r`). Register `ρ` as a buffer. **Route (a) — DERIVED gate (primary):** use `ρ_i` as the ID-channel weight in `item_features`: `g_i = 1 − β·(1−ρ_i)`, `e = g_i ⊙ item_emb(ids) + sbert_proj(s) + m`, with a single global `β = softplus(β_raw)` (β init 0 ⇒ no-op). The model can only turn the schedule **on/off**; the **per-item SHAPE is theory-fixed**, so it can't null the structure the way X1's `c` nulled the whole effect. (This is exactly XF-8's gate with a non-learnable schedule substituted for the learned sigmoid — the two compose: XF-8 supplies the routing, XF-9 supplies the gate it can't kill.) **Route (b) — reserve-mass target:** Good–Turing reserves `N_1/N` for the unseen ⇒ add a small **training-target floor on the COLD band only** (mass ∝ local coverage deficit) — a popularity-aware label smoothing that floors tail items rather than uniformly (LS) or by text geometry (heat-target). Route (a) is the cleanest first probe. Cost: O(n) precompute + one elementwise op.
- **Why no CS person would default to this:** Good–Turing lives in NLP n-gram smoothing, but as a **per-item embedding-reliability schedule keyed to the catalog's species-coverage spectrum** it is sampling/coverage theory, not representation learning. The orthodox CS fix for rare embeddings is a **learned** gate/regularizer — precisely what voted itself off three times here. The move "our learnable scalars keep dying ⇒ replace them with a **data-derived schedule from coverage estimation that cannot collapse**" comes from ecology/sampling theory, not ML hyperparameter tuning.
- **Novelty vs our ledger:** Every freq-based lever so far (W1 β, X1 c) was a **learnable single scalar the model nulled**. This is the first **NON-learnable, data-derived per-item schedule** — structurally different and specifically engineered against the voted-off failure mode. Distinct from LS (uniform floor) and heat-target (semantic spread): route (b) floors the **cold band by coverage deficit**. Distinct from IPS (a loss reweighting): this is a representation-reliability schedule. Composes with XF-8 (provides its gate) but stands alone.
- **Feasibility / risk:** Leak-free (train-only freq-of-freq), no-op start (β=0), cheap. Main risk: simple Good–Turing is unstable at sparse high `r` (needs the standard smoothed/log-linear `N_r` fit — well documented); if the catalog's coverage is actually good (ρ≈1 everywhere) ⇒ near no-op (honest null). Route (b)'s floor touches the loss target only (training-only, no eval gaming). Report tail vs head deltas; the realized ρ-distribution (how poorly-covered VG's tail is) is itself an interpretable, paper-worthy readout.

## XF 2026-06-17-3 — Welfare economics (Atkinson inequality-aversion EDE): training-only tail-exposure equity regularizer — WITH an explicit generalized-Gini prior-art caveat  [status: PASS 2026-06-20 (in-field): MODEL-LEVER (training-only exposure-equity regularizer), foreclosed. New-lever search CLOSED (cycles 4/6/7/8, CF1); cycle-8 CONVERGENCE forbids new levers. Author already flags this as the weakest-novelty of its batch (differentiable generalized-Gini ranking-fairness regularizers exist). Foreclosed, not refuted on merit.]
- **Domain & principle:** The **Atkinson index** (Atkinson 1970). Social welfare via the **"equally distributed equivalent" (EDE)**: with CRRA utility `u(y)=y^(1−ε)/(1−ε)`, `EDE = (E[y^{1−ε}])^{1/(1−ε)}` and `A_ε = 1 − EDE/mean`. The **inequality-aversion parameter ε** is an explicit ethical knob: ε→0 weights every unit equally; ε→∞ puts all weight on the poorest. Unlike Gini, ε *concentrates sensitivity on the bottom of the distribution* in a single interpretable scalar.
- **The analogy:** The model's predicted next-item **exposure** across the catalog is extremely unequal (head-concentrated); the "income distribution" is the softmax mass per item and the **tail items are the "poor."** A welfare planner with aversion ε raises the EDE by lifting the bottom. A **training-only** regularizer that pushes the predicted-exposure distribution toward higher Atkinson-EDE (lower inequality), with ε controlling how hard the tail is favored, encourages the encoder to keep tail items competitive — attacking head-concentration at its source rather than re-ranking post-hoc.
- **Concrete model mechanism (pure-PyTorch implementable here):** New flag **`--atkinson-eps` with weight `--atkinson-lambda` (default λ=0 ⇒ exact no-op).** In `chunked_full_softmax_loss`, accumulate per-item predicted mass over the batch's union of targets + the chunked catalog items `q_j` (softmax probs already computed); compute the power-mean `EDE_ε = (mean_j q_j^{1−ε})^{1/(1−ε)}` and add `+λ·(mean_q − EDE_ε)` (the Atkinson absolute inequality gap) to the loss — minimizing it raises tail mass. ε swept `{0.5,1,2}` (ε=1 ⇒ log/Theil form); λ=0 ⇒ no-op. **Training-only: eval scoring untouched ⇒ no eval-gaming trap.** Cost: a power-mean over the batch item-union per step.
- **Why no CS person would default to this:** Facing exposure fairness, a recsys engineer reaches for IPS or Gini re-ranking; the **Atkinson EDE with an explicit CRRA inequality-aversion ε** — concentrating loss pressure on the tail via a social-welfare function — is a public-economics framing, not an ML one.
- **Novelty vs our ledger AND HONEST PRIOR-ART CAVEAT:** This is the **weakest-novelty of the three and I flag it as such.** Web research shows **differentiable Gini / GENERALIZED-Gini exposure-fairness ranking regularizers ALREADY EXIST in CS** (e.g. "Optimizing Generalized Gini Indices for Fairness in Rankings"; differentiable Gini upper bounds for recommendation). Generalized-Gini already offers tunable inequality weighting, so a plain exposure-inequality regularizer is **not novel**. The only defensible narrow novelty is (i) the **Atkinson-EDE/CRRA form with a single interpretable ε** (vs the Gini weight *vector*), (ii) applied to the **sequential next-item softmax mass at training**, and (iii) evaluated as a **tail-NDCG** lever under this mission. Nearest ledger probe = W1 niche-share (a crowding penalty, DEAD on overall) — Atkinson is a *distribution-level welfare objective*, not a per-item crowding term. **Queue BELOW XF-8/XF-9** as a candidate, not a headline; its main value is as a training-only tail-equity **baseline** to compare against the representation-routing ideas.
- **Feasibility / risk:** λ=0 no-op; training-only/leak-free; cheap power-mean over the batch item-union. Main risk: it trades head accuracy for tail (acceptable/desired under the tail mission, but watch overall doesn't collapse); the prior art caps the publishability of *this lever alone* — its role is a comparison baseline. ε/λ sweepable. Note `q_j` over the full 25k catalog per step is costly — restrict to the chunked-softmax item-union actually scored, an unbiased-enough proxy.

**Handoff to in-field (priority order): XF-8 (fit-get-richer routing) FIRST — it is the literal mission thesis (text rescues cold items) with a *reliable* cold-item channel and answers X1's exact failure; XF-9 SECOND — it hardens XF-8's gate against the three-voted-off-scalars failure mode with a non-learnable, coverage-derived schedule (and can be run as XF-8's `ρ`-gate variant in the same code site); XF-10 (Atkinson) only as a training-only tail-equity baseline given its generalized-Gini prior art.** Sources: [Bianconi–Barabási model](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bianconi%E2%80%93Barab%C3%A1si_model); [Chao1 / Good–Turing coverage](https://www.statology.org/how-to-calculate-and-interpret-chao1-species-richness-estimator/); [Atkinson index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atkinson_index); prior-art caveat: [Optimizing Generalized Gini Indices for Fairness in Rankings](https://www.researchgate.net/publication/361828920_Optimizing_Generalized_Gini_Indices_for_Fairness_in_Rankings).

### 2026-06-17 batch #2 — RETARGETED per SUPERVISOR FEEDBACK 2026-06-17 cycle 3. The tail-rescue-via-text thesis is single-seed REFUTED; the robust signal is a **monotone** text-lift ordering **tail −7.6% < head +2.6% < mid +14.5%** ("text binds in proportion to per-item DATA, not inversely"). The supervisor explicitly retargeted researchers to: **(a)** mid-density-regime mechanisms where text wins; **(b)** genuinely **non-text, TRAINING-ONLY** tail mechanisms (eval-side popularity/exposure correction is auto-rejected); **(c)** **theory for why the tail is irreducibly sparsity-bottlenecked**, which would harden the honest negative. Also binding: stop proposing "add text to cold items"; and the three-voted-off-scalars failure mode (W1 β, X1 c, Y1 T → all driven to ≈0) means any **single learnable scalar lever is dead-on-arrival** — mechanisms must be **non-collapsible / data-derived**. The two ideas below are aimed precisely at (a)+(c) with non-collapsible mechanisms; the first is a deep centerpiece that does triple duty.

## XF 2026-06-17-4 — Random-matrix theory (BBP detectability transition + optimal shrinkage): a DATA-DERIVED per-item ID-reliability schedule that explains the mid-win, predicts the tail-null, and cannot be voted off  [status: GROUNDED→PROPOSAL 2026-06-17-2 — grounded via its Gavish–Donoho optimal-shrinkage variant (parameter-free SVD denoising of the item-embedding MATRIX spectrum), NOT the literal per-item ρ_i convex blend: the literal blend routes cold items off ID onto text, which at the deep tail is bit-identical to the KILLED Z1/fitness-gate (α=1 crashed the tail −75%) ⇒ that arm is the killed cold-text-routing direction and was discarded. The spectral-shrinkage realization keeps every item's ID channel, denoises only the shared spectrum, is non-collapsible (no learnable scalar), and is the embedding-space dual of the confirmed causal temporal filter. The per-item BBP ρ_i schedule is retained as the directive-(c) diagnostic logged by the proposal.]
- **Domain & principle:** The **Baik–Ben Arous–Péché (BBP) phase transition** for the spiked covariance/Wigner model, and **Marchenko–Pastur (MP)** spectral theory with **Gavish–Donoho optimal singular-value shrinkage**. Core law: estimate a rank-1 signal direction (population eigenvector) from `n` noisy samples in `p` dimensions, aspect ratio `γ = p/n`. The *sample* top eigenvector's squared overlap with the *true* direction is a **hard phase transition**: it is **exactly 0 when the signal strength (SNR) `ℓ ≤ ℓ₊ = 1 + √γ`**, and for `ℓ > ℓ₊` it rises as `overlap² = (1 − γ/(ℓ−1)²) / (1 + γ/(ℓ−1))` (Benaych-Georges & Nadakuditi; standard spiked-model result). Below threshold the estimate is **information-theoretically uninformative** — *no estimator* recovers the direction, more capacity/text cannot help; above it the estimate aligns, and the MP/Gavish–Donoho law gives the **closed-form optimal shrinkage** of that noisy estimate (parameter-free, data-derived, AMSE-optimal). The deep point: **estimation reliability is a known, sharp function of `(samples, dimension, SNR)` — not a free knob.**
- **The analogy:** Each catalog item's learned **ID embedding `item_emb(ids)` is a noisy estimate of that item's "true taste direction," built from exactly `n_i` interaction events in `d = 64` dims.** So per item, `γ_i = d / n_i`. **Tail items** have `n_i` of a few ⇒ `γ_i ≫ 1` ⇒ threshold `ℓ₊ = 1+√(d/n_i)` is huge ⇒ for any plausible fixed signal strength the ID estimate sits **below the BBP transition: overlap² = 0, provably uninformative**. **Head items** (`n_i` large ⇒ `γ_i → 0`) are well above threshold ⇒ overlap → 1, ID is reliable, text is redundant. **Mid items** sit *just above threshold*, where overlap is partial — precisely the regime where a *second, fully-observed* channel (frozen text) maximally complements the half-formed ID estimate. This reproduces the analyst's monotone finding **exactly**: tail null (below transition) → head small text gain (ID already reliable, text redundant, +2.6%) → **mid peak text gain (ID just-detectable, text complements, +14.5%)**. RMT is the theory the supervisor asked for in (c): *the tail is irreducibly sparsity-bottlenecked because per-item ID estimation is below the detectability threshold — a hard information floor, not a modelling deficiency.*
- **Concrete model mechanism (pure-PyTorch implementable here):** New flag **`--bbp-reliability` (default OFF ⇒ exact no-op).** (a) Reuse the **leak-free train-only `item_freq` buffer** already registered for X1 (`run_sasrec_sbert.py:615`). (b) Precompute a **NON-learnable per-item reliability** `ρ_i = clamp( (1 − γ_i/(ℓ−1)²) / (1 + γ_i/(ℓ−1)), 0, 1 )` with `γ_i = d_model / max(n_i, 1)` and `ℓ` a **fixed assumed-SNR constant** (a derived *hyperparameter*, swept `ℓ∈{2,4,8}`, **not** a per-item learnable scalar — this is the antidote to W1/X1/Y1) ⇒ `ρ_i = 0` for items below the BBP transition (the deep tail), rising smoothly to 1 for the head; register as a buffer. (c) In `item_features` (≈L645–663, where `e = item_emb(ids) + sbert_proj(s) + m`), replace with an **inverse-variance / BLUE fusion** of the ID channel (reliability `ρ_i`) and the frozen-text channel (reliability ≈ const): `w_i = ρ_i / (ρ_i + (1−ρ_i))` simplified to the **convex blend** `e = ρ_i ⊙ item_emb(ids) + (1−ρ_i) ⊙ sbert_proj(s) + m` — i.e. *trust the ID vector exactly as much as RMT says it is recoverable, and fall back to fully-observed text for the rest*. At the master-flag-OFF state `ρ_i ≡ 1` ⇒ `e = item_emb + sbert + m` = **bit-identical to current**. Applies identically in `encode` and `all_item_features` (representation-side; **no eval-time popularity logit ⇒ does not court the eval-manipulation trap**). Cost: one O(n) precompute + one elementwise blend. **Ambitious variant (defer):** apply **Gavish–Donoho optimal shrinkage to the ID embedding matrix spectrum** once per epoch (SVD of the `n×d` table, shrink singular values below the MP edge to 0, optimal-shrink the rest) — a parameter-free *restricted inductive bias on the item spectrum*, the embedding-space analogue of the confirmed causal **temporal**-spectrum filter. Start with the cheap blend; the spectral version is the higher-novelty follow-up if the blend shows a mid signal.
- **Why no CS person would default to this:** The recsys reflex for an under-trained rare embedding is to **add capacity / signal** (TAPE, augmentation, frequency-tiered dims) or warm-start it; nobody asks *"is this direction even estimable from `n_i` samples in `d` dims?"* RMT answers that there is a **hard detectability floor** below which the question is closed — and prescribes the **exact, parameter-free** trust weight above it. Treating the ID table as a bank of spiked-covariance estimates with per-item BBP thresholds, and fusing by inverse-variance, is statistical-physics-of-random-matrices, not representation learning. It is also the *opposite* move to the field's instinct: it says *the tail cannot be fixed by any channel*, and quantifies why.
- **Novelty vs our ledger:** Nearest probes are **X1 (James–Stein, DEAD)**, **Z1/XF-8 (fitness-gate, QUEUED)**, and **XF-9 (Good–Turing schedule)**. Crucial distinctions: **X1** used a *learnable* `c` (voted to ≈0.004) and shrank toward the *learnable, also-under-trained* TAPE prototype mean — a noisy target; this uses a **non-learnable closed-form `ρ_i(n_i,d,ℓ)`** and fuses toward the **frozen, fully-observed text** (reliable exactly where ID is not), and is judged on the **mid/tail buckets** X1 washed out. **Z1/XF-8** uses a *learnable-α sigmoid* gate (collapsible); this supplies the **BBP detectability curve as the gate SHAPE** (non-collapsible) — it is the principled, voted-off-proof hardening of Z1's gate, and adds the **detectability theorem Z1 lacks**. **XF-9** derives its schedule from the *coverage* spectrum (frequency-of-frequencies); this derives it from the *signal-detection* spectrum (SNR vs dimension) with an explicit **phase transition** — complementary, different mechanism. Prior-art caveat (honest): spectral embedding regularizers exist — **"Balancing Embedding Spectrum for Recommendation" (arXiv:2406.12032)** optimizes the *global* embedding-matrix spectrum to prevent collapse; it does **not** use per-item interaction count, the **BBP detectability threshold**, or **per-item frequency-derived fusion** — the per-item-`n_i`-keyed reliability and the detectability theorem are the defensible novelty, not "RMT for recsys" in general.
- **Feasibility / risk:** Zero-risk no-op flag; one O(n) precompute + one elementwise blend reusing an existing buffer; ~7 min/VG run; emits `by_popularity`. **Non-collapsible by construction** — there is no learnable scalar the model can null (the `ℓ`-sweep is a fixed hyperparameter), so it gives a *direct, un-nullable measurement* either way (the lesson from the three voted-off levers). Honest expected outcome, given the analyst's data: **tail stays null** (below threshold, `ρ→0`, representation→text, and text already shown not to bind rare items) — but that is the *predicted* result and it **hardens the irreducibility theory**; the *live upside* is the **mid regime**, where the BBP-optimal fusion should match or beat the uniform always-on text add (testable as a mid-NDCG lift vs the V2 text stack). Main failure mode: if the blend doesn't lift mid either, the contribution is purely the **theorem** (still high-value per directive (c)). Apply the analyst's ep10–20 gap go/no-go. The realized `ρ_i` distribution (what fraction of VG's catalog is provably below the BBP detection threshold) is itself a paper-worthy interpretable readout that *quantifies* the sparsity bottleneck.

## XF 2026-06-17-5 — Percolation / branching-process threshold (network science): a SECOND, independent irreducibility theorem for the tail (collaborative-signal connectivity floor) + a degree-gated propagation mechanism for the mid  [status: PASS 2026-06-20 (in-field): foreclosed. Arm 2 (graph propagation) is a MODEL-LEVER barred by the CLOSED search (cycles 4/6/7/8, CF1) and is prior-art-limited (LightGCN/long-tail GNN, per the author). Arm 1 (percolation-threshold irreducibility diagnostic) is a read-only CPU artifact, but the second-irreducibility-theorem role is already covered by the GROUNDED BBP/RMT proposal (2026-06-17-2) + the φ_i rigidity artifact (2026-06-19-2); cycle-8 forbids new paper-support proposals. Foreclosed.]
- **Domain & principle:** **Percolation theory & the giant-component (branching-process) threshold.** In a random/co-occurrence graph with mean degree `⟨k⟩`, a macroscopic connected component emerges only above a **sharp percolation threshold** (Erdős–Rényi: `⟨k⟩ = 1`; configuration-model: the Molloy–Reed criterion `⟨k²⟩/⟨k⟩ = 2`). Below it the graph is a dust of tiny isolated fragments; above it information/flow propagates across a giant component. Equivalently a branching process with mean offspring `R₀<1` dies out, `R₀>1` survives — a phase transition in *reachability*.
- **The analogy:** Collaborative signal reaches an item only through its **co-occurrence edges** (items appearing in the same user histories). A learned ID embedding is trained by the gradient flowing through those co-occurrences; a **tail item with too few co-occurrence edges is below the percolation threshold — it is disconnected from the collaborative "giant component," so essentially no collaborative gradient reaches it**, and its ID vector stays at initialization-noise *regardless of model capacity*. This is a **second, mechanism-independent reason the tail is irreducible** (BBP/XF-4 is the *statistical-estimation* floor; percolation is the *graph-connectivity* floor): the two triangulate the honest negative — "the tail is bottlenecked by both sample-count detectability AND co-occurrence connectivity, neither fixable by adding a text channel." The mid regime is the band of items that *just* cross the percolation threshold, where a little propagated collaborative signal binds — matching the +14.5% mid peak.
- **Concrete model mechanism (pure-PyTorch implementable here):** Two arms. **Arm 1 (THEORY/diagnostic, the primary deliverable — zero training risk):** build the train-only item co-occurrence graph once (sparse `n×n`, items co-occurring within a user history; leak-free), compute each item's degree `k_i` and the catalog's Molloy–Reed ratio, and report **which popularity terciles sit below vs above the percolation threshold**. If the deep tail is provably sub-percolation, that is a clean, paper-hardening figure for the honest negative — no GPU training needed, and it can be handed to the analyst as a read-only artifact. **Arm 2 (mechanism, optional):** a **degree-gated one-hop collaborative-signal propagation** of ID embeddings over the co-occurrence graph, `ê_i = e_i + s_i · Σ_{j∈N(i)} (w_ij / k_i) e_j`, where the per-item propagation strength `s_i` is **NOT a free learnable scalar** but a **data-derived degree gate** `s_i = clamp(1 − k_i/k_threshold, 0, 1)` (push borrowed signal *into* just-connected mid items, none into the saturated head, and — honestly — little into the truly-disconnected tail, since it has almost no neighbors to borrow from). Default OFF ⇒ no-op. Training-only/representation-side; emits `by_popularity`.
- **Why no CS person would default to this:** Recsys does use graphs (LightGCN etc.), but the framing here is inverted and physics-first: rather than "propagate to improve everything," it asks *"which items are below the percolation threshold and therefore receive no collaborative signal at all?"* — using the giant-component transition as a **diagnostic of irreducibility**, and gating propagation by each node's *distance to threshold* rather than a global learned weight. The headline is a **negative-result theorem** (a connectivity floor), which no recommender paper sets out to prove.
- **Novelty vs our ledger AND HONEST PRIOR-ART CAVEAT (I flag this as the weaker-novelty of the two):** **Graph propagation of embeddings is heavily prior-arted** (LightGCN, GNN long-tail methods — see search) ⇒ Arm 2 *as a mechanism* is **NOT novel** and is at real risk of the votable-weight failure mode if `s_i` were learnable (it must stay the data-derived degree gate). The defensible contributions are narrow: **(i)** Arm 1 — the **percolation-threshold diagnostic as a second irreducibility argument** for the honest negative (genuinely uncommon framing, and exactly directive (c)); **(ii)** the **degree-to-threshold-gated, non-learnable** propagation strength (vs the usual global learned propagation), positioned as a *mid-regime* lever, not a tail fix. No ledger probe builds a co-occurrence graph at all — this is a new data structure for the campaign. Nearest probe = none directly; X1/Z1 are per-item representation gates, not graph-coupled.
- **Feasibility / risk:** Arm 1 is **pure CPU/read-only analysis (no GPU, no training)** — cheapest possible, and the in-field/analyst can run it without contending for the GPU; it strengthens the paper even if Arm 2 is a null. Arm 2: no-op-OFF flag, sparse matmul one-hop (cheap on VG's ~25k items), reuse `item_freq`/a precomputed sparse adjacency. Main risks: (a) prior-art ceiling on Arm 2's novelty (hence: lead with Arm 1's theory, treat Arm 2 as a comparison mechanism, keep `s_i` non-learnable); (b) the tail has too few neighbors for propagation to help (expected — *that is the point*, and it corroborates irreducibility); (c) building the co-occurrence graph must be train-only (leak-free) — assert it. Queue **below XF-4**; its main value is the **second irreducibility theorem** triangulating BBP, not the propagation mechanism.

**Handoff to in-field (priority order): XF-4 (RMT/BBP) FIRST and deepest — it is non-collapsible by construction (the antidote to the three voted-off scalars), it *quantitatively explains* the observed monotone tail<head<mid pattern, it gives directive (c)'s irreducibility theorem AND a directive (a) mid-regime fusion mechanism in one flag, and it hardens the queued Z1 gate with a principled BBP gate-shape. XF-5 (percolation) SECOND, and primarily as Arm-1 THEORY — a CPU-only, read-only second irreducibility argument (connectivity floor) that triangulates BBP for the honest-negative section; its propagation mechanism (Arm 2) is prior-art-limited and should stay a non-learnable degree-gated comparison lever, not a headline.** Sources: [BBP / spiked-model eigenvector overlap](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2301.05331); [Optimal Shrinkage of Eigenvalues in the Spiked Covariance Model](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1311.0851); [The Optimal Hard Threshold for Singular Values (Gavish–Donoho)](https://www.researchgate.net/publication/236935805_The_Optimal_Hard_Threshold_for_Singular_Values_is); prior-art differentiation: [Balancing Embedding Spectrum for Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.12032); [percolation threshold / giant component](https://www.sciencedirect.com/topics/computer-science/percolation-threshold); [long-tail GNN collaborative filtering (Arm-2 prior art)](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/abs/pii/S0925231225016200).
