### 2026-06-17 batch #3 — RETARGETED at SUPERVISOR FEEDBACK 2026-06-17 cycle 3 directives (a)+(c) with the hard non-collapsibility constraint. The mission's tail-rescue-via-text thesis is single-seed REFUTED; the robust, multi-seed-pending signal is a **monotone** text-lift ordering **tail −7.6% < head +2.6% < mid +14.5%** — "text binds in proportion to per-item DATA, not inversely." The supervisor's LIVE targets are: **(a)** mechanisms for the **mid-density regime where text demonstrably wins** (the real, winnable lever), and **(c)** **theory for why the tail is irreducibly sparsity-bottlenecked** (hardens the honest negative). Binding constraints: **single learnable scalars are dead-on-arrival** (W1 β, X1 c, Y1 T all voted to ≈0) ⇒ mechanisms must be **non-collapsible / data-derived**; and any eval-side popularity/exposure correction is **auto-rejected** ⇒ training/representation-side only. The two ideas below come from a field the ledger has NOT mined (multisensory neuroscience) and a physics framing (fluctuation–dissipation) that XF-4 did not use; both attach at the SAME fusion site `item_features` (`run_sasrec_sbert.py:657-691`, currently the plain add `e = item_emb(ids) + sbert_proj(s) + m`), both are non-collapsible, and both are aimed squarely at directive (a) — *amplify the mid win* — while their reliability curves also predict the tail null (directive c). **The deep distinction from XF-4/BBP, stated up front so in-field doesn't treat these as a re-skin:** XF-4 is a *one-sided* ID-reliability `ρ_i(n_i,d,SNR)` convex blend that treats the **text channel as uniformly reliable** (and its grounded form pivoted to global spectral shrinkage, discarding the per-item blend). XF-6 below adds the missing half — a **per-item TEXT reliability** — and does proper **two-sided inverse-variance (Bayesian-MLE) fusion**, whose math *derives* inverse-effectiveness (peak enhancement at mid, zero at tail) and prescribes *where* to expect the mid gain. XF-7 replaces the *assumed* `n_i`-based reliability with an *empirically measured* per-item learnability, catching the items frequency miscounts.

## XF 2026-06-17-6 — Multisensory neuroscience (principle of inverse effectiveness + Bayesian MLE cue combination): two-sided reliability-weighted fusion of the ID and TEXT channels, with a NEW per-item TEXT reliability  [status: GROUNDED→PROPOSAL 2026-06-17-3]
- **Domain & principle:** **Optimal multisensory cue integration** (Ernst & Banks 2002; Meredith & Stein superior-colliculus work; review PMC5447489). When two noisy sensory channels (e.g. vision σ_v, touch σ_t) estimate the same quantity, the statistically optimal (max-likelihood / MAP, Gaussian) combined estimate is the **inverse-variance-weighted average** `Ŝ = w_v S_v + w_t S_t`, `w_c = (1/σ_c²)/Σ_c'(1/σ_c'²)` — weights = **normalized reliability** (reliability ≡ precision ≡ 1/σ²). Crucially, **Fisher information adds**: the fused precision is `1/σ_fused² = 1/σ_v² + 1/σ_t²`, so the fused estimate is *strictly better than either channel alone*. This yields the **principle of inverse effectiveness (PoIE)**: the *relative* multisensory enhancement `(σ_best − σ_fused)/σ_best` is **largest when both channels are weak-but-comparable, and →0 when one channel is already reliable (it dominates the average) OR when one channel is absent (no second cue to add).** The enhancement is a hump: zero at the extremes, peaked in the middle.
- **The analogy:** Our two channels for an item's representation are **ID** (`item_emb(ids)` — a per-item MLE whose noise σ_ID² shrinks with interaction count `n_i`) and **TEXT** (`sbert_proj(s)` — frozen, exposure-invariant, but with its own per-item noise σ_text² that depends on **how informative the description is**: a long, specific title in a dense semantic neighborhood is a low-variance cue; a 3-word generic title in a sparse region is high-variance). The model **currently fuses by a plain unweighted ADD** (`e = item_emb + sbert_proj(s) + m`, L688/690) — implicitly `w_ID = w_text = 1` for *every* item regardless of either channel's reliability. PoIE says this is exactly wrong, and it *predicts the analyst's monotone curve from first principles*: **head** — ID precise (large `n_i`), σ_ID≪σ_text ⇒ ID dominates, adding full-weight text injects noise ⇒ text barely helps / can hurt (**+2.6%**); **mid** — ID and text *both moderate and comparable* ⇒ the inverse-effectiveness peak, fusion is maximally superadditive (**+14.5%**); **deep tail** — ID is *absent* (below detectability, σ_ID→∞) **AND** the cold item's description is often generic (σ_text also large) ⇒ both cues weak, the precision sum `1/σ_ID²+1/σ_text²` is tiny, no informative fusion exists ⇒ **text gives no rescue (−7.6%/null)**. This is a *second, independent* (neuroscience, not random-matrix) derivation of the tail<head<mid ordering — and unlike XF-4 it tells you the lever: **replace the unweighted add with reliability-weighted fusion so text is up-weighted *exactly in the mid band* where it wins and down-weighted in the head where it's redundant.**
- **Concrete model mechanism (pure-PyTorch implementable here):** New flag **`--cue-fusion` (default OFF ⇒ exact no-op).** All reliabilities are **data-derived buffers, NOT learnable scalars** (the non-collapsibility requirement). Precompute once at load (leak-free, train-only):
  - **ID precision** `π_ID,i = n_i` (interaction count; Fisher info of a mean estimate ∝ sample size) — reuse the existing `item_freq` buffer (L616/649). Optionally the BBP-detectability form `π_ID,i ∝ max(n_i − d_model, 0)` so deep-tail ID precision hits exactly 0 (ties this to XF-4's threshold).
  - **TEXT precision** `π_text,i` = a **NEW per-item text-informativeness signal**, the load-bearing novelty no prior idea has: e.g. `π_text,i = ‖sbert_proj(s_i)‖² · (1 / local_text_density_i)` or, simplest, the **z-scored token count of the description × inverse mean-cosine-to-K-nearest-text-neighbors** (a generic title in a crowded region = low precision; a specific title in a sparse region = high precision). Computed once from the frozen SBERT table — no training, no leakage, no eval-popularity term.
  - **Fusion** in `item_features`, replacing `e = item_emb + sbert_proj(s) + m` with the inverse-variance combination: `w_ID,i = π_ID,i/(π_ID,i+π_text,i)`, `w_text,i = π_text,i/(π_ID,i+π_text,i)`, then `e = w_ID,i ⊙ item_emb(ids) + w_text,i ⊙ sbert_proj(s) + m`. **No-op control:** gate behind the master flag; when OFF, `w_ID=w_text=1` (the current add) bit-identically. Because both weights are fixed functions of frozen data, there is **no scalar the optimizer can null** — the result is a direct, un-votable measurement. Representation-side (applies identically in `encode` and `all_item_features`); it is a fusion-geometry change like dot-vs-cosine, **not** a popularity logit at eval, so it does not court the eval-manipulation trap. (Optional secondary — *confidence calibration*: also scale the fused norm by the total precision `√(π_ID,i+π_text,i)` normalized across the catalog, so provably-low-precision deep-tail items are softly down-weighted in the softmax; this *does* touch eval scoring as a representation change — run it only after the clean fusion, and flag it for the supervisor's eval-side scrutiny.)
- **Why no CS person would default to this:** A recsys engineer fuses ID and text by **concatenation, a gating MLP, or a plain sum** — all of which either weight the two channels *uniformly* or learn a weight *end-to-end* (which, here, is exactly the single-scalar gate the model voted off three times). The idea that the fusion weight should be the **normalized inverse-variance reliability of each channel, computed from first principles**, and that this *automatically produces a superadditive enhancement peaked at intermediate reliability*, is the Ernst–Banks/superior-colliculus account of perception — a sensory-neuroscience normative law, not a representation-learning instinct. And the specific move "give the **text** channel its own per-item reliability from description informativeness" is something no recsys pipeline does — text is always treated as uniformly trustworthy.
- **Novelty vs our ledger:** Nearest probes are **XF-4/BBP** and always-on additive SBERT. **Three concrete distinctions from XF-4** (stated honestly, since both are reliability-weighted fusions): (1) XF-4 is **one-sided** — it derives only the *ID* reliability and treats text as constant; XF-6's defensible core is the **per-item TEXT reliability** (informativeness), which is what lets fusion *re-balance toward text in the mid band and away from it in the head* — the directive-(a) mid amplifier XF-4 lacks. (2) XF-4's grounded form **abandoned the per-item blend** for global Gavish–Donoho spectral shrinkage (a denoiser); XF-6 keeps the **per-item two-channel MLE fusion** that the spectral form discarded, and is *not* equivalent to cold-text-routing (Z1/fitness-gate, KILLED) because at the deep tail *both* precisions are low ⇒ it does **not** dump cold items onto full-strength text (the killed move) — it correctly predicts *no* reliable fusion exists there. (3) It supplies a **falsifiable superadditivity test** (below) that no prior probe has. Distinct from X1 (learnable `c`, voted off; shrinks ID toward the *under-trained* TAPE mean) and Z1 (learnable-α sigmoid, collapsible). New axis: **two-sided, data-derived, inverse-variance channel fusion with an explicit text-reliability signal.** Honest prior-art caveat: reliability-weighted multimodal fusion exists in deep learning (uncertainty-weighted multi-task/sensor-fusion, Kendall–Gal-style); the narrow novelty is the **frozen-data-derived (non-learnable) per-item ID+text precision fusion, motivated by PoIE, evaluated on the popularity-banded NDCG**, and the text-informativeness precision — not "uncertainty-weighted fusion" in general. Frame it that way or it isn't publishable-novel.
- **Feasibility / risk:** Zero-init no-op flag; 2 precomputed buffers + 2 elementwise weights at the existing fusion site (negligible cost); ~7 min/VG run; emits `by_popularity`. **Non-collapsible by construction** (no learnable scalar). Leak-free (train-only `n_i`; frozen-text informativeness). **Built-in falsification (the clean test the supervisor wants):** run the three configs ID-only / text-only / fused, per popularity band, and measure **superadditivity** `NDCG_fused − max(NDCG_ID, NDCG_text)` per band — PoIE predicts it is **largest in mid, ≈0 in tail**; if the data shows that, the mechanism *and* the irreducibility story are jointly confirmed; if fusion doesn't beat the plain add in mid, that is itself a clean negative ("uniform add is already near-optimal; VG's per-item reliabilities are too flat to exploit"). Main failure mode: the text-informativeness precision is a *proxy* and could be miscalibrated (a bad title-length heuristic) — mitigate by trying 2 simple definitions (norm-based, neighbor-density-based) and reporting which, and by noting that even a crude precision that merely *down-weights redundant head-text* is a testable prediction. Apply the analyst's ep10–20 gap go/no-go. The realized per-item `w_text` distribution (where the model is told to trust text) is an interpretable, paper-worthy readout that should **peak in the mid tercile** if the theory holds.

## XF 2026-06-17-7 — Statistical physics (fluctuation–dissipation): per-item learnability measured from embedding gradient-noise, as an EMPIRICAL (non-assumed) fusion reliability  [status: PASS 2026-06-20 (in-field): MODEL-LEVER (fusion-reliability variant), foreclosed AND empirically pre-empted. The reliability-weighted ID×text fusion family it feeds was GROUNDED and RAN as CF1 (PROPOSAL 2026-06-17-3) → DEAD on BOTH VG and MI (no mid lift), which is the result that CLOSED the new-lever search. A gradient-noise-measured reliability is unlikely to revive a fusion mechanism already shown additive-optimal. Cycle-8 CONVERGENCE bars new levers regardless. Foreclosed + pre-empted.]
- **Domain & principle:** The **fluctuation–dissipation theorem** (Einstein 1905; Kubo 1966) — at stationarity, a system's *response* (how stiffly a coordinate is pinned by the potential) is tied to its *fluctuations* (the equilibrium variance of that coordinate): a well-determined coordinate sits in a steep, narrow well (small fluctuations); a poorly-determined one sits in a flat well (large fluctuations). Under SGD-as-Langevin-dynamics, the analogue is exact (Yaida 2018, *Fluctuation–dissipation relations for SGD*, arXiv:1810.00004): a parameter whose loss landscape is **well-constrained by data** has a *small* stationary gradient-noise/fluctuation, while an **under-determined** parameter (few or conflicting gradients) wanders with *large* fluctuation. The signal-to-noise of a parameter's gradient history is therefore a **direct, measured proxy for how reliably that parameter has been estimated** — without any assumption about *why* (it captures sample count, gradient conflict, and curvature jointly).
- **The analogy:** Each item-embedding row `item_emb[i]` is a coordinate in exactly this sense. The **frequency-based reliabilities** used by XF-4/8/9 and XF-6 all assume *reliability ≈ f(n_i)* — but `n_i` is a *crude proxy* for learnability: a **frequent-but-ambiguous** item (appears in many unrelated contexts → conflicting gradients → its vector never settles) is *over-trusted* by an `n_i` rule, and a **rare-but-consistent** item (few interactions, all in one tight context → its vector settles fast) is *under-trusted*. Fluctuation–dissipation says: don't assume reliability from the sample count — **measure it from the embedding's own gradient fluctuations.** An item whose embedding-gradient SNR is high has a well-determined ID vector (trust ID); one whose gradient is all noise has an unlearnable ID vector (lean on text). This is the *empirical* realization of the same two-channel fusion as XF-6, with a reliability that is **observed, not posited** — and it is the natural mechanism for directive (a): the mid regime is precisely the band where ID gradients are *just* starting to settle, which a measured SNR detects more sharply than a frequency bin.
- **Concrete model mechanism (pure-PyTorch implementable here):** New flag **`--fdt-reliability` (default OFF ⇒ no-op).** Maintain two cheap per-item running buffers updated each step from the **already-computed** embedding gradients (a backward hook on `item_emb.weight`, or read `.grad` post-backward): an EMA of the per-row gradient mean `g_i` and of the per-row gradient second moment `v_i` (exactly the statistics Adam already tracks — can be read from optimizer state at near-zero extra cost). Define the **measured ID reliability** `π_ID,i = ‖g_i‖² / (v_i + ε)` (a per-row gradient SNR; high = well-determined, low = wandering), updated with a stop-gradient and a one-step lag (use last step's estimate to weight this step's forward, so no differentiable feedback loop). Feed `π_ID,i` into **the exact XF-6 fusion** in place of (or multiplied with) the `n_i`-based ID precision: `w_ID,i = π_ID,i/(π_ID,i+π_text,i)`. **No learnable scalar anywhere** — the reliability is a measured statistic, so it cannot be voted to zero. Master-flag OFF ⇒ `w_ID=w_text=1` ⇒ current add, bit-identical. Representation-side; training-derived; leak-free (gradients come from train batches only). Cost: 2 length-`n` EMA buffers + one elementwise op; the gradient stats are already materialized by autograd/Adam.
- **Why no CS person would default to this:** The ML reflex for "which embeddings are under-trained" is to **count occurrences** (`n_i`) or add capacity; reading the answer off the **gradient-noise spectrum via a fluctuation–dissipation relation** — treating the embedding table as a thermalized physical system whose per-coordinate fluctuations *report* their own estimation reliability — is statistical-physics-of-learning, not a recsys instinct. And using that measured reliability to set a **fusion weight** (rather than, say, a per-parameter learning rate, which is the usual FDT-for-SGD application) is a further non-default step.
- **Novelty vs our ledger AND honest prior-art caveat:** No ledger probe reads gradient statistics for *anything*; every reliability/frequency lever (W1, X1, Z1, XF-4/8/9, XF-6) uses **`n_i` or a closed-form**, none uses the **measured** learnability. **Prior-art flag (must cite, do not over-claim):** FDT-for-SGD exists (Yaida 2018) and per-parameter gradient-variance is used for *learning-rate adaptation* (Adam/AdaGrad) and for *curriculum/example-weighting*; the narrow, defensible novelty is (i) aggregating it to a **per-item-embedding reliability** and (ii) using it as the **ID-vs-text fusion weight** under the popularity-banded mission — NOT "gradient variance in optimization" in general. It is the **empirical complement** to XF-4 (assumed reliability) and XF-6 (frequency+text reliability): same fusion site, reliability *measured* instead of *assumed*.
- **Feasibility / risk:** No-op-OFF flag; reuses autograd/Adam gradient stats (near-zero added compute); ~7 min/VG run; emits `by_popularity`. **Non-collapsible** (measured statistic, no parameter to null). Risks, honestly: (a) **more invasive than a buffer** — needs a backward hook or optimizer-state read and a one-step-lagged, stop-gradient update to avoid a differentiable feedback loop (mechanically straightforward but the most code of any idea this batch); (b) the gradient SNR is a **moving target** early in training — warm it up for a few epochs with `w` frozen at the `n_i`-based value, then switch on, so the reliability is measured from a settled-ish state; (c) it may simply **track `n_i`** (if frequency *is* the dominant learnability driver in VG, the measured and assumed reliabilities coincide ⇒ near-neutral vs XF-6, which is itself an informative finding: "learnability in VG is frequency-bound, no hidden ambiguity signal"). Queue **below XF-6** (XF-6 is cleaner/cheaper and carries the primary mid-amplifier + falsification test; XF-7 is the measured-reliability upgrade to run *only if* XF-6's fusion shows a mid signal and you want to test whether a *better* reliability than `n_i` sharpens it). The realized per-item gradient-SNR distribution (which items are frequent-but-unlearnable vs rare-but-settled) is an interpretable readout that directly tests whether `n_i` is the right reliability proxy at all.

**Handoff to in-field (priority order): XF-6 (multisensory cue fusion) FIRST — it is the directive-(a) mid-amplifier the queue currently lacks (XF-4 grounded into a global denoiser, not a per-item mid lever), it is non-collapsible (data-derived two-sided weights), it adds the genuinely-new per-item TEXT-reliability signal, it carries a clean built-in superadditivity falsification test, and its PoIE math gives a SECOND independent (neuroscience) derivation of the tail<head<mid ordering for directive (c). XF-7 (fluctuation–dissipation) SECOND and only if XF-6 shows a mid signal — it swaps XF-6's assumed `n_i` reliability for a measured gradient-SNR learnability, testing whether a better reliability sharpens the mid gain; more code, prior-art-flagged, but the same non-collapsible fusion site.** Sources: [Computational principles and models of multisensory integration (PMC5447489)](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5447489/); [A Bayesian view on multimodal cue integration — Ernst (MPG)](https://pure.mpg.de/rest/items/item_1791241_5/component/file_3169376/content); [Principle of inverse effectiveness — statistical considerations (Springer)](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10548-009-0097-2); [Fluctuation–dissipation relations for SGD (Yaida 2018)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1810.00004).

### 2026-06-17 batch #4 — RETARGETED at SUPERVISOR FEEDBACK 2026-06-17 cycle 4. **The model-lever search is CLOSED** (CF1 cue-fusion DEAD on both datasets — the last sanctioned lever; every regularizer axis W1/X1/Y1/Z1/GD1 dead). The mission has resolved to a **DATASET-CONDITIONAL LAW**: text-augmentation's long-tail benefit is governed by the dataset's text-signal-vs-ID-signal ratio — VG (dense, generic titles) tail-NULL (5-seed, all 3 text components tail-negative), MI (sparse, distinctive titles) tail-WIN (5-seed, CI excludes 0, TAPE-driven), Beauty (3rd dataset) running. The supervisor's **explicit #1 live research target**: *"propose a concrete, measurable text-distinctiveness / ID-density statistic that PREDICTS where text helps the tail, so a 3rd-dataset test can confirm a LAW, not just 'two datasets disagree'."* The analyst's missing-ablation #2 is the same gap and flags that the campaign has **no measured text-distinctiveness statistic**, and that the crude density proxy (users/item) **fails to separate VG≈Beauty (3.70≈3.51) from each other** even though it separates MI (2.34) — so density alone cannot be the predictor; a text-distinctiveness axis is required. **These two ideas are read-only CPU MEASUREMENT/THEORY artifacts, NOT model levers** (a model flag would violate the closed-lever pivot) — exactly the form the supervisor + analyst asked for ("read-only, CPU, NO training"). They attach as a small standalone analysis script over the cached frozen `sbert_*.npy` matrices + train interaction sequences, not as a flag in `run_sasrec_sbert.py`. The first is the deep, decisive law-completion artifact; the second is a cheap embedding-free cross-check on the distinctiveness axis.

## XF 2026-06-17-8 — Quantitative genetics (variance-component "heritability" / GREML + Haseman–Elston) & ecology (partial Mantel): partition the NEXT-ITEM choice signal between a TEXT-kinship and an ID-kinship matrix → a per-tercile "text signal share" that PREDICTS the tail law  [status: GROUNDED→PROPOSAL 2026-06-17-4]
- **Domain & principle:** **Variance partitioning of a trait between relationship (kinship) matrices.** In quantitative genetics, **narrow-sense heritability** `h² = V_A/(V_A+V_E)` is the *share of a phenotype's (co)variance explained by the genetic relationship matrix* (GRM). **GREML / GCTA** (Yang et al. 2010) estimates it by REML in the linear mixed model `y = g + e`, `Cov(g)=σ_g²·A` (A = GRM), `Cov(e)=σ_e²I` — *the extent to which the relationship matrix `A` predicts phenotypic similarity is the variance it explains.* Its cheap, distribution-free regression form is **Haseman–Elston** (Haseman & Elston 1972; "HE-revisited"): regress the **mean-centred cross-product of a pair's phenotypes** `(y_i−ȳ)(y_j−ȳ)` on their relatedness `A_ij` — the slope estimates `σ_g²`. With *two* relationship matrices entered jointly, the two partial slopes partition the trait covariance between them. Ecology's **Mantel / partial-Mantel test** (Mantel 1967; Legendre) is the same object framed as "the correlation between two distance matrices, controlling for a third," with a permutation null. Across all three the question is identical and exactly the dataset-law question: ***how much of the observed pattern does THIS similarity matrix explain, controlling for THAT one?***
- **The analogy:** We have **two "kinship" matrices over the ~25k catalog items**: a **TEXT-kinship** `K_text` (frozen SBERT cosine — already cached) and a **collaborative/ID-kinship** `K_id` (item–item co-occurrence: cosine of co-purchase count vectors, or Jaccard of the user-sets, from TRAIN only). The **"phenotype" is the realized next-item choice signal** — for an item pair `(i,j)`, how much more often than chance they occur **adjacently / both in the next-item position** in user histories (an adjacency-PMI, or the shared-user-set overlap). Partition that choice-covariance between `K_text` and `K_id` → a per-dataset **"text signal share"** `s_text = V_text/(V_text+V_id)` (the recsys analogue of `h²`, with "text-kinship" playing the role of the GRM). **This IS the mechanism hypothesis made measurable:** the analyst's law says *text rescues the tail where text-signal exceeds ID-signal*; `s_text` **computed on the TAIL tercile** (pairs of rare items) is precisely "how much of rare items' co-choice is explained by text vs by their (near-absent) ID co-occurrence." Predicted ordering, directly testable: tail-`s_text` should be **LOW on VG** (text explains little of rare-item choice beyond ID; tail-null) and **HIGH on MI** (text explains rare-item choice where ID is starved; tail-win), and the measured per-dataset tail-`s_text` should **rank with the measured text−ID tail Δ** (VG −0.0002 < Beauty ? < MI +0.0003). That rank-match converts "two datasets disagree" into a **predictive law**.
- **Concrete model mechanism (read-only analysis here — NOT a training flag):** A standalone CPU script (≈80 lines, pure NumPy/PyTorch) over cached artifacts; **no GPU, no training, runs alongside any live job.** Per dataset (VG/MI/Beauty): (1) load the frozen `sbert_*.npy` table and the TRAIN interaction sequences; compute train item-frequency terciles (the same leak-free tail/mid/head as `by_popularity`). (2) **Sample M≈1e5 item pairs** (stratified: tail×tail, mid×mid, head×head, and all-pairs) — full 25k² is unnecessary, HE/Mantel are pair-sampling estimators. (3) For each pair build: `y_ij` = the **choice-co-occurrence phenotype** (adjacency-PMI from train sequences, i.e. `log[ p(i,j adjacent)/p(i)p(j) ]`, *or* Jaccard of the two items' train user-sets — report **both** for robustness); `x1 = cos(text_i,text_j)`; `x2 = ID-kinship(i,j)` (cosine of co-occurrence-count vectors / Jaccard). (4) **z-score** y,x1,x2; run the **joint Haseman–Elston OLS** `y ~ β_text·x1 + β_id·x2`; the standardized partial coefficients (equivalently partial-R²) give `s_text = R²_text /(R²_text+R²_id)`. (5) Emit per dataset `{s_text^overall, s_text^tail, s_text^mid, s_text^head}` + the partial-Mantel r (text vs choice, controlling ID) with a permutation p. (6) **Cross-dataset:** tabulate `(s_text^tail, users/item, text−ID tail Δ)` for VG/MI/Beauty and check the **rank-correlation** of `s_text^tail` with the tail Δ — the LAW. Cost: O(M·d), seconds/dataset on CPU. Hand the table to the analyst (read-only artifact, not a bus-owned file).
- **Why no CS person would default to this:** A recsys engineer measures "text quality" with retrieval metrics, or — as the analyst proposed for missing-ablation #2 — a **marginal** text-spread number (mean nearest-neighbour SBERT cosine, intrinsic dimensionality) computed on the text embeddings *in isolation*, **blind to whether that text geometry actually predicts the choices, and blind to the competing ID channel.** Partitioning a trait's covariance **between two relationship matrices** (the GREML "how much does this kinship explain" question, or partial-Mantel "controlling for the other matrix") is a quantitative-genetics / spatial-ecology instinct — you ask it when you have two candidate explanatory similarity structures and need their *relative, mutually-controlled* contributions. No representation-learning pipeline frames the dataset as "a phenotype with two competing kinship matrices."
- **Novelty vs our ledger:** The nearest queued idea is the analyst's missing-ablation #2 ("mean top-1 SBERT cosine / intrinsic dimensionality"). **Three decisive differences:** (1) it measures the text channel's **predictive share of the actual next-item signal**, not a text-only spread proxy that may not correlate with choice at all; (2) it **jointly controls for the ID channel**, so it isolates *text signal beyond ID* — the exact quantity the mechanism hypothesis names (text *complements* where ID is starved), which a marginal text statistic cannot see; (3) it is **per-tercile**, giving the tail-specific predictor the law needs. Distinct from BBP/XF-4 and GD1 (those analysed the **ID spectrum alone** — one channel, one matrix); this is a **two-matrix partition of the choice variance**, an axis no ledger artifact touches. It is also not a model lever (cannot be "voted off" — there is no parameter), sidestepping the W1/X1/Y1 failure mode entirely.
- **Feasibility / risk:** Read-only, CPU, no GPU, no training, **leak-free by construction** (TRAIN interactions + frozen text only; val/test targets never read) — it can run *now*, alongside the live Beauty GPU job, on all three datasets. **Honest risk #1 (the binding caveat): with only 3 datasets the cross-dataset law is a 3-point fit** — report it as a **rank/ordering check** (does `s_text^tail` rank VG < Beauty < MI in step with the tail Δ?), NOT an over-claimed regression slope; this is the same skeptical discipline the supervisor applied to the val=0.0765 and seed08 traps, now on the dataset axis. Each *additional* AR2023 category (Office/Tools/Arts) the experiment agent runs adds a point and hardens it — the statistic is the cheap part; breadth is what makes it a law. **Risk #2:** the choice-phenotype definition matters — hence report **both** adjacency-PMI and user-set-Jaccard and confirm `s_text` ranks the datasets the same way under both. **Risk #3 (cite):** raw Mantel inflates type-I error under spatial/embedding autocorrelation (Legendre 2010; Guillot & Rousset) — use the **regression/partial form and the permutation null**, and present `s_text` as a descriptive predictor with the caveat, not a significance claim. The realized per-tercile `s_text` per dataset is itself a **paper-worthy figure** (it *quantifies* the dataset-conditional law and the VG tail-irreducibility, complementing the BBP-ρ figure with the missing *text-side* axis).

## XF 2026-06-17-9 — Quantitative linguistics (Zipf / Heaps / type–token): a training-free, embedding-free text-distinctiveness scalar of the item-description corpus, paired with ID-density as the 2-axis dataset predictor  [status: PASS 2026-06-20 (in-field): SUPERSEDED + REFUTED. The text-distinctiveness predictor this proposes was already MEASURED and found FLAT (VG 0.823 ≈ MI 0.824 ⇒ does not discriminate the tail law); the companion s_text^tail predictor is REFUTED (inverted). Both are on the standing DEAD-predictor list (cycles 5–8). Cycle-8 CONVERGENCE forbids new paper-support proposals. Superseded, no remaining purchase.]
- **Domain & principle:** **Statistical laws of vocabulary.** **Heaps' law** `V(N) = k·N^β` — the number of distinct types `V` grows sublinearly with corpus size `N` (`β∈(0,1)`, typically ≈0.5); a **higher β / higher type–token ratio (TTR) / higher unigram entropy** ⇒ a *more lexically diverse, less repetitive, more distinctive* corpus, while a **steeper Zipf exponent** ⇒ a few tokens dominate ⇒ *more templated/generic* text. The exponents are tightly linked (Zipf-α correlates negatively, Heaps-β positively, with lexical diversity; recent gigaword analysis ties entropy, TTR, Zipf and Heaps analytically). These are decades-old, robust, **embedding-free** corpus statistics.
- **The analogy:** The "text-distinctiveness" axis the analyst says is **unmeasured** (and that the users/item density proxy provably *cannot* supply — it leaves VG≈Beauty indistinguishable) can be read **directly off the raw item-title/description strings**, before any embedding. A catalog of **distinctive** item descriptions (brand+model+variant — plausibly MI, Beauty) has high TTR / high Heaps-β / flatter Zipf; a catalog of **generic, templated** titles (plausibly VG) has low TTR / steep Zipf. Pairing this lexical-distinctiveness scalar with **users/item (ID-density)** gives the **2-D predictor `(text-distinctiveness, ID-density)`** the mechanism hypothesis literally names — and it can *separate VG from Beauty* (same density, predicted different distinctiveness) where density alone fails, making Beauty the clean adjudicator the analyst wants.
- **Concrete model mechanism (read-only analysis here — NOT a training flag):** A ≈30-line CPU script over the **raw description strings already in each dataset** (no embeddings needed): tokenize the concatenated item-description corpus and compute, per dataset, (a) **MATTR** (moving-average type–token ratio, window≈500 — the length-robust TTR that fixes plain-TTR's corpus-size sensitivity); (b) **Heaps-β** via OLS of `log V` on `log N` along the description stream; (c) **mean per-item description self-information / unigram entropy** (how surprising an item's tokens are catalog-wide — a TF-IDF-style per-item distinctiveness, averaged); (d) optionally **Zipf-α** by rank-frequency fit. Emit one small vector per dataset and **rank VG/MI/Beauty**; cross with users/item. Seconds on CPU; hand to the analyst as a read-only artifact.
- **Why no CS person would default to this:** Recsys treats descriptions as **opaque embedding inputs** — you feed the strings to SBERT and never look back. Running **Zipf/Heaps/TTR quantitative-linguistics laws on the catalog's description corpus to predict where downstream text *features* will pay off** is a corpus-linguistics measurement, not a representation-learning step; no recommender pipeline characterizes its item-text corpus by its vocabulary-growth exponent.
- **Novelty vs our ledger AND honest prior-art caveat:** Complements XF-8 cleanly — **XF-8 measures the text channel's *predictive share* of the choice signal** (needs embeddings + interactions, mutually controlled); **XF-9 is the cheaper, embedding-free, *upstream* distinctiveness scalar** measured from raw strings, as an independent cross-check that the distinctiveness axis (not just density) is what moves. **Honest caveat:** lexical-diversity metrics (Herdan/Heaps/Zipf/TTR, MATTR) are **textbook** — there is **zero novelty in the statistics themselves**; the only defensible contribution is **using them as the per-dataset predictor in the text-tail-benefit law**, and only *as corroboration of XF-8*, never as a standalone headline. No ledger artifact characterizes the description corpus at all, so it is new *to the campaign*, but flag the prior art prominently.
- **Feasibility / risk:** Trivial, CPU, no GPU, no training, leak-free (uses item descriptions only, no interactions/labels). **Risk #1:** plain TTR is length-sensitive across catalogs of different size — use **MATTR / Heaps-β** (size-robust) and report which. **Risk #2:** a single corpus scalar is a **weak 3-point predictor alone** — hence it is explicitly the *cheap cross-check on XF-8's predictive share and the second axis next to density*, not the predictor on its own. **Risk #3:** AR2023 titles can be short/noisy (HTML, boilerplate) — strip boilerplate and report MATTR on cleaned tokens. Its value: if `(distinctiveness, density)` **rank-separates** the three datasets in step with the tail Δ — and especially if it explains the VG-vs-Beauty split that density cannot — it is the embedding-free half of the law and a clean, cheap paper figure.

**Handoff to in-field (priority order): XF-8 (variance-partition / "text signal share") FIRST and decisive** — it is the supervisor's explicit #1 ask and the analyst's missing-ablation #2 made rigorous: a read-only, leak-free, CPU statistic that measures the **text channel's predictive share of the next-item signal, controlling for ID, per popularity tercile**, turning the dataset-conditional finding into a **predictive law** (tail-`s_text` should rank VG<Beauty<MI in step with the tail Δ). It is non-collapsible (no parameter), runs *now* alongside the live Beauty job, and yields a paper figure that quantifies the law and complements the BBP-ρ irreducibility figure with the missing text-side axis. **XF-9 (Zipf/Heaps/TTR distinctiveness) SECOND** — the cheap, embedding-free cross-check that supplies the text-distinctiveness axis density alone cannot (the VG-vs-Beauty discriminator), corroborating XF-8; prior-art-flagged, corroboration-only. Both are MEASUREMENT artifacts, not model levers — the correct deliverable now that the lever search is closed and the paper's centre of gravity is the dataset-conditional law. Sources: [Haseman–Elston regression (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haseman%E2%80%93Elston_regression); [SNP-heritability by GCTA-GREML](https://cloufield.github.io/GWASTutorial/14_gcta_greml/); [Mantel test (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mantel_test); [Dismantling the Mantel tests / autocorrelation caveat (Guillot–Rousset)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1112.0651); [Comparison of the Mantel test for spatial genetic data (Legendre 2010)](https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.1111/j.1755-0998.2010.02866.x); [Entropy and type–token ratio in gigaword corpora (Heaps/Zipf links)](https://arxiv.org/html/2411.10227v1); [Revisiting Zipf's law: a new indicator of lexical diversity](https://www.researchgate.net/publication/364727710_Revisiting_Zipf's_law_A_new_indicator_of_lexical_diversity).

### 2026-06-18 batch — RETARGETED at the SINGLE live ask. Per ANALYSIS_LOG -6/-7 + SUPERVISOR FEEDBACK cycle 4: the **model-lever search is CLOSED** (W1/X1/Y1/Z1/GD1/CF1 all dead — a model flag now violates the pivot), and BOTH prior text-side predictors are **REFUTED** (XF-8 `s_text^tail` and XF-9 text-distinctiveness do NOT rank the datasets with the tail Δ; the crude users/item density proxy fails to separate VG≈Beauty). The mission has resolved to a **dataset-conditional tail law governed by per-item ID-EXPOSURE, NOT any text-side property** (VG/Beauty dense ⇒ tail-null; MI sparse ⇒ tail-win, 5-seed CI excludes 0). The analyst's missing-ablation #2 and the supervisor's verbatim #1 research target are now identical and the ONLY live ask: *"sample-complexity / estimation-error theory for when a learned ID embedding beats a frozen content embedding as a function of per-item exposure — convert the empirical ordering into a **predicted threshold**."* Both ideas below answer exactly that. They are **read-only CPU MEASUREMENT/THEORY artifacts** (standalone scripts over cached `sbert_*.npy` + train sequences [+ existing multi-seed runs], NOT flags in `run_sasrec_sbert.py`), **non-collapsible** (no learnable scalar — the dead-on-arrival failure mode), and **leak-free** (train-only). The deep distinction from the already-mined XF-4/BBP: BBP gave a *hard detectability transition* (overlap=0 below a threshold) and treated **text as uniformly reliable**; these two instead give a **smooth bias–variance crossover that incorporates the text error as the crossing LEVEL**, yield a **measurable per-dataset crossover-exposure n\***, and — the load-bearing new contribution — **explain WHY the text-side predictors were refuted** (the bias term is dataset-invariant ⇒ the variance/exposure term is what governs). XF-1 is the decisive parametric centerpiece; XF-2 is its free, assumption-free empirical cross-check over existing seeds and a second non-CS anchor for the n^−1/2 law.

## XF 2026-06-18-1 — Psychometrics (Spearman–Brown reliability + IRT Fisher information): the bias–variance crossover exposure n\* = σ²/b² as a measurable per-dataset predictor that DERIVES the tail law and explains the refuted text predictors  [status: PASS 2026-06-20 (in-field): SUPERSEDED. The sample-complexity / crossover-exposure n* threshold theory this proposes is exactly the "predicted threshold" ask that has SINCE been grounded and executed — the controlled density-titration (PROPOSAL 2026-06-18-1) is the causal in-vivo realization, and its predicted curve-shape/threshold read-out is pre-registered in 2026-06-18-2 + 2026-06-19-3. The titration REFUTED a monotone tail threshold (MI-equiv rho=0.66 most-negative), which a static predictor cannot override. Cycle-8 CONVERGENCE forbids new paper-support proposals. Superseded by the live evidence.]
- **Domain & principle:** **Classical test theory and item response theory** — the century-old psychometric account of *how estimation reliability accrues with the number of observations*. The **Spearman–Brown prophecy formula** (Spearman 1910; Brown 1910): the reliability of a score built from `n` observations of a latent trait is `ρ_n = n·ρ₁ / (1 + (n−1)·ρ₁)`, where `ρ₁` (the single-observation reliability ≡ the intraclass correlation, ICC) is the ratio of between-target (signal) variance to total variance, `ρ₁ = τ²/(τ²+σ²)`. Reliability rises monotonically with `n` toward 1, and the *standard error of the latent-trait estimate* falls as `SE ∝ σ/√n`. **IRT** sharpens this: the **Fisher information** of an item parameter adds across responses, so its estimation error is `Var ∝ 1/(n·I)`, and a target reliability requires a *minimum number of observations* (IRT calibration literature: SEs recover only once `n` clears a model-dependent threshold; e.g. N≈500–2000 for stable item parameters). The load-bearing law: **a per-target estimate built by "averaging" `n` noisy observations has error that shrinks as `σ²/n` (unbiased, variance-limited), and reliability is a fixed function of `n` and the single-shot ICC.**
- **The analogy:** Each catalog item is a "test taker" whose **latent taste direction `θ_i`** is being estimated from exactly `n_i` interaction "responses." The **ID embedding `item_emb(ids)` is the IRT/classical estimate**: unbiased but variance-limited, `E‖ID_i − θ_i‖² ≈ σ²/n_i` (shrinks with exposure; reliability `ρ_ID(n_i)` follows Spearman–Brown). The **frozen-text projection `sbert_proj(s_i)` is a fixed-error, exposure-INVARIANT estimate**: `E‖text_i − θ_i‖² ≈ b²` (a *bias* — the irreducible gap between the SBERT content direction and the true taste direction — with ≈zero variance). The optimal channel for item `i` is simply whichever has lower error: **ID beats text ⟺ σ²/n_i < b² ⟺ n_i > n\* := σ²/b².** Therefore **text helps an item iff its exposure `n_i < n\*`**, and *text helps the TAIL tercile iff that tercile's exposure mass sits below `n\*`*. `n\*` is a **dataset property** (σ² = within-item interaction/taste noise; b² = the frozen-text approximation error to the collaborative signal). This reproduces the analyst's verdict from first principles AND **explains the two refutations the campaign could not otherwise account for:** the refuted predictors (`s_text`, text-distinctiveness) are all proxies for **b² (the text-side bias)** — and if SBERT's content-to-taste error `b²` is roughly **dataset-invariant** across Amazon categories (same encoder, similar title genre), then `n\* ≈ const` and the *only* variable that moves the crossover is the **tail exposure `n_tail`** ⇒ **ID-exposure governs, text-side properties do not** — exactly what the analyst measured. The frame turns "two text predictors mysteriously failed" into "the bias term is flat, so the variance term is the whole story."
- **Concrete mechanism (read-only analysis here — NOT a training flag):** a standalone CPU script (~100 lines NumPy) over cached artifacts; no GPU, no training, runs alongside any live job. Per dataset (VG/MI/Beauty[/future categories]):
  1. **Tail exposure distribution (the analyst's missing-ablation #2):** from TRAIN sequences compute item frequencies `n_i`, the leak-free tail/mid/head terciles, and per tercile: median `n_i`, and the fraction of items with `n_i < k` for `k∈{1,2,3,5,10}`.
  2. **Estimate the single-shot ICC `ρ₁` and σ²/τ²** by a one-way variance decomposition of a **train-only per-interaction target signal** grouped by item: for each item, treat its set of "next-item context" vectors (e.g. the SBERT vectors of the items it co-occurs with / transitions to in train) as repeated noisy observations of `θ_i`; ANOVA → between-item variance `τ²` vs within-item variance `σ²` ⇒ `ρ₁ = τ²/(τ²+σ²)`. (Pure measurement; no labels.)
  3. **Estimate the text bias `b²`** as the residual error of the frozen text in predicting the collaborative target: `b² = (1 − R²)` of regressing the train co-occurrence/transition structure on `cos`-similarity in frozen SBERT space (train-only, leak-free). Report it per dataset to *test* the invariance assumption directly.
  4. **Crossover `n\* = σ²/b²`** (and the Spearman–Brown `n` needed to reach a target reliability ρ*=0.7). Then the decisive cross-dataset check: tabulate `(n_tail, n\*, text−ID tail Δ)` for VG/MI/Beauty and test whether **`sign(n\* − n_tail)` and the rank of `n_tail/n\*` predict the rank of the tail Δ** (VG/Beauty: `n_tail > n\*` ⇒ tail-null; MI: `n_tail < n\*` ⇒ tail-win). A rank-match = the **predicted-threshold LAW** the supervisor asked for. Emit a per-dataset crossover figure (tail-exposure histogram with the `n\*` line) + the rank table. Hand to the analyst as a read-only artifact.
- **Why no CS person would default to this:** the ML cold-start reflex is the *qualitative* "content helps when interactions are scarce" → bolt on a content tower / meta-learn the cold items. Computing **each item's estimate reliability via classical test theory (Spearman–Brown ICC) and IRT Fisher information**, deriving the **explicit crossover exposure `n\*=σ²/b²`**, and then using *bias-invariance* to argue that **the variance term alone governs cross-dataset behaviour (hence text-distinctiveness had to fail)** is psychometric measurement + statistical decision theory, not representation learning. No recsys pipeline models items as test-takers with a per-item reliability and a prophecy-formula exposure threshold.
- **Novelty vs ledger:** Nearest = **XF-4/BBP** (mined) and the **refuted** XF-8/XF-9 text predictors. Distinctions: BBP gives a *hard* detectability transition and treats text as uniformly reliable; this gives a *smooth* bias–variance crossover whose crossing level **is** the text bias `b²`, yields a **measurable `n\*`**, and **explains the refutations** (b² flat ⇒ exposure governs) — a contribution none of them make. XF-8 measured text's *predictive share* (refuted as a predictor); this measures the **exposure threshold**, the analyst's now-live axis. It is read-only, non-collapsible, and directly produces the missing-ablation-#2 exposure statistic *plus* the theory that gives it a predicted threshold rather than a bare correlation.
- **Feasibility / risk:** read-only, CPU, seconds/dataset, leak-free, runs now. **HONEST PRIOR-ART CAVEAT (flag prominently):** the bias–variance view of cold-start (content = low-variance/biased, CF = unbiased/high-variance, content wins when data is scarce) is **textbook** ([cold-start overview](https://www.sciencedirect.com/topics/computer-science/cold-start-problem); [bias–variance tradeoff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bias%E2%80%93variance_tradeoff)), and Spearman–Brown/ICC/IRT are textbook psychometrics — **zero novelty in the ingredients.** The defensible, narrow novelty = (i) the **quantitative crossover `n\*` as a per-dataset predictor whose rank matches the measured tail Δ across categories**, (ii) **estimating `n\*` from cached frozen-text + train interactions via the ICC/Spearman–Brown machinery**, and (iii) the **diagnosis that bias-invariance is why the text-side predictors were refuted** — i.e., the law's *mechanism*, not "content helps cold items." Frame it as that or it is not publishable-novel. **Risk #1 (binding):** a 3-point cross-dataset fit — report as a **rank/ordering check** (same discipline applied to the val=0.0765 and seed08 traps), hardened by each extra AR2023 category the experiment agent adds (the statistic is cheap; breadth makes the law). **Risk #2:** σ²,b² depend on the chosen target-signal definition — report ≥2 definitions (SBERT-neighbour vs co-occurrence target) and confirm the dataset ranking is stable. **Risk #3:** if measured `b²` is in fact NOT dataset-invariant, that is itself the finding (the predictor becomes `n_tail·b²/σ²`, still exposure-dominated if b² varies little) — either way the artifact *measures* which term governs. The realized `(n_tail, n\*)` per dataset is a paper figure that quantifies the law and pairs with the BBP-ρ irreducibility figure on the variance side.

## XF 2026-06-18-2 — Metrology / frequency-stability (Allan / two-sample variance): empirically MEASURE the ID-error-vs-exposure curve and its bias floor from cross-seed embedding variance, locating n\* model-free  [status: PASS 2026-06-20 (in-field): SUPERSEDED, same basis as XF-18-1. The model-free empirical ID-error-vs-exposure / n* crossover artifact is the cross-check for a threshold theory the density-titration already tested in-vivo and REFUTED for the tail. Cycle-8 CONVERGENCE forbids new paper-support proposals; the Allan/cross-seed-variance framing, if useful, folds into the existing titration figure rather than a new block. Superseded.]
- **Domain & principle:** The **Allan variance** (Allan 1966), the standard metrology tool for *how a measurement's error scales with the amount of averaging, and where a fixed floor takes over.* On an Allan-deviation plot vs averaging time τ, error first falls as **τ^(−1/2)** (the white-noise / random-walk *averaging* region, slope −0.5: more data ⇒ less error), then **flattens at the "bias-instability floor"** (a 1/f drift the averaging cannot beat; slope 0). The **minimum of the curve is the optimal averaging count** beyond which collecting more data stops helping because the fixed floor dominates. Crucially, the two-sample (Allan) variance was invented because the *ordinary* variance **fails to converge** for these processes — it is precisely the right estimator when you ask "how does estimation error fall with sample count, and at what point does an irreducible floor cross it?" ([Allan variance (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allan_variance); [NIST Handbook of Frequency Stability Analysis](https://tf.nist.gov/general/pdf/2220.pdf)).
- **The analogy:** An item's learned ID embedding is an estimator that effectively **averages over its `n_i` interactions**, so its estimation error should fall as **`n_i^(−1/2)`** (the white-noise averaging region) — until it would hit a floor. The **frozen-text channel is a separate estimator pinned at a fixed error `b`** (the metrological *bias-instability floor*, exposure-invariant). The exposure where the falling ID-error curve **crosses the text floor is exactly `n\*`** — but now obtained **empirically, without assuming the parametric `σ²/n` form of XF-1.** This gives the same crossover from a *measured* curve, and a second, independent non-CS derivation of the **`error ∝ n^(−1/2)`** exposure law.
- **Concrete mechanism (read-only analysis here — NOT a training flag; uses EXISTING multi-seed runs):** the campaign already has **5–6 independent seeds** of the V2 stack (and ID-only seeds) per dataset — i.e. *independent measurement realizations*, the exact ingredient the two-sample variance needs. A standalone CPU script:
  1. For each item `i`, compute the **cross-seed variance of its learned ID embedding** `Var_seed[item_emb_i]` (after Procrustes/centering alignment across seeds to remove global rotation/scale gauge) — a direct empirical estimate of that item's ID-estimation noise `σ²_ID(n_i)`, the embedding-table analogue of an Allan two-sample variance over independent training runs.
  2. **Bin by exposure `n_i`** and plot mean cross-seed ID variance vs `n_i` on log–log axes; **fit the slope** — a slope ≈ **−1 in variance (−0.5 in deviation)** confirms the white-noise/averaging law (exposure governs the ID error), and any **flattening** locates an ID-side floor.
  3. **Locate `n\*_emp`** = the exposure bin where the falling cross-seed ID variance equals the **ID-vs-text representation gap** (the squared distance between the frozen-text projection and the seed-mean ID vector, the "floor"). Compare `n\*_emp` across VG/MI/Beauty and check it **ranks with the tail Δ** and **agrees with XF-1's parametric `n\*`** (two independent estimates of the same threshold = a strong, falsifiable cross-validation).
- **Why no CS person would default to this:** ML measures embedding "stability" with cosine drift, CKA, or probing accuracy; treating the **cross-seed embedding spread as a metrological two-sample (Allan) variance** and reading the **exposure crossover off an averaging-vs-floor curve** is frequency-stability/metrology, not representation learning. Using *random seeds as independent clocks* to recover a per-item `n^(−1/2)` estimation-noise law and its bias floor is a non-default move.
- **Novelty vs ledger:** No ledger artifact uses **cross-seed variance for anything** — it is a *free* new measurement over runs already on disk, and a structurally different (empirical, non-parametric) route to `n\*` than XF-1's closed form. Complements XF-1: parametric prediction (XF-1) vs measured curve (XF-2); agreement between them hardens the law. **Prior-art caveat (flag):** Allan variance is textbook metrology and cross-seed/ensemble variance is standard ML uncertainty — the narrow novelty is **using it as a per-item, exposure-keyed estimation-noise curve to locate the ID-vs-text crossover**, not "ensemble variance" in general.
- **Feasibility / risk:** read-only, CPU, cheap. **Risk #1 (the binding dependency):** it needs the **per-seed trained `item_emb` tables on disk** — if only `results_*.json` (metrics) were saved and not the embedding checkpoints, this arm cannot run as-is. Mitigation: (a) check the run dir for saved `state_dict`/`*.pt`/`*.npy` embedding dumps; (b) if absent, this degrades to a **standing recommendation** that the experiment agent dump `item_emb.weight` (one `.npy`, megabytes, *no extra training*) on the next batch of seed runs — then the artifact runs for free; (c) even with no checkpoints, the **Allan-variance framing still gives XF-1 its model-free validation path and a second non-CS anchor for the `n^(−1/2)` slope law.** **Risk #2:** seeds must be aligned (Procrustes) before cross-seed variance is meaningful — straightforward but must be done. **Risk #3:** few seeds (5–6) ⇒ noisy per-item variance — bin by exposure (hundreds–thousands of items/bin) so the *binned* curve is stable even if per-item estimates are not. Queue **below XF-1** (XF-1 is the decisive, dependency-free centerpiece; XF-2 is the free empirical cross-check that only needs existing seeds).

**Handoff to in-field (priority order): XF-1 (psychometric crossover `n\*=σ²/b²`) FIRST and decisive** — it is the supervisor's + analyst's verbatim #1 ask (estimation-error theory → a *predicted threshold* on exposure), it is a read-only/leak-free/non-collapsible CPU artifact that also *emits the missing-ablation-#2 tail-exposure statistic*, and its key contribution is the **mechanism that DERIVES the dataset-conditional law and EXPLAINS why both text-side predictors were refuted** (bias-invariant ⇒ exposure governs) — a publishable advance over a bare correlation, paired with the BBP-ρ figure on the variance side. **XF-2 (Allan/cross-seed variance) SECOND** — the free, assumption-free empirical cross-check that *measures* the same `n\*` from existing multi-seed runs and a second non-CS anchor for the `n^(−1/2)` law; gated only by whether per-seed embeddings are on disk (if not, a one-line `.npy` dump on the next seed batch unlocks it). Both are MEASUREMENT/THEORY artifacts, not model levers — correct now that the lever search is closed and the paper's centre of gravity is the exposure-governed dataset-conditional law. Sources: [Spearman–Brown prediction formula (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spearman%E2%80%93Brown_prediction_formula); [Information Matrices and Standard Errors for MLEs of Item Parameters in IRT (Psychometrika)](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s11336-013-9334-4); [Sample Size Requirements for Item Parameter Estimation (PMC)](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC4746434/); [bias–variance tradeoff (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bias%E2%80%93variance_tradeoff); [cold-start problem overview (ScienceDirect)](https://www.sciencedirect.com/topics/computer-science/cold-start-problem); [Allan variance (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allan_variance); [NIST Handbook of Frequency Stability Analysis (Riley)](https://tf.nist.gov/general/pdf/2220.pdf).

### 2026-06-18 batch #2 — THE UNEXPLAINED PUZZLE the existing threshold artifacts cannot resolve. STEP-1 read confirms: lever search CLOSED; the live cross-field ask (SUPERVISOR cycle-5 RESEARCHERS #3) is "continue the estimation-error / sample-complexity theory for the global-sparsity threshold." XF-18-1 (Spearman–Brown `n*=σ²/b²`) and XF-18-2 (Allan `n*`) already nail the **per-item** threshold — but BOTH are **independent single-item averaging** arguments, and the supervisor's own decisive measurement **breaks** them: **tail items have IDENTICAL local exposure across datasets** (5-core floor, median n_i = 4/4/5 on VG and MI alike — "tail-LOCAL exposure FLAT"), yet text rescues MI's tail and NOT VG's, and the governing variable is **GLOBAL** catalog density (interactions/item MI 16.2 ≪ VG 24.5 ≈ Beauty 24.9; users/item MI 2.34 ≪ VG 3.70 ≈ Beauty 3.51). A per-item `n*=σ²/b²` theory predicts NO difference at fixed local n_i — so it cannot, on its own, explain why a *catalog-average* statistic decides a *tail item's* fate. The missing layer is **collective**: in a coupled low-rank inference problem the recovery of ANY single item's embedding depends on the GLOBAL signal density, because all items are estimated jointly in one shared factorization/softmax. This is a textbook **phase-transition** phenomenon in statistical physics, and it is the one framing the ledger's per-item (XF-18-1/2) and single-spike-spectral (XF-4/BBP) artifacts structurally miss. The idea below is a single deep THEORY/MEASUREMENT artifact (read-only CPU, non-collapsible, leak-free) that supplies the *collective* threshold and — using the **already-measured effective rank (22–24)** — yields a falsifiable per-dataset prediction that separates MI from VG/Beauty and predicts Beauty-null.

## XF 2026-06-18-3 — Statistical physics of inference (replica/cavity theory of low-rank matrix estimation + the SBM Kesten–Stigum detectability threshold): a COLLECTIVE recovery phase transition in GLOBAL density that explains why catalog-average ID-density, not local tail exposure, governs the tail law  [status: PASS 2026-06-20 (in-field): STRONGEST remaining NEW idea, evaluated per STEP 2(i) — and the most relevant to the OPEN MI-tail mechanism. The replica/cavity COLLECTIVE low-rank-recovery + SBM Kesten–Stigum detectability-transition framing correctly motivates why GLOBAL (catalog-average) density, not tail-local exposure, would govern a tail law — exactly the mechanism the titration set out to test. BUT it is foreclosed as a new experiment AND partly REFUTED by results that postdate it: (i) the interaction-mode density-titration that would confirm a collective transition is CLOSED and REFUTED for the tail (no monotone crossing across 7 rungs; MI-equivalent rho=0.66 is the most-negative thinned rung — the opposite of a recovery transition toward MI's tail win); (ii) its theory was already absorbed into the GROUNDED curve-shape proposal (2026-06-18-2) and the rigidity addendum (2026-06-19-2, which found the tail 100%-floppy/saturated in all datasets ⇒ a collective transition does not cleanly discriminate there); (iii) the one remaining sanctioned run is the user-mode titration (2026-06-19-1), already queued with its data-collapse / detectability-style read-out pre-registered. Per cycle-8 CONVERGENCE (LAST experiment fixed; no new experiments or paper-support proposals), the genuine theory here belongs FOLDED into the titration figure caption — which cross-field already delivered (XF 2026-06-20-1, FOLD). Foreclosed + partly refuted; not spun into a new block.]
- **Domain & principle:** Two linked results from the **statistical mechanics of inference** (mean-field spin-glass / cavity & replica methods applied to estimation). **(A) Constrained low-rank matrix estimation** (Lesieur–Krzakala–Zdeborová 2017; Barbier–Dia–Macris–Krzakala–Lesieur–Zdeborová replica-formula proof; Lelarge–Miolane): recovering a rank-`r` signal from a noisy/partially-observed `n×n` matrix is a planted spin model whose Bayes-optimal MMSE undergoes a **sharp phase transition in a single global signal-to-noise control parameter** `λ` (∝ observations-per-parameter / inverse noise variance). **Below a critical `λ_c` the optimal estimator recovers NOTHING — the MMSE stays pinned at the prior (the overlap with the truth is 0), no matter how the data is distributed among rows;** above it, recovery sets in (often discontinuously — the "hard"/spinodal phase, and in some priors an "all-or-nothing" jump). For the symmetric spiked model the spectral (BBP) threshold sits at `λ=1`; structured priors open an information-computation gap below it. The decisive qualitative fact: **recovery is a property of the GLOBAL `λ`, not of any single row's degree.** **(B) The stochastic block model detectability / Kesten–Stigum threshold** (Decelle–Krzakala–Moore–Zdeborová 2011; Mossel–Neeman–Sly): in a sparse graph with **mean degree `d`** and signal strength `λ`, latent community structure is **detectable by ANY algorithm iff `d·λ² > 1`**; below the KS line the structure is information-theoretically/computationally invisible — *again a transition in the GLOBAL mean degree, and again an individual node cannot be classified no matter its own local edges if the catalog-wide `d` is subcritical.*
- **The analogy:** Our ID-embedding learning IS a coupled low-rank estimation: the model factorizes the (sparse, observed) user×item interaction structure into `d=64`-dim item vectors, all estimated **jointly** through the shared next-item softmax. Map (A): the global control parameter is the **observations-per-parameter ratio** `α ≈ (interactions per item) / d_eff`, where `d_eff` is the **effective embedding rank the analyst already measured = 22–24** (GD/BBP figure), NOT the nominal 64. Then `α_MI ≈ 16.2/23 ≈ 0.70`, `α_VG ≈ 24.5/23 ≈ 1.07`, `α_Beauty ≈ 24.9/23 ≈ 1.08`. If the recovery threshold `α_c ≈ 1` (the BBP/spectral scale), **MI sits BELOW it — the whole MI item-embedding system is in the poor-/non-recovery phase, so the always-observed frozen-text channel complements everywhere, including the tail (MI tail-WIN)** — while **VG and Beauty sit ABOVE it — ID embeddings recover, text is marginal, tail-null.** Map (B): the bipartite user–item graph has **mean item-degree = users/item** (MI 2.34 ≪ VG 3.70 ≈ Beauty 3.51); the KS line `d·λ²>1` says collaborative (community) signal for an item is **detectable only above a global mean-degree threshold** — MI's far-lower mean degree puts it nearest/below the detectability line, so ID structure is collectively weakest exactly where text wins. **This is the mechanism the per-item theories lack:** it explains why a *catalog-average* density (interactions/item AND users/item — the two statistics the supervisor found rank with the tail Δ) governs a *tail item's* fate **even though every dataset's tail item has the same local 5-core exposure** — because in a coupled estimation problem recovery is a collective phase, set by the global `α`/`d`, not by the lone item's count. It also predicts the analyst's refutations from a different angle than XF-18-1: text-side properties (`s_text`, distinctiveness) don't move because the transition is driven by the **ID-side global SNR**, not by anything about the text.
- **Concrete mechanism (read-only analysis here — NOT a training flag; the closed-lever-compliant deliverable):** a standalone CPU script (~120 lines NumPy/scipy.sparse) over cached train interactions + the already-saved spectra; **no GPU, no training, runs alongside any live job.** Per dataset (VG/MI/Beauty[/future categories]):
  1. **Global SNR `α`:** compute interactions/item and `d_eff` = the effective rank of the trained ID-embedding Gram matrix (participation ratio `(Σσ_i)²/Σσ_i²` of the singular values, or the GD effective-rank already on disk — reuse, don't recompute if available). Report `α = (interactions/item)/d_eff` per dataset.
  2. **KS quantity:** build the train-only bipartite user–item graph, compute the **mean item-degree `d` (=users/item)** and an estimate of the per-item signal `λ` (e.g. from the leading-vs-bulk gap of the **non-backtracking** or co-occurrence operator — the standard SBM-detectability estimator); report `d·λ²` per dataset and its position vs the KS line `=1`.
  3. **Locate the transition empirically as a cross-check (no new training needed):** the campaign already has **trained ID-only embeddings per dataset**; measure the realized **signal overlap** (alignment of the leading ID-embedding subspace with the co-occurrence/transition structure) and confirm it is **low on MI, high on VG/Beauty** — i.e. that MI is observably in the weak-recovery phase. Optionally run a tiny **AMP / Low-RAMP** (or just power-iteration) on the actual sparse interaction matrix at each dataset's `α` to see where the order parameter jumps (a few seconds, CPU).
  4. **The LAW (decisive cross-dataset check):** tabulate `(α, d·λ², text−ID tail Δ)` for VG/MI/Beauty and test whether **`sign(α − α_c)` / `sign(d·λ² − 1)` predicts the rank of the tail Δ** (MI subcritical ⇒ tail-win; VG/Beauty supercritical ⇒ tail-null). Emit a phase-diagram figure (datasets plotted in the `(α, d·λ²)` plane with the critical lines). Hand to the analyst as a read-only artifact.
- **Why no CS person would default to this:** the recsys/ML reflex for "ID embeddings are bad here" is empirical — try more epochs, more dims, a content tower — never "the item-embedding system is a planted spin model undergoing a **collective recovery phase transition** in a global order parameter, and a single tail item is unrecoverable below `α_c`/`d·λ²=1` *regardless of its own interaction count*." Treating the embedding table as a thermalized many-body inference system whose **per-row recoverability is set by a catalog-wide control parameter** (cavity/replica thinking) is statistical-physics-of-inference, not representation learning. The KS-detectability framing — borrowed from community detection on sparse graphs — is likewise a physics/probability instinct no recommender pipeline invokes to explain a *long-tail* result.
- **Novelty vs ledger:** Nearest are **XF-4/BBP** (mined) and **XF-18-1 / XF-18-2** (per-item `n*`) and **XF-17-5** (percolation). Decisive distinctions: (1) **vs BBP** — BBP is the *single-spike, spectral, linear* detectability of ONE planted direction and treats the rest as noise; this is the *full coupled Bayes-optimal recovery transition* of the WHOLE rank-`d_eff` system, where the control parameter is the **global** observations-per-parameter `α` (BBP is the special spectral point `λ=1` inside this richer phase diagram, which can have an info-computation/hard phase below it). (2) **vs XF-18-1/18-2** — those are **per-item, independent-averaging** thresholds in the *local* `n_i`; they cannot explain the supervisor's central puzzle (global density governs at FIXED local tail exposure). This idea's load-bearing new content is exactly that: a **collective** threshold in the *catalog-average* density that resolves "why global, not local." (3) **vs XF-17-5 percolation** — connectivity (giant component, `⟨k⟩>1`) is *necessary but strictly weaker*; the KS line `d·λ²>1` is the **detectability** threshold (you can be well above percolation yet below KS — connected but with no recoverable signal). This upgrades XF-17-5's "is the tail connected?" to "is the catalog above the **detectability** transition?", a quantitative, falsifiable global statistic. No ledger artifact computes a global recovery/detectability control parameter or places the datasets on a phase diagram.
- **Feasibility / risk:** read-only, CPU, seconds–minutes/dataset, leak-free (train interactions + already-trained embeddings only; no val/test, no labels), **non-collapsible** (no learnable parameter — a measurement/theory artifact), runs **now** alongside the live Beauty job. **HONEST PRIOR-ART CAVEAT (flag prominently):** the statistical-physics phase transitions for low-rank estimation (Lesieur–Krzakala–Zdeborová; Barbier et al.) and SBM detectability (Decelle et al.; Kesten–Stigum) are **established physics/probability — zero novelty in the theorems themselves**, and matrix-completion sample complexity is textbook CS. The defensible, narrow novelty = (i) **mapping the recsys ID-embedding-learning to the coupled low-rank/SBM control parameters** (`α = interactions/(item·d_eff)` using the *measured* `d_eff`=22–24; `d·λ²` from users/item), (ii) using the **collective** transition to explain the supervisor's exact unexplained finding — **GLOBAL density governs at FIXED local tail exposure** (which per-item `n*` and single-spike BBP cannot), and (iii) the resulting **falsifiable phase-diagram prediction that separates MI (subcritical) from VG/Beauty (supercritical) and predicts Beauty-null**. Frame it as that or it is not publishable-novel. **Risk #1 (binding, same as all dataset-law artifacts):** a 3-point cross-dataset fit — report as a **rank/ordering + phase-side check** (MI below the line, VG/Beauty above), NOT a fitted `α_c`; each extra AR2023 category the experiment agent adds hardens it (and a SMALL-CATALOG sparse 4th point — the in-field's other ask — would be the ideal adjudicator: a *second* subcritical dataset predicted tail-WIN). **Risk #2:** `α_c` and `λ` are prior/model-dependent — do NOT over-claim `α_c≈1` as exact; the robust, honest statement is the **ordering** (MI has the lowest `α` and lowest `d·λ²` of the three, consistent with being on the recovery-poor side) plus the **empirical overlap cross-check** (step 3) that *directly* shows MI's ID subspace is the least aligned with its collaborative structure — that measurement does not depend on knowing `α_c`. **Risk #3:** `d_eff` and the overlap need the trained ID embeddings on disk (same dependency XF-18-2 flagged); if only metrics JSONs were saved, steps 1/3 degrade to using the published GD effective-rank (22–24) and a one-line `item_emb.weight` `.npy` dump on the next seed batch unlocks the rest — no extra training. **This artifact's payoff:** it is the **collective-mechanism capstone** of the dataset-law section — it converts "global ID-density correlates with the tail Δ" into "the catalog sits on a measurable side of a recovery/detectability **phase transition**," pairing the BBP-ρ (single-item variance) figure and the XF-18-1 (per-item bias–variance) figure with the missing **system-level** explanation for why a catalog-average number, not a per-item count, is what governs.

**Handoff to in-field (priority order): XF-18-3 (collective recovery phase transition) is the one idea this cycle, and it is the capstone — not a re-skin of XF-18-1/XF-4.** It answers the supervisor's exact unexplained puzzle (why GLOBAL catalog density governs the tail at FIXED local 5-core exposure — which the per-item `n*` theories of XF-18-1/18-2 structurally cannot) with a *collective* threshold from the statistical physics of coupled low-rank inference + SBM detectability, computes a falsifiable global control parameter `α = (interactions/item)/d_eff` using the **already-measured** effective rank 22–24 (separating MI≈0.70 below from VG≈1.07/Beauty≈1.08 above, predicting Beauty-null), and ships an empirical overlap cross-check (step 3) that does **not** depend on knowing `α_c`. Read-only/CPU/leak-free/non-collapsible — the correct deliverable now the lever search is closed and the paper centres on the exposure-/density-governed dataset-conditional law. It complements (does not replace) XF-18-1 (per-item bias–variance crossover) and XF-4/BBP (single-item spectral variance): three nested scales — single-item (BBP), per-item independent (XF-18-1/2), and catalog-collective (this) — with this one carrying the explanation for the GLOBAL-density governance the other two miss. Sources: [Constrained low-rank matrix estimation: phase transitions, AMP and applications (Lesieur–Krzakala–Zdeborová, IOPscience)](https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1742-5468/aa7284); [arXiv:1701.00858](https://arxiv.org/abs/1701.00858); [Fundamental limits of symmetric low-rank matrix estimation (Lelarge–Miolane, PMLR)](http://proceedings.mlr.press/v65/lelarge17a/lelarge17a.pdf); [All-or-nothing phase transitions in low-rank estimation (NSF PAR)](https://par.nsf.gov/servlets/purl/10249046); [Community detection and stochastic block models (Abbe, JMLR)](https://jmlr.org/papers/volume18/16-480/16-480.pdf); [Reconstruction/estimation in the planted partition model — KS threshold (Mossel–Neeman–Sly)](https://www.researchgate.net/publication/271105705_Reconstruction_and_estimation_in_the_planted_partition_model); [Spiked tensor estimation phase transitions (arXiv:1701.08010)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1701.08010).
