### 2026-06-18 batch #3 — FROM "derive the threshold" TO "MEASURE it causally." STEP-1 read confirms: lever search CLOSED (W1/X1/Y1/Z1/GD1/CF1 all dead); SUPERVISOR cycle-5 RESEARCHERS-#3 ask = "continue the estimation-error / sample-complexity theory for the global-sparsity threshold." The three threshold artifacts already on the bus this cycle — XF-18-1 (per-item Spearman–Brown crossover `n*=σ²/b²`), XF-18-2 (Allan empirical `n*`), XF-18-3 (collective recovery phase transition in global `α`) — are all **OBSERVATIONAL**: each *derives or measures* a threshold and then checks whether the **3 natural datasets** (VG/MI/Beauty) rank-match. That is the campaign's deepest credibility hole: **the dataset-conditional law rests on N=3 points (Beauty compute-blocked), and no amount of theory upgrades a 3-point correlation to causation.** A skeptical reviewer's first objection is the obvious confound — MI differs from VG in a hundred ways besides density (category, vocabulary, brand structure, review style), so "MI wins the tail because it is globally ID-sparse" is *one* reading of a 3-point coincidence. The missing move is the one every empirical science makes when an observational correlation needs to become a law: **stop comparing whole organisms and instead MANIPULATE the single suspected causal variable, holding everything else fixed, and trace the response to locate the threshold.** That is a *controlled experiment*, not another derivation — and it is the one thing none of XF-18-1/2/3 supplies. The idea below imports the exact methodology three non-CS fields use for precisely this situation (compare-at-unequal-effort → vary-the-dose → locate-the-critical-point), turning the global-density law into a **within-dataset dose–response curve with a predicted, measurable crossing density**.

## XF 2026-06-18-4 — Community ecology (rarefaction) + pharmacology (dose–response / Hill EC50) + condensed-matter (finite-size scaling): a CONTROLLED density-titration that causally locates the tail-law critical density on ONE dataset, instead of correlating 3 natural ones  [status: GROUNDED→PROPOSAL 2026-06-18-1]
- **Domain & principle:** Three convergent "vary the sampling effort / dose, trace the response, locate the threshold" methodologies, each the standard tool in its field for exactly the situation the campaign is in (an observational comparison across systems sampled at *unequal effort*). **(A) Rarefaction (community ecology; Sanders 1968; Hurlbert 1971; Gotelli–Colwell 2001):** you may **not** compare species richness/diversity across communities sampled with *unequal effort* — the deeper-sampled community spuriously looks richer. The fix is to **rarefy**: randomly subsample the more-intensively-sampled community **down to a common depth** and re-compare (or trace the whole expected-richness-vs-effort *rarefaction curve*). Differences that survive rarefaction are real ecology; differences that vanish were sampling-effort artifacts. **(B) Dose–response / the Hill equation (pharmacology; Hill 1910; median-effect, Chou–Talalay):** to establish that an agent *causes* an effect and to quantify *how much*, you **titrate the dose across a ladder** and fit a sigmoid `E(d)=E_min+(E_max−E_min)·dⁿ/(dⁿ+EC50ⁿ)`; the **EC50 / threshold dose** (the inflection / first dose above control) is the causal potency summary, read off the fitted curve with a CI. **(C) Finite-size scaling (condensed matter; Fisher–Barber 1972):** to locate a phase transition's critical control-parameter `α_c` from *finite* systems, you **sweep the control parameter across several system sizes**, find the **crossing point** of a scaled observable, and confirm **data-collapse** (curves for all sizes fall onto one universal scaling function when plotted vs `(α−α_c)·L^{1/ν}`) — collapse is the signature that a single threshold governs all the systems. **Common load-bearing law:** when systems differ in *sampling depth / a control parameter*, you do not compare them as-is — you **vary that one variable on a controlled ladder, trace the response, and locate the threshold**; a correlation across whole systems is replaced by a dose–response curve with a measured critical point.
- **The analogy:** The campaign is comparing VG/MI/Beauty at **unequal global sampling depth** — interactions/item 24.5 / 16.2 / 24.9, users/item 3.70 / 2.34 / 3.51 (the supervisor's own governing statistics) — and reading a *tail-Δ ordering* off that comparison. **This is precisely the ecology error rarefaction exists to correct: MI is the "shallowly-sampled community," and "text rescues MI's tail" might be (the null) a sampling-depth artifact, or (the claim) a real density-driven law — an as-is 3-point comparison cannot tell them apart.** Rarefaction's prescription, transplanted: take the DENSE, confirmed-tail-NULL dataset (VG), **hold the catalog, the model (V2 stack), the text/ID contrast, the text encoder, the eval users/targets, and the eval protocol ALL fixed**, and **thin only the TRAINING interactions** down a ladder of fractions ρ so the *global* density `α=(interactions/item)/d_eff` slides from VG's supercritical ≈1.07 down through MI's ≈0.70 and below. Pharmacology's prescription: at each rung measure the **text−ID tail Δ** (the "effect") and fit it vs density (the "dose"). Physics' prescription: repeat the ladder on a second dense dataset and check the crossing density is the **same** (universality / data-collapse). **The pre-registered prediction, derived from XF-18-3 + the matrix-completion sample-complexity transition `p* ∝ r·d·log d` (confirmed sharp in the literature): the tail Δ is ≈0/negative at full VG density and rises MONOTONICALLY, CROSSING ZERO into positive as density drops below the critical `α_c≈1`** — and the crossing density should land near where MI naturally sits. If VG, thinned to MI's depth, **flips to a tail-WIN**, then "global ID-density causes the tail law" is demonstrated *causally, on one dataset, with category/encoder/protocol held constant* — the confound is killed and the 3 natural points (VG/MI/Beauty) become 3 dots that should lie **on the titration curve** at their own densities. That is the difference between "two datasets disagree" and "a measured dose–response with a critical point the theory predicted."
- **Concrete mechanism (a controlled DATA-ablation EXPERIMENT for in-field to ground + EXPERIMENT to run — NOT a model lever; NOT something I run):** a single **data-loader knob**, not an architecture/loss change: `--subsample-train-frac ρ --subsample-mode {interaction,user} --subsample-seed S` (at `ρ=1.0` ⇒ bit-identical to the current loader ⇒ zero-cost no-op). It thins the TRAIN split only; eval is untouched. Protocol:
  1. **Density ladder on VG** (the fast dataset — VG eval is ~1/62 Beauty's cost, and each thinned run trains on LESS data so is *cheaper* than full VG): ρ ∈ {1.0, 0.75, 0.55, 0.40, 0.30, 0.22}, chosen so interactions/item sweeps 24.5 → ~5, passing MI's 16.2 around ρ≈0.66 and crossing the predicted `α_c≈1` (≈23 interactions/item at d_eff≈23) around ρ≈0.94 — i.e. the ladder brackets the transition.
  2. **Two subsampling modes, report both** (they move different control parameters → a built-in robustness check): **(i) interaction-thinning** (drop each train interaction w.p. 1−ρ) lowers interactions/item AND users/item together = the ecology "rarefy to common depth" move; **(ii) user-thinning** (drop whole users) lowers users/item (the KS mean-degree axis of XF-18-3) more cleanly. If the tail-Δ crossing density agrees across both modes, the result is robust to the thinning scheme.
  3. **Critical CONTROLS (this is what makes it causal, not just "less data → worse"):** keep the **catalog FIXED** (do NOT re-5-core after thinning — same item set throughout); define the **tail/mid/head terciles ONCE from the FULL-density train** so buckets are identical across the ladder; keep the **eval user set + their input histories + targets FIXED at every rung** (thin only *other* users' / *non-eval-history* training interactions where possible, or at minimum hold the eval users' conditioning histories constant) so the manipulated variable is purely *global training density*, not eval-history length. Leak-free by construction (eval targets never enter training at any ρ; terciles are train-only).
  4. **At each rung, train BOTH arms (text V2, ID-only), ≥2 seeds**, record tail/mid/head NDCG+HR and the **text−ID tail Δ**. Hand the (ρ, α, tail Δ) table + the per-rung eval-history-length distribution (the confound check) to the analyst.
  5. **Analyst-side (read-only, the non-CS estimators):** fit a **Hill/log-logistic sigmoid** to (α, tail Δ) and report the **crossing density α₀** ("the EC50 of the tail-sign flip") with a bootstrap CI; overlay the 3 natural datasets (VG/MI/Beauty) as points at their measured α and test whether they **fall on the titration curve**; if a 2nd dense dataset's ladder is run, attempt the **finite-size data-collapse** (plot tail Δ vs α/α₀ for both → one curve = universality of the threshold).
- **Why no CS person would default to this:** the recsys/ML reflex with "MI behaves differently from VG" is to attribute it to *category/domain* and move on, or — if quantitative at all — to run a one-directional "more data → higher NDCG" **scaling-law** ablation (monotone, no threshold, no sign-flip, no causal contrast against a held-fixed baseline). **Nobody in recsys frames the cross-dataset comparison as a RAREFACTION problem** (you are comparing communities at unequal sampling depth — rarefy before you compare), **nor titrates training density to locate the EC50 of a *cold-start sign reversal***, **nor invokes finite-size data-collapse to claim the threshold is universal across datasets.** The entire apparatus — rarefy-to-common-depth, dose–response with a critical dose, data-collapse across system sizes — is imported wholesale from ecology / pharmacology / condensed-matter; an ML practitioner reaches for "train on x% of data and plot the learning curve," which is the monotone scaling curve, not a controlled threshold-titration of a *differential* (text−ID) effect.
- **Novelty vs our ledger:** The ledger's threshold work is **all observational**: XF-4/BBP (single-spike variance), XF-18-1/18-2 (per-item bias–variance `n*`), XF-18-3 (collective `α` transition) each *derive/measure* a threshold and then **passively correlate it against the 3 natural datasets**. **This is the first artifact that MANIPULATES the governing variable to TEST the law causally** — it converts XF-18-3's *predicted* `α_c` from a number checked against 3 dots into a *measured crossing density* with a CI, traced within one dataset where every confounder (category, encoder, eval protocol, catalog) is held constant. It is the **experimental complement** the three derivations structurally cannot be (they have no manipulation). It also directly executes, in a *controlled* form, the supervisor's own two asks — "a SMALL-CATALOG sparse 4th data point" and "more AR2023 categories harden the law" — by **manufacturing an entire density axis from VG** rather than scavenging natural datasets one at a time (natural variation = the in-field's 4th-dataset hunt; *controlled* variation = this titration; the two are the classic observational+interventional pair that together identify a causal effect). No ledger probe varies training density at all.
- **Feasibility / risk:** It is a **data-ablation experiment, not a model lever** — no architecture/loss/scoring change (the `--subsample-train-frac` knob only decides which existing train rows the loader yields), so it is **compatible with the "lever search CLOSED" directive**: the supervisor explicitly wants *more density points / more datasets to harden the law* (cycle-5 RESEARCHERS-#2), and this is the controlled-experiment way to get a continuum of them. **Pre-empting the KILL-discipline check (cf. the GD1 incident):** this is NOT a V2-regularizer, NOT forced ID→text routing, NOT eval-time popularity/exposure manipulation, NOT a new learnable scalar to be "voted off" — it adds zero parameters and touches only the training-data sampling. It is leak-free (eval fixed; terciles train-only). **Cost:** GPU training, ~6 rungs × 2 arms × 2 seeds ≈ 24 VG-sized-or-smaller runs; each thinned run is *cheaper* than full VG and uses the FAST dataset ⇒ affordable, but it MUST be sequenced to **not contend with the live Beauty 3rd-point job** (run when the GPU is free; it is lower-priority than landing Beauty, higher-value than any further lever). **Risk #1 (the real one — must be controlled, per step 3):** interaction-thinning also shortens users' histories, so a tail-Δ change could be a *sequence-length* effect, not a *global-density* effect. Mitigation: hold eval users' conditioning histories fixed; report the per-rung history-length distribution; cross-check that interaction-mode and user-mode (which perturb length differently) give the same crossing — if they diverge, length is the driver and that is itself a clean finding (refines the law from "global density" to a specific mechanism). **Risk #2:** thinning pushes some items below 5 interactions — *do not re-core* (keep catalog fixed); that items get rarer IS the manipulation, and terciles are frozen from full density so buckets stay comparable. **Risk #3 (honesty):** a 6-rung sigmoid fit on a ~110-hit tail bucket is noisy — report the **monotone trend + sign-flip as the primary claim** (robust), the fitted α₀/EC50 as secondary with a CI, and require the crossing to replicate across both subsampling modes before any "α_c measured at X" statement; a second dense dataset's ladder (Beauty/3rd category, when GPU allows) is needed before any *universality / data-collapse* claim — without it, the deliverable is the single strong causal result "VG, thinned to MI-like density, flips its tail Δ from null to positive at the predicted density," which alone converts the law from a 3-point correlation to a controlled dose–response. **Payoff:** the paper figure becomes *tail Δ vs global training density, one titration curve with the 3 natural datasets sitting on it at their own densities* — the single most reviewer-proof object the campaign could produce for the dataset-conditional law, and the causal capstone that XF-18-1/2/3 (which only derive the threshold) cannot supply on their own.

**Handoff to in-field (priority order): XF-18-4 (controlled density-titration / rarefaction series) is the one idea this cycle, and it is the experimental complement — not a re-skin — of the three observational threshold derivations already on the bus.** Those (XF-18-1 per-item `n*`, XF-18-2 Allan `n*`, XF-18-3 collective `α` transition) all *derive or measure* the threshold and then passively correlate it against 3 natural datasets; this one **manipulates the governing variable (global training density) on a ladder, holding category/encoder/eval/catalog fixed, to locate the tail-law crossing density causally on ONE dataset** — converting the campaign's weakest link (a 3-point, Beauty-blocked correlation a reviewer will call a category confound) into a within-dataset dose–response curve with a predicted, measurable critical density. It is a **data-ablation experiment, not a model lever** (zero new parameters, leak-free, `ρ=1.0` no-op), so it is compliant with the closed-lever pivot and is the *controlled* form of the supervisor's own "more density points / 4th sparse dataset" ask. Implementation = one `--subsample-train-frac ρ --subsample-mode {interaction,user}` data-loader flag + the existing text/ID V2 contrast; run on the FAST VG dataset, sequenced to never contend with the live Beauty job. Pair the interventional titration here with the in-field's *natural*-variation 4th-dataset hunt — observational + interventional together identify the causal effect. Sources: [Rarefaction (ecology) — Gotelli & Colwell, methodology overview](https://www.davidzeleny.net/anadat-r/doku.php/en:rarefaction); [Quantifying biodiversity: rarefaction & extrapolation (Hill numbers, iNEXT)](https://besjournals.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/full/10.1111/j.2041-210x.2011.00178.x); [Dose–response relationship (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dose%E2%80%93response_relationship); [Dose–response curves and determination of EC50 (J. Med. Chem. 2024)](https://pubs.acs.org/doi/10.1021/acs.jmedchem.4c02052); [Phase transitions and finite-size scaling (lecture notes)](https://www.mv.helsinki.fi/home/rummukai/simu/fss.pdf); [Matrix completion sharp sample-complexity threshold `N=O(r·d·log d)` (arXiv:2512.08689)](https://arxiv.org/html/2512.08689v1); [On the fundamental limits of matrix completion — recovery phase transition (arXiv:2109.05408)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2109.05408).

### 2026-06-18 batch #4 — FROM "run the titration" TO "predict its CURVE SHAPE so it adjudicates a MECHANISM." STEP-1 read confirms the state precisely: STEP 0 not triggered (no `SOTA_ACHIEVED.md`); lever search CLOSED (W1/X1/Y1/Z1/GD1/CF1 dead); the density-titration is SANCTIONED (PROPOSAL 2026-06-18-1, supervisor cycle-6, runs on VG after Beauty closes). The supervisor's **cycle-6 RESEARCHERS-#1 binding ask is now the single live cross-field target, verbatim:** *"make the threshold theory QUANTITATIVELY PREDICTIVE of the density-titration curve — don't just argue a sign; specify the predicted curve SHAPE (crossing density α_c, slope, whether a sharp transition or a smooth bias–variance crossover) so the titration can confirm/refute a NUMBER, not just a direction. The collective recovery-transition framing (XF-18-3) using the already-measured effective rank (22–24) is the right vehicle."* This is NOT a new mechanism (the bus already has three: XF-18-1 per-item bias–variance crossover, XF-18-3 collective recovery transition, XF-4/BBP single-spike) — it is the **missing prediction layer**: each of those mechanisms predicts a DIFFERENT functional form for the tail-Δ-vs-density curve, and the titration's SHAPE can therefore *discriminate which mechanism is true* — exactly the supervisor's "sharp transition vs smooth crossover" question. The idea below is one deep read-only THEORY artifact (a pre-registered shape prediction + the analyst-side fitting/collapse protocol), non-collapsible (no parameter), leak-free, and it tells the experiment which rungs to add and the analyst which forms to fit. It is the quantitative complement that turns XF-18-4's "the tail Δ rises and crosses zero" into "the tail Δ follows form F with crossing α_c=X and Hill slope n, and a finite-size sharpening test says it is a genuine transition not a crossover."

## XF 2026-06-18-5 — Critical phenomena (Landau order-parameter scaling + BBP √-cusp vs all-or-nothing jump) + finite-size scaling (Fisher–Barber data-collapse): a pre-registered QUANTITATIVE prediction of the density-titration curve SHAPE that lets the experiment discriminate "sharp collective transition" from "smooth per-item crossover"  [status: GROUNDED→PROPOSAL 2026-06-18-2]
- **Domain & principle:** **Critical-phenomena theory of how an order parameter behaves near a transition, and how finite size rounds it.** Three established results, each giving a *different curve shape* near the critical control parameter: **(A) Continuous (2nd-order) transition / spectral BBP** — the order parameter rises from zero with a power law `q ∝ (α−α_c)^β`; the **mean-field exponent is β=1/2**, and for the spiked-matrix BBP transition this is exact: the leading-eigenvector overlap² obeys `|⟨v₁,x⟩|² = 1 − 1/λ²` for `λ>1` and `0` below, so near threshold `overlap ≈ √(2(λ−1)) ∝ √(λ−α_c)` — a **√-CUSP with vanishing/zero value below `α_c` and INFINITE slope at `α_c`** (Benaych-Georges–Nadakuditi; Barbier RMT/BBP notes). **(B) First-order / all-or-nothing** — for **sparse** signal priors (Bernoulli, Bernoulli–Rademacher) the MMSE **JUMPS discontinuously** from its maximum to ≈0 at a sharp SNR threshold (Barbier–Krzakala–et al., NeurIPS 2020): the order parameter is a **STEP** (β=0, a jump), i.e. a Hill sigmoid with coefficient `n→∞`. **(C) Finite-size scaling** (Fisher–Barber 1972): a sharp transition exists only at `N→∞`; a finite system **rounds and shifts** it over a window controlled by `N`, with the universal scaling form `A(α,N) = N^{κ/ν̄} f((α−α_c)·N^{1/ν̄})` — and crucially, on a *mean-field / random-graph* geometry (the right analogue here — items couple all-to-all through the shared softmax) the rounding variable is **`(α−α_c)·N^{1/2}`** (e.g. Ising on random-regular graphs rounds as `[T/T_c−1]·S^{1/2}`, S = #sites). So the **transition width in the control parameter shrinks as `Δα ∝ N^{−1/2}`** — a *calculable* number — and curves at different `N` **collapse** onto one universal function when plotted vs the scaling variable. The load-bearing law: *the shape of the dose–response near the threshold (smooth-analytic vs √-cusp vs step) and its sharpening with system size are the fingerprints of WHICH transition (or non-transition) governs.*
- **The analogy:** The sanctioned VG density-titration (XF-18-4 / PROPOSAL 2026-06-18-1) traces the **text−ID tail Δ as a function of global density** `α = (interactions/item)/d_eff`, sliding ρ from 1.0 downward. The three live mechanisms predict three *distinguishable* curve shapes for that exact plot, because the tail Δ is (to leading order) a decreasing function of the ID channel's recovery quality `q(α)` — text wins the tail by the margin that ID *fails* to recover it:
  - **Mechanism A — per-item bias–variance crossover (XF-18-1):** ID error `≈ σ²/n ∝ 1/α` (smooth), text error `= b²` (flat) ⇒ `tailΔ(α) ∝ (σ²/n − b²) ∝ c/α − b²`. This is a **SMOOTH, ANALYTIC hyperbola**: monotone-decreasing, FINITE slope everywhere, **NO plateau, NO cusp**, crossing zero at `α* = c/b²` with an ordinary (Hill `n≈1`) slope. Width of the "transition" = the whole ladder (it is a crossover, not a transition).
  - **Mechanism B-continuous — collective spectral transition (XF-18-3, dense prior):** ID overlap `q=0` for `α<α_c` (ID is pure noise ⇒ text wins by a **CONSTANT margin = a FLAT POSITIVE PLATEAU**), then `q ∝ √(α−α_c)` rises above `α_c` ⇒ `tailΔ(α)` is **plateau-positive below `α_c`, a √-CUSP (infinite-slope kink) at `α_c`, then a decline crossing zero** — a shape with a *non-analytic point* the hyperbola lacks.
  - **Mechanism B-first-order — all-or-nothing (XF-18-3, sparse prior):** `q` **JUMPS** 0→≈1 at `α_c` ⇒ `tailΔ(α)` is a **near-vertical STEP** (flat-positive plateau, sharp drop at `α_c`, flat-negative) — a Hill sigmoid with very high coefficient.
  **The decisive, pre-registered NUMBERS** (using the campaign's measured `d_eff≈23`): the crossover/critical density is `α_c≈1` (the BBP/spectral scale; honestly prior-dependent, so pre-register the **band `α_c∈[0.7,1.1]`** spanning info-theoretic→spectral thresholds). In VG units (full = 24.5 interactions/item ⇒ `α_VG=1.07`): **the tail Δ should cross zero at `ρ_c ≈ α_c·23/24.5 ≈ 0.94`** (if `α_c=1.0`), reaching a **positive plateau by `ρ≈0.66`** (= MI's `α≈0.70`). The three natural datasets must then **sit on the titration curve at their own α**: VG (1.07) and Beauty (1.08) just *above* `α_c` (tail-null), MI (0.70) *below* (tail-win) — and the finite-size width at the real catalog `N≈25k` is `Δα ∝ N^{−1/2}≈0.006` (in scaled units), i.e. the transition, *if collective*, should be SHARP (within a few % of density), whereas a per-item crossover would be BROAD across the whole ladder. The single discriminating scalar is the **Hill slope `n` at the crossing**: `n≈1` ⇒ smooth bias–variance crossover (Mechanism A); `n≫1` ⇒ collective transition (B); `n→∞`/step ⇒ all-or-nothing (sparse B). This makes the titration **confirm or refute a NUMBER and a SHAPE**, not just a sign — precisely the ask.
- **Concrete mechanism (read-only THEORY + analyst-side fitting protocol — NOT a training flag, NOT a model lever):** no code in `run_sasrec_sbert.py`; this is a pre-registered prediction + a ≈60-line CPU fitting script the analyst runs over the titration's `(ρ, α, tailΔ, tail-HR)` table once the experiment produces it:
  1. **Pre-register (BEFORE the titration runs) the three candidate forms** with shared `α_c`: (A) hyperbola `tailΔ = A/α − B`; (B-cont) plateau+√-cusp `tailΔ = P − C·√(max(α−α_c,0))`; (B-1st) logistic `tailΔ = L·(1 − 1/(1+e^{−k(α−α_c)})) + D` with free Hill `k`. Fit all three by least-squares to the rung data; report **AIC/BIC** and the **fitted `α_c` (⇒ `ρ_c`) with a bootstrap CI** on the ~110-hit tail bucket.
  2. **Shape test:** report the **Hill slope `n` at the zero-crossing** (the single discriminator above) and whether a **plateau** is statistically present at low ρ (B vs A) — a constant-positive segment below `ρ_c` is the collective-transition signature absent in the hyperbola.
  3. **Finite-size sharpening test (the clean physics adjudicator, optional 2nd ladder):** re-run the titration on a **down-sampled catalog** (randomly keep ~50% of items, so `N`→N/2) — if the transition **WIDENS** by `≈√2` (width `∝N^{−1/2}`) and the two ladders **data-collapse** when plotted vs `(α−α_c)·N^{1/2}`, that is a *genuine finite-size-rounded transition* (Mechanism B); if the width is **insensitive to `N`**, it is a true smooth crossover (Mechanism A). Use the published data-collapse quality measure (Bhattacharjee–Seno) to score the collapse.
  4. **Place the 3 natural datasets on the fitted curve** (VG/MI/Beauty at their measured α) and report whether they fall within the curve's CI — the observational law and the interventional titration agreeing is the reviewer-proof object.
  Cost: seconds on CPU; the only GPU cost is the optional 2nd (down-sampled-catalog) ladder, which is *lower* priority than the primary VG ladder and must not contend with Beauty.
- **Why no CS person would default to this:** an ML practitioner who runs a data-ablation reports "NDCG vs % data" as a monotone **scaling curve** and fits, at most, a power law — they do **not** ask whether the curve has a **√-cusp vs a step vs a smooth hyperbola**, do not read the **Hill slope as a mean-field critical exponent**, and never run a **finite-size data-collapse across two catalog sizes to decide if a sign-flip is a genuine phase transition or a crossover.** Pre-registering competing **order-parameter functional forms** and adjudicating them by **critical-exponent shape + finite-size sharpening** is condensed-matter / statistical-physics-of-inference methodology, not representation-learning practice. The very question "is the cold-start sign reversal a sharp transition or a smooth crossover, and does it sharpen with system size?" is one no recsys paper has posed.
- **Novelty vs our ledger:** XF-18-3 supplies the *mechanism* (a collective transition exists) and XF-18-4 supplies the *experiment* (titrate density), but **neither predicts the curve's SHAPE or gives a discriminating fit** — XF-18-4 explicitly hedges to "monotone trend + sign-flip as the primary claim, fitted EC50 secondary." This artifact is the missing quantitative layer: it (i) derives **three distinguishable shapes** from the three mechanisms already on the bus, (ii) gives the **pre-registered crossing `α_c≈1 ⇒ ρ_c≈0.94` on VG** as a falsifiable number, (iii) supplies the **Hill-slope + plateau + finite-size-collapse tests** that say *which mechanism* the data support — turning the titration from a sign-confirmation into a mechanism-adjudication. No ledger artifact predicts a functional form or proposes a finite-size-collapse test; XF-18-4 is a sibling (it runs the dose; this predicts and fits the response).
- **Feasibility / risk:** read-only/CPU/leak-free/**non-collapsible** (a prediction + fit, no learnable parameter — cannot be "voted off"); compliant with the closed-lever pivot (it adds nothing to the model); it RIDES the already-sanctioned titration (only the optional 2nd down-sampled-catalog ladder needs GPU, and it is explicitly lower-priority than Beauty/the primary ladder). **HONEST PRIOR-ART CAVEAT (flag prominently):** Landau β=1/2, the BBP `1−1/λ²` overlap, all-or-nothing MMSE jumps, and Fisher–Barber finite-size scaling are **textbook physics/probability — zero novelty in the ingredients.** The defensible, narrow novelty = (i) mapping the recsys cold-start titration to an **order-parameter dose–response** and pre-registering **competing critical shapes** for it, (ii) using the **Hill slope / plateau / finite-size data-collapse** to *discriminate* the campaign's two live mechanisms (per-item crossover vs collective transition), and (iii) the **pre-registered VG crossing `ρ_c≈0.94`** as a number the titration can refute. Frame it as that or it is not publishable-novel. **Risk #1 (binding):** a 6-rung fit on a ~110-hit tail bucket is noisy — the ROBUST deliverable is the **shape class** (plateau-and-cusp/step vs smooth-hyperbola) + the **monotone crossing**, with `α_c`/Hill-`n` reported with bootstrap CIs as secondary; do NOT over-claim a precise exponent. **Risk #2:** `α_c` is prior-dependent (spectral λ=1 vs an info-comp-gap value vs the all-or-nothing threshold) — pre-register the **band [0.7,1.1]** and let the *fit* locate it, rather than asserting 1.0. **Risk #3:** the finite-size test needs a 2nd (down-sampled-catalog) ladder = extra GPU; if unaffordable, the single-catalog Hill-slope + plateau test still discriminates A from B (just without the cleanest physics confirmation) — present the finite-size collapse as the optional gold-standard, the slope/plateau as the affordable core. **Payoff:** the paper figure becomes *tail Δ vs density with a fitted critical form, the pre-registered `ρ_c` marked, the 3 natural datasets sitting on the curve, and (if run) a two-size data-collapse* — the difference between "we observed a sign-flip" and "we measured a phase transition at the density our theory predicted." This is the quantitative spine the supervisor asked for, built on XF-18-3 + the measured `d_eff=22–24` exactly as directed.

**Handoff to in-field (priority order): XF-18-5 is the one idea this cycle and it is the supervisor's verbatim cycle-6 #1 ask** — it does NOT add a mechanism (the bus has three) but supplies the **missing quantitative prediction**: three distinguishable curve SHAPES (smooth hyperbola = per-item crossover / plateau+√-cusp = continuous collective transition / step = all-or-nothing), a **pre-registered crossing `α_c≈1 ⇒ ρ_c≈0.94` on VG** using the measured `d_eff≈23`, and the **Hill-slope + plateau + finite-size-data-collapse tests** that let the sanctioned titration *adjudicate which mechanism is true*, not just confirm a sign. It is a read-only THEORY/fitting artifact (pre-register before the titration runs; fit after) — non-collapsible, leak-free, closed-lever-compliant, riding the already-sanctioned PROPOSAL 2026-06-18-1 (only the optional 2nd down-sampled-catalog ladder needs GPU, lower-priority than Beauty). Ground it as: (1) the pre-registration block to drop into the titration plan NOW (so the rungs bracket `ρ≈0.94` finely and a low-ρ plateau region is sampled), and (2) the analyst's fit-and-collapse script for after. Sources: [Discontinuous BBP transitions (arXiv:2604.27992)](https://arxiv.org/html/2604.27992); [Random Matrix Theory & the BBP phase transition — overlap `1−1/λ²` (Barbier notes)](https://jeanbarbier.github.io/jeanbarbier/docs/part1_rmt_bbp_beamer.pdf); [All-or-nothing statistical and computational phase transitions in sparse spiked matrix estimation (Barbier et al., NeurIPS 2020, arXiv:2006.07971)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2006.07971); [Phase transitions in spiked matrix estimation: information-theoretic analysis (arXiv:1806.04343)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1806.04343); [Fundamental limits of Non-Linear Low-Rank Matrix Estimation — only a "smoothed-out trace" persists in proportional rank (arXiv:2403.04234)](https://arxiv.org/html/2403.04234v1); [Finite-size scaling theory (pyfssa)](https://pyfssa.readthedocs.io/en/stable/fss-theory.html); [A measure of data-collapse for scaling (Bhattacharjee–Seno, cond-mat/0102515)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/cond-mat/0102515); [Data collapse in the critical region with subleading corrections (cond-mat/0505194)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/cond-mat/0505194).
