### 2026-06-19 batch — AIMED AT THE FRESHEST LIVE RESULT (postdates the cycle-6 status board). STEP-0 not triggered (no `SOTA_ACHIEVED.md`). STEP-1 read of the bus changes the target decisively: **the sanctioned interaction-mode density-titration (PROPOSAL 2026-06-18-1/-2) has RUN and REFUTED tail density-causation.** Per ANALYSIS_LOG -7/-8: across the full 7-rung VG interaction-thinning ladder the text−ID **tail Δ shows NO monotone crossing** — it goes *more negative* as density drops (ρ=1.0 −0.000148 → ρ=0.66/MI-equiv −0.00045, 0/2 positive, the most-negative thinned point), so thinning VG to MI's global density yields a tail **LOSS, not** MI's tail win. BUT the same ladder revealed a clean NEW positive: a **monotone HEAD/overall dose-response** (text's head advantage ≈doubles +0.0020→+0.0039 as VG thins, 11/11 cells positive). So **global interactions/item is a causal driver of the HEAD/overall text-complementarity, but only a descriptive CORRELATE of the tail** — the tail win is a **density-irreducible, MI-specific residual.** The analyst's now-verbatim live question (and the in-field's grounding target): *"what MI-specific property, co-varying with but distinct from global interactions/item, drives the TAIL win?"* — lead candidate **users/item** (MI 2.34 ≪ VG 3.70), directly testable by the **already-implemented `--subsample-mode user`** titration (thin whole users, not interactions). The idea below is the cross-field MECHANISM + a falsifiable functional-form prediction for exactly that experiment — it is NOT a model lever (a pre-registered prediction + read-only fit riding the existing data-loader flag), non-collapsible (no learnable scalar), leak-free (train-only thinning, frozen terciles, fixed eval), and it does triple duty: it *explains* the titration's head/tail split, it turns the analyst's bare "try users/item" into a mechanism with a pre-registered double-dissociation, and it adjudicates *which* co-limitation form the tail obeys.

## XF 2026-06-19-1 — Plant-nutrient ecology (Liebig's Law of the Minimum + Tilman resource-ratio R* + the co-limitation functional-form taxonomy): ID-learnability as a TWO-RESOURCE co-limited process — count (interactions/item) limits the HEAD, connectivity (users/item) limits the TAIL — with a pre-registered double-dissociation for the `--subsample-mode user` titration  [status: GROUNDED→PROPOSAL 2026-06-19-1]
- **Domain & principle:** **Liebig's Law of the Minimum** (Sprengel 1828; popularized by Justus von Liebig 1840): the yield/growth of an organism is set by the single **scarcest** essential, non-substitutable resource — the "Liebig's barrel" whose shortest stave fixes the water level; adding a *non-limiting* resource produces **no response** until the binding one is relieved. Modern multiple-resource theory refines this into a **functional-form taxonomy** that the recsys problem can borrow wholesale: (1) **Liebig MIN** — growth `∝ min_k(R_k/K_k)` (strict single-limitation, a kinked/piecewise response); (2) **multiplicative / synthesizing-unit (SU)** — `∝ Π_k f(R_k)` (smooth interactive co-limitation); (3) **additive** — `∝ Σ_k f(R_k)`. Tang & Riley 2021 (*Ecological Applications*, "Finding Liebig's law of the minimum") show these forms are **empirically distinguishable** by fitting growth-vs-multi-substrate data and comparing fit (additive/SU often beat strict-Liebig for microbes/plants). Harpole et al. 2011 (*Ecology Letters*) give the **co-limitation taxonomy** — simultaneous, independent (sub-/super-additive), serial — and show co-limitation is *common*, i.e. the "one limiting nutrient" default is often wrong. **Tilman's resource-ratio (R\*) theory** (Tilman 1980/1982) adds the decisive twist for *strata*: **which resource is limiting depends on the SUPPLY RATIO of the two resources** — along a gradient of `R1:R2` supply, the identity of the binding resource *switches*. The load-bearing law: when growth depends on two non-substitutable resources, **(a)** depleting the *non-binding* one does nothing (Liebig), **(b)** *which* resource binds is set by the local supply ratio (Tilman), and **(c)** the *shape* of the joint response (min vs multiplicative vs additive) is a measurable, model-selectable fingerprint of the co-limitation type.
- **The analogy:** A catalog item's **ID-embedding learnability** is a growth process fed by **two distinct, non-substitutable data resources**: **R1 = interactions/item** (per-item *sample count* / observation depth — Fisher-information of the item's own mean estimate) and **R2 = users/item** (collaborative *connectivity* — the bipartite mean item-degree = how many distinct users *bridge* this item to the rest of the catalog; the gradient-carrying co-occurrence edges, = XF-18-3's KS detectability degree). These are **not substitutable**: 100 interactions from 2 users (high R1, low R2) is a near-isolated dyad with almost no collaborative bridges; 4 interactions from 4 distinct users (low R1, moderate R2) is well-connected. **The just-completed titration is a textbook Liebig signature.** Interaction-thinning depletes **R1** and moved the **HEAD** (head text-complementarity doubled, monotone, causal) ⇒ *R1 is the HEAD's binding resource*. The **same** R1 depletion did **NOT** move the **TAIL** (no crossing, even at MI-equiv density) ⇒ by Liebig, *R1 is NOT the tail's binding resource* — depleting a non-binding resource yields no response, **exactly what was observed.** Therefore the tail's binding resource must be the **OTHER** one: **R2 = users/item / connectivity.** This is **Tilman's supply-ratio switch made concrete**: head items (popular ⇒ high R1 *and* high R2, but R1-rich) are count-limited; tail items (rare ⇒ low R1 *and* low R2) sit at a different supply ratio where **connectivity binds**. MI wins the tail because its catalog-wide **users/item is far lower (2.34 ≪ 3.70)** ⇒ tail items are connectivity-starved ⇒ their ID embeddings are unlearnable *for lack of collaborative bridges* (not lack of raw count, which is flat at the 5-core floor 4/4/5 across datasets — the supervisor's own "tail-local exposure FLAT" finding, which a pure R1/count theory cannot reconcile but the R2/connectivity theory *predicts*), and the always-observed frozen-text channel complements. **This is the mechanism the bus lacks:** it reconciles (i) flat tail-local count, (ii) interactions/item driving the head but not the tail, and (iii) users/item ranking with the tail Δ — three facts no single prior framing explains together — by saying *the head and the tail are limited by different data resources.*
- **Concrete mechanism (a pre-registered PREDICTION + read-only analyst fit — NOT a model lever; rides the ALREADY-IMPLEMENTED `--subsample-train-frac/--subsample-mode user` data-loader flag):** no code in `run_sasrec_sbert.py` (the flag exists from PROPOSAL 2026-06-18-1); this contributes the *experiment design + the pre-registered functional-form prediction* the user-mode titration needs to be decisive rather than just "another ablation."
  1. **The decisive new run (EXPERIMENT, when GPU free — analyst already flagged it idle):** the **`--subsample-mode user` VG titration** — drop whole users w.p. 1−ρ so **R2 (users/item) falls** while **each surviving user's full history length is PRESERVED** (the key design advantage: it isolates connectivity from the **sequence-length confound** that contaminated the interaction-mode ladder, XF-18-4 Risk #1). Ladder ρ_user chosen so users/item slides 3.70 → through MI's 2.34 → below; freeze tail/mid/head terciles on the FULL train; hold eval users/histories/targets/catalog fixed; BOTH arms (TEXT=V2, ID-only=`--no-sbert`), ≥2 seeds/rung.
  2. **Pre-registered DOUBLE-DISSOCIATION prediction (the falsifiable core):** *interaction-mode moves HEAD-not-tail (already observed); user-mode moves TAIL-not-head.* Concretely — as users/item drops under user-thinning, **VG's tail Δ RISES toward positive** (the null→positive crossing the interaction ladder *missed*), while the head dose-response is comparatively flat (the head's binding resource R1 is barely relieved by removing users at matched interaction loss). A clean head/tail × interaction/user double dissociation is the **causal proof that the two terciles have different binding resources** — far stronger than either ladder alone.
  3. **The matched-R1 contrast (defeats the non-orthogonality risk):** user-thinning also lowers total interactions, so it isn't a *pure* R2 knob. Mitigation = compare the two modes **at the SAME interactions/item**: at a rung where interaction-mode and user-mode reach equal R1 but different R2, any **difference in tail Δ is attributable to R2 (connectivity) alone** — the recsys analogue of an epidemiological matched/negative-control design. Report R1 *and* R2 per rung so the analyst can build this matched contrast.
  4. **Functional-form adjudication (analyst-side, read-only CPU fit ≈40 lines):** pool both ladders' `(R1, R2, tail Δ, head Δ)` cells and fit the three co-limitation forms — **Liebig** `tailΔ ∝ g(min(R1/K1, R2/K2))`, **multiplicative/SU** `∝ g(R1)·g(R2)`, **additive** `∝ g(R1)+g(R2)` — by least-squares + **AIC/BIC**, exactly as Tang–Riley 2021 / Harpole 2011 select co-limitation type from fertilization assays. Test the **data-collapse**: both modes' tail-Δ curves should **collapse onto one curve when plotted vs the BINDING resource (predicted: users/item)** and **diverge vs the non-binding one (interactions/item)** — collapse-on-R2 is the single cleanest confirmation that connectivity is the tail's limiting resource. Place VG/MI/Beauty on the **(R1, R2) Tilman resource-supply plane** (a paper figure): the law upgrades from the *refuted* "tail win iff interactions/item < R1\*" to "tail win iff **users/item < R2\***," with the three natural datasets sitting on the correct side of the connectivity threshold.
- **Why no CS person would default to this:** an ML practitioner models "more data → better" as a **single scalar** (one learning/scaling curve, monotone, one limiting quantity). The idea that learnability is **co-limited by two non-substitutable data resources** (sample *count* vs collaborative *connectivity*), that **different popularity strata are limited by different resources** because they sit at different *supply ratios* (Tilman), that depleting a *non-binding* resource produces *no response* (Liebig — the literal explanation for the tail's non-response to interaction-thinning), and that you **adjudicate the co-limitation type by AIC-fitting min-vs-multiplicative-vs-additive forms to a two-mode titration with a double-dissociation design** — is plant-nutrient / agronomic ecology methodology (fertilization-assay thinking), not representation learning. No recsys pipeline runs a two-resource co-limitation assay or asks "which data resource is the shortest stave for *this* popularity stratum."
- **Novelty vs our ledger:** Nearest are **XF-18-3** (collective recovery transition), **XF-18-4** (interaction-mode titration, now run), **XF-18-5** (curve shape). Decisive distinctions: **(1) vs XF-18-3** — it derived R1-based `α` and R2-based `d·λ²` as *two separate transition conditions* but treated them as **REDUNDANT** (MI below both, VG/Beauty above both) and had no titration data; it never predicted the two resources would **DISSOCIATE**, and a redundant-condition view is now *refuted* (R1-depletion via interaction-thinning failed to move the tail — so R1 and the tail are decoupled, not redundant). This idea's load-bearing new content is precisely that **the two density resources are NON-redundant and stratum-specific** (R1→head, R2→tail), which *explains the titration's head/tail split that XF-18-3 cannot.* **(2) vs XF-18-4/-5** — those treat density as **ONE control parameter** and titrate **ONE mode** (interaction); both implicitly assume a single limiting resource, which the data just refuted for the tail. This is the first **two-resource, two-mode** framing with a **double-dissociation** prediction. **(3)** No ledger artifact fits **co-limitation functional forms** (Liebig-min vs multiplicative vs additive) or builds a **Tilman resource-supply plane** for the datasets. It also converts the analyst's bare recommendation ("test users/item via `--subsample-mode user`") into a *mechanism + pre-registered falsifiable prediction + a form-selection fit* — i.e. it tells the experiment *what to expect and how to read it*, not just *what to run*. **Honest prior-art caveat:** Liebig/Tilman/co-limitation are textbook ecology — **zero novelty in the ingredients**; the defensible, narrow novelty is (i) mapping ID-learnability to **count-vs-connectivity co-limitation**, (ii) the **pre-registered double-dissociation** across the two titration modes as a causal identification of *which* resource limits *which* stratum, and (iii) the **co-limitation-form fit + Tilman-plane** as the law's mechanism. Frame it as that, not "ecology for recsys."
- **Feasibility / risk:** **Not a model lever** (rides the existing `--subsample-mode user` data-loader flag; zero new params, no arch/loss/scoring change ⇒ compliant with the closed-lever pivot — it is the *user-mode* sibling of the already-sanctioned interaction titration, which the supervisor explicitly classed as a DATA-ablation, not a lever). **Leak-free** (train-only thinning; terciles frozen on full train; eval users/histories/targets/catalog fixed). **Non-collapsible** (no learnable scalar; the fit is read-only). GPU cost = one user-mode ladder on the FAST VG dataset (each thinned run trains on *less* data ⇒ cheaper than full VG), sequenced to **never contend with the live Beauty 3rd-point job** (lower priority than landing Beauty, higher-value than the queued Digital_Music 4th point because it is *causal*, not another correlation point). **Risk #1 (the real one):** user-thinning lowers R1 as well as R2 (not perfectly orthogonal) — fully handled by step 3's **matched-R1 contrast** (compare modes at equal interactions/item; the residual tail-Δ difference *is* the R2/connectivity effect) and by reporting both R1,R2 per rung. **Risk #2:** ~110-hit VG tail bucket ⇒ noisy — report the **sign + monotone trend + the double-dissociation** as the primary, robust claim; the fitted R2\* / co-limitation form (AIC winner) as secondary with bootstrap CIs (same discipline as the val=0.0765 and seed08 traps). **Risk #3 (the clean fork — both outcomes publishable):** if user-mode **DOES** lift the tail where interaction-mode didn't ⇒ **connectivity (users/item) is the tail's binding resource, causally identified on ONE dataset with category/encoder/protocol fixed** — this *resurrects* a causal tail law (on the correct resource axis) that the interaction titration had refuted, the single most valuable repair the campaign could make. If user-mode **ALSO** fails to move the tail ⇒ **neither global density resource binds the tail** ⇒ the MI tail win is driven by a non-density, MI-idiosyncratic property (text-domain distinctiveness / category structure), a clean publishable negative that *hardens* the honest-negative section and tells the in-field to stop hunting density and look at MI's content. Either way the artifact is decisive and the (R1,R2) Tilman-plane figure stands. **Payoff:** the paper figure becomes *tail Δ vs the two data resources, with a co-limitation-form fit and a double-dissociation table* — it explains the otherwise-puzzling titration result (R1 drives the head, not the tail) and either repairs the causal tail law on the connectivity axis or cleanly closes it as content-driven.

**Handoff to in-field (priority order): XF-2026-06-19-1 is the one idea this cycle and it targets the freshest result on the bus** — the interaction-mode titration just *refuted* interactions/item as a tail cause (while confirming it as a HEAD cause), and the analyst's verbatim open question is "which MI-specific property distinct from interactions/item drives the tail." This idea answers it with a MECHANISM (count vs connectivity **co-limitation** — head limited by interactions/item, tail by users/item, per Liebig + Tilman supply-ratio), grounds the analyst's own recommended `--subsample-mode user` titration into a **pre-registered double-dissociation** (interaction-mode = head-not-tail [observed]; user-mode = tail-not-head [predicted]) with a **matched-R1 contrast** that isolates connectivity and a **co-limitation-form AIC fit** (Liebig-min vs multiplicative vs additive) + a **Tilman (R1,R2)-plane** figure. It is a DATA-ablation + read-only fit (not a model lever ⇒ closed-lever-compliant), leak-free, non-collapsible, runs on FAST VG, and is **causal** (one dataset, all confounders fixed) — strictly higher-value than the queued natural-variation Digital_Music 4th point. Ground it as: (1) the user-mode ρ-ladder spec (bracket users/item from 3.70 through 2.34 and below; both arms; report R1 AND R2 per rung), and (2) the analyst's read-only form-fit + double-dissociation table to run after. Sources: [Liebig's law of the minimum (Wikipedia — Sprengel/von Liebig, the barrel)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liebig's_law_of_the_minimum); [Finding Liebig's law of the minimum — Tang & Riley 2021, Ecological Applications (Liebig-MIN vs additive vs synthesizing-unit form selection)](https://esajournals.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.1002/eap.2458); [Nutrient co-limitation of primary producer communities — Harpole et al. 2011, Ecology Letters (simultaneous/independent/serial co-limitation taxonomy)](https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.1111/j.1461-0248.2011.01651.x); [Multiple resource limitation: Liebig's minimum rule vs interactive co-limitation — Sperfeld et al. 2012, Ecology Letters](https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.1111/j.1461-0248.2011.01719.x); [Tilman resource-ratio (R*) theory — supply ratio sets which resource limits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R*_rule_(ecology)).

## XF 2026-06-19-2 — Structural rigidity / condensed-matter network glasses (Maxwell–Calladine constraint counting, isostaticity, rigidity percolation): the tail's "floppy-mode" transition — distinct-user interactions are INDEPENDENT constraints that rigidify an item embedding, repeat-user interactions are REDUNDANT constraints (self-stress) that don't — giving the *mechanism* and a *sharp, discriminating prediction* for the user-mode titration  [status: GROUNDED→PROPOSAL 2026-06-19-2]
- **Domain & principle:** **Maxwell constraint counting** (J.C. Maxwell 1864) and its modern form, **rigidity percolation / isostaticity** in condensed matter (Thorpe 1983 on network glasses; Jacobs–Thorpe pebble-game 1995; Calladine 1978's index theorem). A mechanical network of nodes with `D` degrees of freedom (DOF) each, tied by `C` bar-constraints, is **rigid** iff the constraints span the DOF; the boundary `C = DOF` is the **isostatic point**. Below it, **floppy modes** appear — continuous zero-energy deformations along which the structure is *under-determined* (an entire manifold of equally-valid configurations). The load-bearing subtleties that make this more than "count edges": **(1)** the **Maxwell–Calladine index theorem** `f − s = D·N − C` (floppy modes `f` minus states of self-stress `s` = total DOF minus constraints) — adding a constraint to an *already-rigid* region does **not** remove a floppy mode; it creates a **state of self-stress** (the structure becomes *over-stressed*, not *more rigid*). Stress ⟺ redundancy. **(2)** Only **independent** constraints (linearly independent rows of the rigidity matrix) remove a DOF; **redundant** constraints are "wasted" on rigidity. **(3)** **Rigidity percolation has a STRICTLY HIGHER threshold than connectivity percolation** — empirically e.g. mean coordination ⟨r⟩≈4.41 vs Maxwell mean-field 4 on a hierarchical lattice, and universally a *rigid* cluster requires more/better-placed bonds than a merely *connected* one. **A graph can be fully connected yet globally floppy.** The CS bridge that recsys never uses but is exact: **Singer & Cucuringu 2010** (*Uniqueness of Low-Rank Matrix Completion by Rigidity Theory*, SIAM J. Matrix Anal. 31(4):1621–1641; their stated applications include **collaborative filtering**) prove that completing a **rank-`r`, `m×n`** matrix from observed entries is a rigidity problem in disguise — inner products play the role distances play in bar-frameworks: the matrix has exactly **`r(m+n−r)` DOF**, the bipartite observation graph must be **RIGID (not just connected)** to pin them down uniquely, and their **"completion matrix"** (Jacobian of the observation map) is the analogue of the **rigidity matrix** — its **generic rank deficiency = floppy modes = non-unique completion**.
- **The analogy:** A sequential recommender's learned **ID-embedding table is a low-rank matrix factorization** (user-state × item-embedding inner products reconstruct the interaction signal). Each item's `d`-dim ID vector is a **node** in this framework; each observed **user–item interaction is a bar-constraint** tying that item to a point in user-state space. *Learning an item embedding ⇄ completing/pinning its node against its constraints.* This reframes the long-tail exactly: a tail item is a node with **too few independent constraints to escape its floppy modes** — its embedding lives on an under-determined manifold (the high-variance, untrustworthy vector the campaign keeps diagnosing), and the frozen-text channel (always "observed", constraint-free) is the rigid scaffold that complements it. **The decisive new content this brings over XF-19-1 (Liebig two-resource co-limitation): rigidity theory says WHICH interactions count.** Distinct users supply **independent** constraints (each ties the item to a *different* region of user-state) → each removes a DOF → rigidifies. Repeated interactions from the **same few users** (the high-R1/low-R2 dyad: high interactions/item, low users/item) are **redundant** constraints → they add a **state of self-stress, not rigidity** → the item stays floppy no matter how many you pile on. **This is the rigorous mechanism for the titration's central puzzle** — why thinning *interactions/item* (R1) moved the head but NOT the tail (raw count adds mostly redundant self-stress on tail items, never crossing their isostatic point), and why *users/item* (R2 = independent-constraint supply) is the lead tail candidate. It also explains the supervisor's "tail-local raw count is FLAT at the 5-core floor (4/4/5) across datasets" finding that a pure-count theory cannot: raw count is flat *and irrelevant* — only the **independent-constraint count** (distinct-user connectivity) varies and binds. MI wins the tail because at **users/item = 2.34** its tail items sit **below the isostatic point** (floppy → text rigidifies); VG at **3.70** and Beauty at **3.51** sit **above** it (rigid enough → text null). Regime-migration falls out: as catalogs densify, items cross the isostatic point tail→mid→head.
- **Concrete model mechanism — primarily a SHARP, DISCRIMINATING PREDICTION for the already-sanctioned user-mode titration + a parameter-free analyst-side per-item predictor (closed-lever-compliant); implementable model arm flagged as likely-dead and discarded honestly:**
  1. **Sharper prediction than XF-19-1 (the falsifiable upgrade).** XF-19-1 predicts only a *monotone* "tail Δ rises as users/item falls." Rigidity theory predicts a **SHARP isostatic KNEE**, not a gradual slope: VG's tail Δ should stay ≈null while users/item is above the isostatic point and then **cross null→positive abruptly** as user-thinning drives users/item below a critical `u* ≈ r_eff` (the *effective collaborative rank* of the item factor — far below the nominal `d=64`; estimable from the SVD spectrum of the ID table). Two extra discriminating teeth no prior framing has: **(a) `u*` sits ABOVE the connectivity-percolation point** — so the tail can fail (floppy) even where the user–item graph is still fully connected; the knee should appear at a *higher* users/item than a naïve "graph falls apart" account predicts. **(b)** Pre-register `u*` from the spectrum (Singer–Cucuringu: need ≳ `r_eff` independent bars per item) and check whether MI's 2.34 lands below it and VG's 3.70 above — a *quantitative* placement of the three datasets, not just an ordering.
  2. **The self-stress / matched-R1 prediction (the mechanism's signature).** At **matched interactions/item**, the two titration modes differ only in *constraint independence*: user-thinning removes whole users = **independent** constraints (de-rigidifies → tail Δ moves); interaction-thinning that preserves user diversity removes mostly **redundant** repeat-user constraints = **self-stress** (rigidity unchanged → tail Δ flat). So the predicted **double dissociation is not just "different resources" but "independent vs redundant constraints"**, and it predicts the *matched-R1 contrast* (XF-19-1 step 3) will show a tail-Δ gap **in the direction of whichever mode stripped more distinct users**. This is a strictly stronger, mechanism-level version of XF-19-1's prediction and shares its one decisive run (no new GPU cost).
  3. **Parameter-free per-item rigidity predictor (analyst-side, read-only ≈30-line CPU fit — non-collapsible, cannot be voted off like W1/X1/Y1's three nulled scalars).** For each item compute an **independent-constraint count** — cheapest proxy = number of **distinct users** who interacted with it (its bipartite degree); sharper = the **generic rank of its local completion submatrix** (rank of the stacked user-state rows for that item, the Singer–Cucuringu completion-matrix block) — and a **floppiness index** `φ_i = max(0, r_eff − indep_i)/r_eff ∈ [0,1]`. Predictions the analyst can test on EXISTING runs without any training: (i) `φ_i` should **correlate with per-item text−ID advantage** *better than raw frequency or users/item alone* (it is the theory-derived combination); (ii) the **tail-tercile mean `φ`** should be **high for MI, low for VG/Beauty**, quantitatively tracking the tail-Δ ordering; (iii) within a dataset, items matched on raw count but differing in distinct-user count should differ in text advantage in the direction of `φ`. A clean parameter-free predictor that *derives* the tail law is exactly the artifact the paper's mechanism section needs.
  4. **Implementable model arm — flagged LIKELY-DEAD and discarded (honesty).** The literal mechanism would gate each item's ID-vector trust by `1−φ_i`, routing floppy (tail) items onto the frozen-text scaffold — but at the deep tail `φ_i→1` this is **bit-identical to the KILLED Z1 / fitness-gate forced ID→text routing (tail −75%) and the discarded literal-blend arm of XF-17-4**. So the routing realization is **not proposed** (it reduces to a dead direction, and the closed-lever pivot forbids new levers anyway). The one *non-routing* model realization that is NOT dead is the **Gavish–Donoho optimal-spectral-shrinkage of the ID-embedding MATRIX** already grounded as **PROPOSAL 2026-06-17-2** (denoise only the shared spectrum, keep every item's channel) — rigidity theory simply **re-derives its `r_eff` cutoff as the isostatic rank** and supplies the per-item `φ_i` as that proposal's directive-(c) diagnostic. No new lever; this idea's deliverable is the **prediction + predictor + mechanism**, not code.
- **Why no CS person would default to this:** ML models "more data → better" as a **single monotone scalar** (one scaling curve, one limiting quantity) and treats every observed interaction as **equally informative**. Rigidity theory says the opposite on both counts: learnability has a **sharp isostatic phase transition** (not a smooth curve), it is governed by **constraint *independence* not count** (so repeat-user interactions are *self-stress, wasted on rigidity*), and the binding threshold sits at the **rigidity-percolation point, strictly above mere connectivity**. "Your tail item embeddings have floppy zero-modes because their observation graph is connected-but-not-rigid, and piling on redundant interactions only over-stresses them" is **mechanical-engineering / network-glass condensed-matter physics** (pebble-game rigidity analysis), not representation learning. The Singer–Cucuringu matrix-completion↔rigidity bridge exists in math but is **completely absent from sequential-recsys practice**, which reaches for popularity debiasing, IPS, or cold-start side-info — never a Maxwell constraint count.
- **Novelty vs our ledger:** Nearest neighbours are **XF-19-1** (Liebig two-resource co-limitation — count vs connectivity), **XF-18-3** (replica/cavity + SBM Kesten–Stigum *spectral detectability* transition), **XF-17-5** (connectivity percolation / branching floor), and **XF-17-4 / PROPOSAL 2026-06-17-2** (BBP + Gavish–Donoho spectral shrinkage). Decisive distinctions: **(1) vs XF-19-1** — Liebig says "two non-substitutable resources, one binds the tail"; rigidity says *why* and *which interactions count* — the binding resource is **independent-constraint supply**, and it gives the **self-stress mechanism** (redundant repeat-user constraints add stress not rigidity) that explains the flat-raw-count finding Liebig only labels. It upgrades XF-19-1's *monotone* titration prediction to a **sharp isostatic knee with a pre-registered `u*` and an above-connectivity placement** — a strictly more falsifiable test on the *same* run. **(2) vs XF-18-3** — that is a **spectral/statistical detectability** transition (signal-eigenvalue vs MP bulk, a *collective* recovery threshold for the whole matrix); rigidity is a **combinatorial/geometric** transition about **per-item local completability** (the completion-matrix rank, floppy modes of a single node) — different threshold (rigidity > connectivity, and generally ≠ the spectral KS point), different object (a node's local DOF vs a global eigenvalue), and it yields a **per-item `φ_i`** that XF-18-3's global `α`/`d·λ²` cannot. **(3) vs XF-17-5** — connectivity percolation is *exactly the threshold rigidity exceeds*; this idea's headline prediction (the tail fails *above* the connectivity point, while still connected) is the precise content XF-17-5 lacks. **(4) vs XF-17-4** — BBP/Gavish–Donoho act on the **global spectrum** of the embedding matrix; rigidity re-derives their `r_eff` cutoff as an **isostatic rank** and adds the **local per-item** floppiness `φ_i` and the **independent-vs-redundant** distinction, which a spectrum-only view cannot express. **Honest prior-art caveat:** Maxwell counting, rigidity percolation, the pebble game, and Singer–Cucuringu's rigidity↔matrix-completion theorem are **all textbook / published — zero novelty in the ingredients.** The defensible, narrow novelty is **(i)** mapping the **sequential-recsys long-tail** to a **floppy-mode / isostatic transition** of the item-factor framework, **(ii)** the **independent-vs-redundant-constraint (self-stress)** account of why distinct-user connectivity binds the tail but raw count does not, and **(iii)** the **sharp-knee + above-connectivity + pre-registered-`u*`** prediction that turns the user-mode titration into a *mechanism* test and the parameter-free per-item `φ_i` predictor. Frame it as exactly that, not "rigidity theory for recsys."
- **Feasibility / risk:** **Not a model lever** — it rides the **already-sanctioned, already-implemented `--subsample-mode user` titration** (no new GPU run beyond the one the pipeline is converging on; the analyst-side `φ_i` fit is read-only CPU on existing artifacts) ⇒ fully compliant with the closed-lever pivot. **Leak-free** (`indep_i`/distinct-user counts and the local completion-submatrix rank are TRAIN-only; terciles frozen on full train; eval untouched). **Non-collapsible** (no learnable scalar; `φ_i` and `u*` are computed, not fit by SGD — immune to the W1/X1/Y1 "model votes the scalar off" failure mode). **Risk #1 (the real one):** `r_eff` estimation is the load-bearing free quantity — estimate it from the ID-table SVD spectrum (e.g. participation ratio / Gavish–Donoho hard threshold) and report `u*` with a sensitivity band; if `u*` brackets none of {MI 2.34, VG 3.70, Beauty 3.51} cleanly, the *placement* claim weakens to the *ordering* claim (still publishable). **Risk #2:** the ~110-hit VG tail bucket is noisy ⇒ the **sharp-knee** prediction may be unresolvable on so few hits — fall back to the robust claims (the sign of the matched-R1 self-stress contrast; `φ_i`'s correlation with text advantage across the full catalog where N is large). **Risk #3 (the clean fork, both branches publishable):** if the user-mode titration shows the **knee** → the tail law is causally an **isostatic / floppy-mode transition on independent-constraint supply** — the deepest mechanism the campaign could land, and it *predicts* `u*` rather than just correlating density. If it shows **no knee / no crossing** → rigidity-of-the-observation-graph is **refuted** as the tail driver too, hardening the honest-negative section and pointing the in-field decisively at MI's **content/text-domain** idiosyncrasy (not any graph/density property). Either branch sharpens the paper's mechanism section. **Payoff:** converts XF-19-1's monotone connectivity prediction into a **mechanism-level, pre-registered, sharp-transition** test on the *same* decisive run, and yields a **parameter-free per-item floppiness predictor `φ_i`** that derives the tail law for the paper — with the model-lever arm honestly discarded as reducing to the killed Z1 routing. Sources: [Singer & Cucuringu 2010, *Uniqueness of Low-Rank Matrix Completion by Rigidity Theory* (arXiv 0902.3846; SIAM J. Matrix Anal. 31(4):1621–1641 — DOF `r(m+n−r)`, rigid-not-connected observation graph, completion matrix = rigidity-matrix analogue; CF application)](https://arxiv.org/abs/0902.3846); [Comparison of connectivity and rigidity percolation (rigidity threshold strictly above connectivity)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/cond-mat/0702635); [Frictional rigidity percolation — new universality class, Phys. Rev. X 9 021006 (2019)](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevX.9.021006); [Maxwell–Calladine index theorem & states of self-stress / redundancy (rigidity-theory lecture notes, Northwestern)](http://www.eecs.northwestern.edu/~peters/references/RigidityIntro.pdf); [Energetic rigidity: self-stress vs constraint counting, arXiv 2102.11310](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2102.11310).

**Handoff to in-field (priority order): XF-2026-06-19-2 is this cycle's one idea and it is a MECHANISM-and-PREDICTION upgrade to the already-grounded XF-2026-06-19-1, targeting the same single decisive run (the `--subsample-mode user` titration) — so it costs no extra GPU.** XF-19-1 grounded *that* titration with a Liebig two-resource framing and a *monotone* prediction; XF-19-2 supplies the **mechanism** (distinct-user interactions = independent constraints that rigidify; repeat-user interactions = redundant self-stress that don't — the rigorous reason raw count doesn't move the tail) and a **strictly sharper, more falsifiable prediction** (a *sharp isostatic knee* at a pre-registered `u* ≈ effective collaborative rank, which sits ABOVE the connectivity-percolation point* — not a gradual slope) plus a **parameter-free, non-collapsible per-item floppiness predictor `φ_i`** the analyst can test on EXISTING runs today (correlate `φ_i` with per-item text−ID advantage; check tail-mean `φ` tracks the MI>VG≈Beauty ordering). **Ground it as an addendum to PROPOSAL 2026-06-19-1, not a new run:** (1) add the sharp-knee + above-connectivity + pre-registered-`u*` expectations to the titration's read-out spec (estimate `r_eff` from the ID-table SVD spectrum); (2) add the matched-R1 contrast's *self-stress* interpretation (the tail-Δ gap should follow whichever mode stripped more distinct users); (3) commission the analyst's read-only `φ_i` correlation on existing artifacts. The model-lever arm is **deliberately NOT proposed** — it reduces to the killed Z1 forced ID→text routing; the only live model realization is the existing PROPOSAL 2026-06-17-2 spectral-shrinkage, for which rigidity merely re-derives the `r_eff` cutoff. This is closed-lever-compliant (data-ablation + read-only fit), leak-free, non-collapsible, and directly serves the convergence-to-paper mandate by deepening the mechanism section.

## XF 2026-06-19-3 — Cognitive-neuropsychology methodology (double-dissociation = a single INTERACTION test, NOT two separate tests; Nieuwenhuis 2011; Crawford–Garthwaite small-sample dissociation criteria) + statistical-physics data-collapse quality (Houdayer–Hartmann reduced-χ²): the correct, honest statistical+figure protocol for reporting the user-mode titration "double result"  [status: GROUNDED→PROPOSAL 2026-06-19-3]
- **⚠ Scope note (convergence-compliant):** Per AGENT_FEEDBACK cycle 7's binding CONVERGENCE + RESEARCHERS-RETARGET directives, the lever/mechanism search is CLOSED and the user-mode titration (PROPOSAL 2026-06-19-1, grounded by XF-19-1/-2) is the ONE final experiment. This block proposes **no new run, no new axis, no model lever, no GPU** — it is the supervisor's explicitly-requested *paper-grade support*: the cross-field-imported **statistical test** and **figure-quality metric** that make the titration's already-planned "double result" defensible instead of inflatable. It attaches to the existing run's read-out, owned by the analyst/in-field.
- **Domain & principle:** **(1) Cognitive neuropsychology — the double-dissociation statistics.** A *double dissociation* (factor A impairs task X but spares Y; factor B does the reverse) is the field's gold-standard claim that two processes are distinct — *exactly* the structure of "interaction-thinning (R1) moves the HEAD not the tail; user-thinning (R2) moves the TAIL not the head." The decisive, repeatedly-violated methodological law: **a dissociation is established by a single statistical test on the INTERACTION (factor × task), never by running two separate tests and noting one is significant and the other is not.** Nieuwenhuis, Forstmann & Wagenmakers 2011 (*Nat. Neurosci.* 14:1105, "Erroneous analyses of interactions: a problem of significance") audited 513 papers in five top journals and found ~half committed the error — "**the difference between significant and non-significant is not itself significant.**" The correct object is the **difference-of-differences** (here a *triple* difference: [textΔ on tail − textΔ on head] under user-mode *minus* the same under interaction-mode), and it must clear its own test. Crawford & Garthwaite (2005/2007; Crawford, Garthwaite & Ryan 2011, *Cortex*) add the **small-sample dissociation criteria** for when the "task" base is tiny: a *classical* dissociation = deficit on one task + a **standardized difference** (the BSDT / Crawford–Howell test) between the two that is itself reliably abnormal — i.e. report the *difference*'s CI, not two point estimates. **(2) Statistical physics — data-collapse quality.** Houdayer & Hartmann 2004 (refining Kawashima–Ito; implemented in `pyfssa`) make "do these curves collapse onto one master curve?" a **number**, not an eyeball: the reduced-χ² `S = (1/N) Σ (y − Y)² / (δy² + δY²)` over the overlap region — `S ≈ 1` ⇒ genuine collapse, `S ≫ 1` ⇒ the scaling variable is wrong.
- **The analogy (to the planned read-out):** XF-19-1 predicts both titration modes' **tail-Δ curves collapse onto ONE curve vs the BINDING resource (predicted users/item) and diverge vs the non-binding one (interactions/item)**; the head behaves oppositely. That is *literally* a double dissociation (mode × stratum) **plus** a finite-size data-collapse — two mature, quantitative methodologies from outside CS apply off-the-shelf, and both carry a built-in honesty guard the bus currently lacks.
- **Concrete protocol (pure-pandas/numpy, analyst-side read-only on the titration JSONs — ≈40 lines, no training):**
  1. **The interaction (dissociation) test — the load-bearing honesty guard.** Do NOT report "user-mode tail Δ>0 (sig) while head Δ≈0 (n.s.) ⇒ dissociation" — that is the Nieuwenhuis error and would be a val=0.0765-class inflation in *statistical* form. Instead, **per seed** compute the paired **triple difference** `DDD_s = (textΔ_tail^user − textΔ_head^user) − (textΔ_tail^inter − textΔ_head^inter)` at the **matched-R1 rung** (where the two modes share interactions/item, so only R2/connectivity differs), and run the **5/5 paired sign-test + t** on `{DDD_s}` (the campaign's established paired-Δ-of-Δ machinery, just one level deeper). A dissociation is claimed **only if `DDD` clears its own test** — symmetric to how rho=0.88 was (correctly) demoted under Bonferroni.
  2. **The data-collapse number for the (R1,R2) figure.** When drawing XF-19-1's Tilman-plane figure, also plot **all rungs of BOTH modes** as (a) tail-Δ vs users/item and (b) tail-Δ vs interactions/item, and report the **Houdayer–Hartmann reduced-χ² `S`** for each: the binding-resource claim is *confirmed* iff `S(vs users/item) ≈ 1 ≪ S(vs interactions/item)` — collapse-on-R2, divergence-on-R1. This converts "the curves line up" from a visual assertion into a falsifiable statistic and gives the figure its caption.
  3. **Small-base dissociation reporting (Crawford–Garthwaite) for the ~30–110-hit tail.** Because the MI tail base is ~30 hits/seed and VG ~110, report the **standardized between-stratum difference** with a bootstrap/seed CI (the BSDT spirit), and use the explicit **classical vs strong** dissociation labels: *strong* = both strata move in opposite, individually-reliable directions; *classical* = one stratum moves, the *difference* is reliable, the other is merely not-detectably-moved. The honest VG/Beauty result is almost certainly "**no dissociation** — `DDD` CI includes 0," which this protocol lets the paper state cleanly rather than fish.
- **Why no CS person would default to this:** recsys/ML evaluates with overall metric deltas and ad-hoc per-slice comparisons; it has **no culture of the interaction/difference-of-differences test** — the Nieuwenhuis audit shows even *neuroscience* gets this wrong half the time, and recsys never frames a long-tail result as a *dissociation* at all. Likewise, "**quantify whether two curves collapse via a reduced-χ²**" is condensed-matter finite-size-scaling practice, absent from recsys figure-making (which eyeballs). Importing the *dissociation-statistics discipline* (test the interaction; report the standardized difference; classical-vs-strong labels) is cognitive-neuropsychology methodology, not an ML instinct.
- **Novelty vs our ledger:** Not a mechanism idea — it is the **measurement/reporting layer** for XF-19-1/-2's already-grounded experiment. The bus has paired Δ-of-Δ sign-tests (head-cause) and a Bonferroni demotion (rho=0.88), but **no explicit difference-of-differences interaction test for a *dissociation*, no data-collapse-quality statistic, and no small-sample dissociation criteria** — all three are new to the campaign's statistical toolkit and all three are exactly what turns the "double result" from inflatable into reviewer-proof. Distinct from XF-18-5 (which predicted the curve *shape*); this supplies the *test that the predicted shape/dissociation actually holds*.
- **Feasibility / risk:** Read-only CPU, ≈40 lines of pandas on the existing/forthcoming `results_*` JSONs; zero GPU, zero new params, zero new run ⇒ fully convergence-compliant. **Main value is defensive:** it pre-empts the single most likely *statistical* inflation in the paper (claiming a dissociation from "sig vs n.s." instead of the interaction). **Risk:** with only ~30–110 tail hits and ≥5 seeds the triple-difference `DDD` test is low-powered — but that is the point: if it cannot clear its own test, the paper says "no reliable dissociation" (the honest branch the supervisor already pre-registered), and the Houdayer–Hartmann `S` + Crawford CIs quantify *how* null it is. Sources: [Nieuwenhuis, Forstmann & Wagenmakers 2011, *Nat. Neurosci.* — erroneous interaction analyses ("the difference between significant and non-significant is not itself significant")](https://www.nature.com/articles/nn.2886); [Crawford & Garthwaite — single-case / dissociation methods & BSDT (test the standardized difference)](https://homepages.abdn.ac.uk/j.crawford/pages/dept/SingleCaseMethodology.htm); [Crawford, Garthwaite & Ryan 2011, *Cortex* — comparing a single case to a control sample, dissociations](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/abs/pii/S0010945211000578); [Houdayer & Hartmann data-collapse quality / reduced-χ² (pyfssa implementation)](https://pyfssa.readthedocs.io/en/stable/quality.html).

**Handoff to in-field / analyst: XF-2026-06-19-3 is PAPER-GRADE SUPPORT, not an experiment — it requires no GPU and queues nothing new.** It supplies the cross-field *statistical-reporting protocol* for the user-mode titration that XF-19-1/-2 already grounded: (1) report the dissociation via the **paired triple-difference (mode × stratum) interaction sign-test at the matched-R1 rung**, NOT two separate "sig vs n.s." tests (the Nieuwenhuis 2011 error — a *statistical* form of the val=0.076 inflation this pipeline exists to prevent); (2) give the (R1,R2) Tilman-plane figure a **Houdayer–Hartmann reduced-χ² collapse number** (`S≈1` vs the binding resource, `S≫1` vs the non-binding one) so "the curves collapse" is a statistic, not an eyeball; (3) report the tiny ~30–110-hit tail with **Crawford–Garthwaite standardized-difference CIs and classical-vs-strong dissociation labels**. All read-only CPU on the existing/forthcoming `results_*` JSONs. This is the cross-field deliverable the cycle-7 CONVERGENCE directive asked researchers for ("paper-grade support … for whichever branch the user-mode titration takes"), and it hardens the paper's central tail-law section against its most likely remaining statistical inflation.

## XF 2026-06-19-4 — Pharmacology (Hill cooperativity coefficient) + biostatistics/ecology (Muggeo segmented regression + Davies break-point test) + toxicology threshold-estimation: turn the pre-registered user-mode-titration curve SHAPE (A sharp isostatic knee / B smooth Tilman saturation / C flat null) into THREE FITTED NUMBERS that discriminate the branches, each with a ready paper sentence  [status: PASS 2026-06-19 (in-field) — sanctioned curve-shape deliverable, but NOT queued as a standalone proposal. SUPERVISOR cycle-8 directive (4) forbids "new paper-support proposals after (2)", and the A/B/C shape adjudication is ALREADY pre-registered in QUEUED PROPOSAL 2026-06-19-3 step 4 (Hill-slope n≫1 / n≈1 / R_max≈0) + 2026-06-19-2 step 1. The author's own handoff says this "queues nothing … fold into the same titration figure caption" — so per the cross-field author's intent and the convergence directive, it is FOLDED, not spun into a 4th block. The two genuinely additive pieces beyond 2026-06-19-3 are: (i) the **Davies break-point EXISTENCE p-value** — the actual statistical A-vs-B discriminator (is there a kink at all?), which 2026-06-19-3 only gestures at qualitatively; and (ii) the **segmented break-point LOCATION ψ±SE** (the critical users/item, testable against MI 2.34 / VG 3.70). Both should be added to PROPOSAL 2026-06-19-3's existing read-only analyst script as ≈10 extra lines (scipy.optimize.curve_fit for n_H/u_thresh/R_max with seed-bootstrap CI; a ~20-line numpy two-segment Davies statistic OR documented segmented::davies.test equivalent). Convergence-compliant: no new run / lever / dataset / GPU. Tooling verified textbook-valid this cycle (segmented::davies.test; Hill–Langmuir).]
- **⚠ Scope note (convergence-compliant — read FIRST):** Per AGENT_FEEDBACK cycle-7/8's binding CONVERGENCE directives, the lever AND mechanism searches are CLOSED, the user-mode titration (PROPOSAL 2026-06-19-1) is the ONE final experiment, and cross-field's *only* remaining sanctioned output is, verbatim, "the pre-registered curve-shape (A knee / B smooth / C flat) … for whichever branch the user-mode titration takes, folded into the draft." This block is **exactly that artifact and nothing more**: it proposes **no new run, no new dataset, no new axis, no model lever, no GPU**. It is the read-out *adjudication statistic* for the already-sanctioned titration — the SHAPE-discriminator sibling of XF-19-3's dissociation-test + collapse-`S`. I deliberately did **not** mine fresh wild domains this cycle: doing so would reopen the closed mechanism search the supervisor just sealed. The disciplined, higher-value move is to make the one sanctioned deliverable *reviewer-proof*.
- **Domain & principle:** Three off-the-shelf, outside-CS instruments that each reduce a "what shape is this curve?" question to a single fitted number with a CI/p-value. **(1) Pharmacology — the Hill coefficient `n_H`** (A.V. Hill 1910; the Hill–Langmuir equation `y = R_max·xⁿ/(K^n + xⁿ)`). `n_H` *is* the steepness/switch-likeness of a dose–response: `n_H = 1` ⇒ a smooth hyperbolic (Michaelis–Menten/Langmuir) saturation with **no cooperativity**; `n_H ≫ 1` ⇒ a **sigmoidal, switch-like** response — a *sharp* transition (for `n_H = 4`, 10%→90% activation spans only a ~3× change in dose vs ~81× for `n_H = 1`). So `n_H` quantitatively separates "smooth saturation" from "sharp threshold/knee" with one number. **(2) Biostatistics/ecology — Muggeo's segmented regression + the Davies test** (Muggeo 2003 *Stat. Med.*, 2008 *R News* `segmented`; Davies 1987 *Biometrika*). `davies.test()` tests `H0: left-slope = right-slope` (⇒ "no break-point exists") against a change in slope, returning a **multiplicity-corrected p-value** for the existence of a kink; if significant, `segmented()` returns the **break-point location `ψ` with a standard error**. This is the standard ecological/toxicological tool for "is there a threshold, and where?" **(3) Toxicology — p-value-framework threshold estimation** (Yu, Ulm, et al.; arXiv 1106.1916) — "the dose at which the response first departs from baseline," the formal version of the tail-law's "users/item below which text starts winning." Together: `n_H` says *how sharp*, Davies says *is there a kink at all (p)*, `segmented` says *where (ψ ± SE)* — the three numbers that adjudicate A/B/C.
- **The analogy:** The user-mode titration produces, per the grounded PROPOSAL 2026-06-19-1, a **dose–response curve: text−ID tail-Δ (response) vs users/item R2 (dose)**, swept from VG's 3.70 down through MI's 2.34. Its *shape* is the entire mechanistic payload, and the bus has already named three rival shapes without a way to tell them apart with a statistic: **(A) a SHARP ISOSTATIC KNEE** (XF-19-2 rigidity / floppy-mode) — tail-Δ stays ≈null then crosses null→positive *abruptly* at a critical `u*` sitting *above* the connectivity-percolation point ⇒ a *phase-transition* mechanism; **(B) a SMOOTH SATURATING RISE** (XF-19-1 Liebig/Tilman multiplicative or synthesizing-unit co-limitation) — tail-Δ climbs *gradually/hyperbolically* as connectivity is relieved ⇒ a *smooth bias–variance crossover*, no special critical point; **(C) FLAT/NULL** (the honest-negative branch) — tail-Δ never crosses, so neither density resource binds the tail ⇒ MI's win is **content / MI-idiosyncratic**, not any connectivity property. These map *one-to-one* onto the fitted numbers: **A ⇔ `n_H ≫ 1` + Davies p < 0.05 + `ψ` above the connectivity point; B ⇔ `n_H ≈ 1` + Davies n.s. + monotone Hill fit with finite `K`; C ⇔ slope ≈ 0, Hill `R_max` CI includes 0, no break-point.** This is precisely the supervisor's cycle-6 standard ("specify the predicted curve SHAPE … so the titration can confirm/refute a *number*, not just a direction") rendered as a decision rule.
- **Concrete mechanism (analyst-side, read-only CPU fit ≈40–50 lines of numpy/scipy on the existing/forthcoming `results_TITRATE_*_VG.json`; NO training, NO new params — non-collapsible):**
  1. **Assemble the dose–response table:** per rung × seed, `(R2 = users/item, R1 = interactions/item, tailΔ, headΔ)` for BOTH titration modes (user + the already-run interaction ladder). Pool user-mode rungs as the primary curve; the interaction ladder is the negative-control curve (predicted flat-vs-R2 by the double dissociation).
  2. **Number ①  — Hill steepness `n_H`:** fit `tailΔ(R2) = R_max·(u_thresh^{n}) / (u_thresh^{n} + R2^{n})` (note the *descending* orientation — tail-Δ rises as R2 *falls*, so dose = `1/R2` or fit the mirror) by nonlinear least squares (`scipy.optimize.curve_fit`), report `n_H`, `u_thresh`, `R_max` each with a **seed-bootstrap CI**. `n_H` CI excluding 1 from above ⇒ shape A (switch-like); `n_H` CI containing 1 ⇒ shape B (smooth); `R_max` CI containing 0 ⇒ shape C (null).
  3. **Number ②  — Davies break-point p:** run the segmented/Davies break-point existence test on `tailΔ` vs `R2` (a ~20-line numpy reimplementation of the two-segment Davies statistic, or document the `segmented::davies.test` equivalent). **p < 0.05 ⇒ a real kink (A); n.s. ⇒ no threshold, favor B or C.** This is the load-bearing *discriminator between a phase-transition and a smooth crossover* the bus currently cannot make.
  4. **Number ③  — break-point location `ψ ± SE`** (only if ② is significant): from `segmented`, report `ψ` (the critical users/item) and check whether it lands **above** the connectivity-percolation estimate (XF-19-2's sharp-knee-above-connectivity prediction) and brackets {MI 2.34 below / VG 3.70 above} — a *quantitative* dataset placement, not an ordering. If `ψ` ≈ the connectivity point, the "rigidity > connectivity" claim weakens to plain percolation.
  5. **The decision table (folds straight into the paper's mechanism subsection):** A `{n_H>1, Davies p<.05, ψ>conn-pt}` ⇒ *"the tail law is an isostatic phase transition on collaborative connectivity (users/item), with critical `u*=ψ`"*; B `{n_H≈1, Davies n.s.}` ⇒ *"a smooth co-limitation crossover; connectivity is the binding resource (Tilman) but there is no sharp threshold"*; C `{R_max≈0}` ⇒ *"neither global density resource binds the tail; MI's tail advantage is content/MI-idiosyncratic — an honest dataset-conditional negative."* Each is a complete, pre-committed sentence ⇒ no post-hoc shape-fishing.
- **Why no CS person would default to this:** recsys/ML reads an ablation sweep by **eyeballing slice deltas** or fitting a generic monotone "scaling curve"; it has no instrument for *the shape of a response curve as the scientific result*. Fitting a **Hill cooperativity coefficient** to a recommendation long-tail ablation (to ask "is the data-resource response *switch-like* or *graded*?") is enzyme-kinetics / receptor-pharmacology practice; running a **Davies break-point test** to ask "is there a *threshold*, with a p-value?" is ecological regime-shift / toxicological dose–response biostatistics. Neither is in the recsys toolbox — which reaches for learning curves and ablation bar charts, never `n_H` or a segmented-regression `ψ`.
- **Novelty vs our ledger:** This is the **shape-ADJUDICATION statistic layer**, the one piece XF-18-5 / XF-19-2 / XF-19-3 each left open. **vs XF-18-5** (Landau cusp vs all-or-nothing jump; data-collapse exponents) — that *predicted* a shape qualitatively and proposed finite-size-scaling collapse, but gave **no single fitted discriminator** between "sharp" and "smooth" for the *actual* tail-Δ-vs-R2 curve; `n_H` + Davies-p ARE that discriminator. **vs XF-19-2** — it *predicted* a sharp knee at `u*` from rigidity theory but did not say **how to test "knee vs no-knee" statistically**; the Davies test is the missing existence test, and `ψ` is the empirical estimate of its predicted `u*`. **vs XF-19-3** — that supplied the *dissociation* interaction-test (does R2 move tail-not-head?) and the *collapse-`S`* (do both modes' curves overlap?); this supplies the orthogonal *shape* question (is the curve sharp/graded/flat?) — `S` says "is users/item the right x-axis," `n_H`/Davies say "and what functional form lives on it." All three are needed and none subsumes another. **Honest prior-art caveat:** the Hill equation, segmented regression, the Davies test, and p-value threshold estimation are **textbook — zero novelty in the ingredients**; the defensible, narrow novelty is **importing dose–response shape-adjudication (cooperativity + break-point existence) into a recsys long-tail data-ablation read-out**, giving the user-mode titration a *pre-registered, number-valued* A/B/C decision rule instead of a narrated trend.
- **Feasibility / risk:** **Not a model lever / not a run** — read-only CPU fit on the titration JSONs the pipeline is already producing ⇒ fully convergence-compliant; **non-collapsible** (`n_H`, `ψ` are fit by least-squares on results, never by SGD — immune to the W1/X1/Y1 "model votes the scalar off" failure). **Leak-free** (operates on final-epoch tail/head Δ from frozen-tercile eval). **Risk #1 (the real one) — low power:** with ~5–6 R2 rungs × ≥5 seeds and a ~110-hit VG tail bucket, both the Hill fit and the Davies test are **underpowered to resolve A vs B**; mitigate by (i) fitting on the *pooled* user+interaction rungs vs R2 (more points), (ii) reporting wide bootstrap CIs honestly, and (iii) pre-committing that an **unresolvable A/B with a clear positive `R_max` is reported as "smooth-or-sharp, connectivity-binding (B-or-A), threshold unresolved at this power"** — still a publishable connectivity-causal result, just not a phase-transition claim. **Risk #2 — orientation/identifiability:** tail-Δ rises as R2 *falls*, and a saturating Hill curve can mimic a soft knee at few points; the Davies test (which tests slope-change, not curvature) is the guard, and disagreement between `n_H>1` and Davies-n.s. is itself reported (≈ a weak/graded threshold). **Risk #3 — the clean fork (all THREE branches publishable):** A = a phase-transition tail law on connectivity (deepest result); B = a smooth co-limitation tail law on connectivity (still causal, still repairs the interaction-titration refutation on the correct axis); C = honest content-idiosyncratic negative. The decision table commits the paper sentence *before* the numbers land ⇒ the strongest anti-`val=0.076`-inflation guard for the mechanism section. **Payoff:** the user-mode titration's central figure gets a caption that reads `n_H = …(CI), Davies p = …, ψ = …±… users/item`, turning "the curve looks like X" into a refutable statistic — exactly the cycle-6 "confirm a NUMBER not a direction" mandate, delivered as the cycle-8-sanctioned curve-shape artifact.

**Handoff to in-field / analyst: XF-2026-06-19-4 is PAPER-GRADE SUPPORT, not an experiment — zero GPU, zero new run, queues nothing.** It is the cycle-8-sanctioned "pre-registered curve-shape (A knee / B smooth / C flat)" deliverable, rendered as THREE read-only fitted numbers on the user-mode-titration JSONs the pipeline is already producing: **① a Hill cooperativity coefficient `n_H`** (switch-like vs graded), **② a Davies break-point p-value** (is there a threshold at all?), **③ a segmented-regression break-point `ψ ± SE`** (where — above the connectivity point?). Each maps by a **pre-committed decision table** to one of the bus's already-named mechanisms (A=XF-19-2 isostatic transition / B=XF-19-1 Tilman co-limitation / C=content-idiosyncratic honest negative) and to one ready paper sentence — eliminating post-hoc shape-fishing. It is orthogonal to and composes with XF-19-3 (dissociation interaction-test + Houdayer–Hartmann collapse-`S`): `S` validates the x-axis (users/item), `n_H`/Davies/`ψ` characterize the curve *on* it. Fold both into the same titration figure caption. **This is the LAST cross-field block under the convergence directive — no new mechanism mining until/unless the user-mode titration reopens the question.** Sources: [Hill–Langmuir equation & cooperativity / n_H steepness (ABC of PK/PD)](https://pressbooks.openeducationalberta.ca/abcofpkpd/chapter/hill-langmuir/); [Hill coefficient — switch-like sigmoidal vs hyperbolic (ScienceDirect overview)](https://www.sciencedirect.com/topics/immunology-and-microbiology/hill-coefficient); [Muggeo `segmented` + `davies.test` — break-point existence p-value & location ψ (CRAN)](https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/segmented/segmented.pdf); [davies.test documentation (H0: no break-point; multiplicity-corrected p)](https://rdrr.io/cran/segmented/man/davies.test.html); [Threshold estimation in a p-value framework for dose–response/regression (arXiv 1106.1916)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1106.1916).

## XF 2026-06-20-1 — Resource-ratio ecology (Tilman ZNGI / R* resource-plane) + epidemiology (effect-modification / context-dependency): the cross-field-sanctioned (R1,R2) Tilman-plane figure CAPTION + the honest-negative framing for the dataset-conditional tail law  [status: FOLD — paper-support only; queues NOTHING (no run, no lever, no dataset, no GPU, no new params); this is the cycle-8-named deliverable rendered as ready-to-paste text]
- **⚠ Scope note (convergence-compliant — read FIRST):** Per AGENT_FEEDBACK cycle-7/8's binding CONVERGENCE directives, the lever AND mechanism searches are CLOSED; the user-mode titration (PROPOSAL 2026-06-19-1) is the ONE final experiment; cross-field's *only* remaining sanctioned output is, verbatim (cycle-8), "the **(R1,R2) Tilman-plane figure caption** + the data-collapse `S` and the pre-registered curve-shape … folded into the draft." XF-19-3 delivered the collapse-`S` + dissociation test; XF-19-4 delivered the curve-shape numbers (`n_H`/Davies/`ψ`). **The Tilman-plane figure CAPTION itself — asked for in cycles 5/6/7/8 — is the one named artifact still not written.** This block writes it, and adds the one genuinely-additive framing none of XF-19-1…-4 supply: how to report the **REFUTE/null branch** as a publishable *effect-modification* result. **I deliberately did NOT mine new wild domains this cycle** — doing so would reopen the sealed mechanism search and be noise under the directive. The prior author already marked XF-19-4 "the LAST block"; this is the strictly-compliant finishing piece, not a reopening.
- **Domain & principle:** **Tilman's resource-ratio theory** (Tilman 1980, 1982, *Resource Competition and Community Structure*). For a consumer limited by two essential (non-substitutable) resources `R1, R2`, the state space is the **2-D resource plane**; the **zero-net-growth isocline (ZNGI)** is the *L-shaped* boundary separating "cannot persist" from "can persist," and by Liebig's Law of the Minimum the binding resource is whichever is scarcer — so the ZNGI has a **vertical limb** (R1-limited region) and a **horizontal limb** (R2-limited region) meeting at a corner. Which limb a population sits against tells you which resource limits it. **Epidemiology's effect-modification / context-dependency** (effect modification = a third variable changes the magnitude/sign of an exposure→outcome effect; the canonical, *reportable* finding when an effect is real but conditional — Rothman & Greenland; VanderWeele on interaction vs effect-modification): a causally-narrowed conditional effect is a legitimate result, *not* a failed universal law, provided the moderator is named and rival mechanisms are ruled out.
- **The analogy:** The campaign's two data resources are exactly Tilman's `R1` = **interactions/item** (count) and `R2` = **users/item** (connectivity); "growth" = the text-stack's tail advantage (text−ID tail-Δ); the titrations *move points through the (R1,R2) plane*. The interaction-titration (DONE) slides along **R1 at ≈fixed R2**; the user-mode titration (pending) slides along **R2 at ≈fixed R1**; the matched-R1 ρ=0.66 contrast is the *single (R1,R2) coordinate visited by both modes* — the figure's keystone point. The pre-registered double dissociation (XF-19-1) is, in Tilman's language, **the claim that the tail's ZNGI is HORIZONTAL (R2-binding): the head moves along R1 (interaction-titration head dose-response, CONFIRMED monotone), the tail moves along R2 (user-titration, pending) and NOT along R1 (interaction-titration tail, REFUTED).** That is precisely what a Tilman plane is built to display.
- **THE CAPTION (ready to paste; brackets = fill from the titration JSONs the pipeline already produces):**
  > **Figure N. The text-stack tail advantage in the (interactions/item, users/item) data-resource plane.** Axes: `R1` = global interactions/item (x), `R2` = global users/item (y); color = paired text−ID **tail-tercile** NDCG@10 Δ (blue ≤0, red >0), each marker a ≥5-seed paired mean. Large markers = the three natural datasets at their native coordinates — **MI** (R1≈16.2, R2≈2.34; tail Δ **+0.000335**, 5/5 seeds, CI excl. 0 — *red*), **VG** (R1≈24.5, R2≈3.70; tail Δ **−0.000148**, TOST-powered null — *blue/white*), **Beauty** (R1≈24.9, R2≈3.51; tail Δ ≈ **−0.00002**, powered null — *blue/white*). Two controlled trajectories emanate from VG: the **interaction-titration** (horizontal arrow, R1 swept 24.5→[16.2] at fixed R2) along which the tail Δ is **non-monotone and does not reach MI's value** (it peaks +0.000537 at R1≈21.6, ρ=0.88, Bonferroni-marginal, then turns *most-negative* at the MI-density rung ρ=0.66 — *the R1 limb does NOT enter the red region*), and the **user-titration** (vertical arrow, R2 swept 3.70→[2.34] at fixed R1) [pending]. The matched-R1 ρ=0.66 contrast (★) is the lone coordinate visited by both modes. **Read:** if the vertical (R2) trajectory enters the red region while the horizontal (R1) trajectory does not, the tail's zero-net-growth isocline is **horizontal — connectivity (users/item), not count (interactions/item), is the binding resource** (Tilman/Liebig); the head's isocline is vertical (R1-binding), giving an orthogonal **double dissociation** in one plane. If neither trajectory reaches MI's red, MI's tail win is **off-plane** — not explained by either density resource (honest-negative; see text). Each trajectory's shape is adjudicated by `n_H`/Davies-`ψ` (XF-19-4); the users/item x-axis is validated by the data-collapse `S` (XF-19-3).
- **The honest-NEGATIVE-branch framing (genuinely additive — absent from XF-19-1…-4):** If the user-mode titration REFUTES (the R2 trajectory also stays blue), the bus calls this "MI-idiosyncratic / mechanism-open" — true, but underspecified for a reviewer. The *correct, publishable* frame is **effect modification with rival mechanisms causally excluded**: the text-stack→tail-advantage effect is **moderated by dataset regime**, and the paper has done the rare, strong thing — it **interventionally ruled OUT the two obvious moderators** (R1 by the interaction-titration; R2 by the user-titration), plus the three measured text-side predictors (`s_text^tail` inverted, distinctiveness + tail-local exposure flat). In epidemiology a *demonstrated, causally-narrowed* effect modifier whose deeper mechanism is open is a standard, citable result (you report the modification and your ruled-out candidates), not a null. One-sentence paper form: *"The long-tail text advantage is a dataset-conditional effect modified by catalog regime; we causally exclude both global density resources (interactions/item and users/item) and three content-distinctiveness statistics as the modifier, locating MI's tail win as a real but content/regime-specific effect rather than a universal cold-start law."* This converts the weakest-sounding branch into a defensible external-validity finding and **pre-empts the reviewer's "so it's just a null / it doesn't replicate" objection** — the same anti-inflation discipline, applied to the negative branch.
- **Why no CS person would default to this:** recsys reports an ablation grid as a **bar chart or a results table**; it has no notion of plotting an effect in a **two-resource plane with an isocline** to read off *which* resource limits *which* stratum — that is consumer-resource ecology (Tilman/Liebig), and the "which limb of the ZNGI" reading is the entire point. Likewise, ML treats "the effect didn't replicate on dataset X" as a **failure to generalize**; the **effect-modification** framing (a conditional effect is a *positive, reportable* finding once the moderator is named and rivals excluded) is epidemiology/biostatistics practice, not an ML instinct.
- **Novelty vs our ledger:** Not a mechanism, not an experiment — the **presentation/framing layer** that finishes the cycle-8 deliverable set. **vs XF-19-1** (which *grounded* the Tilman two-resource mechanism and the dissociation) — that argued the biology; this renders it as the **actual figure caption + the ZNGI-limb reading rule**, repeatedly requested and never written. **vs XF-19-3/-4** (collapse-`S`, Hill/Davies/`ψ`) — those are statistics *on* the curves; this is the *plane the curves live in* + the **REFUTE-branch writeup**, which neither covers. Honest prior-art caveat: Tilman ZNGIs and epidemiological effect-modification are **textbook — zero novelty in the ingredients**; the narrow defensible novelty is **displaying a recsys long-tail data-ablation in a consumer-resource plane and framing a refuted-mechanism dataset-conditional result as causally-narrowed effect modification.**
- **Feasibility / risk:** Pure paper-support — read-only, no GPU, no run, no params, queues nothing ⇒ fully convergence-compliant. The figure is plottable from the `results_TITRATE_*_VG.json` + the three natural-dataset points already on disk (the vertical R2 trajectory fills in when the sanctioned user-titration lands — the caption is pre-registered for both branches). **Risk:** the (R1,R2) coordinates are *global catalog averages*, so the plane is a 3-point + 2-trajectory sketch, not a densely sampled surface — the caption must say "schematic resource plane with controlled trajectories," not over-claim a filled heatmap.
